“Absolutely! My mistake, of course, but there it was. The fellow might have been his double.”

“But you’ve never seen the man.”

“Oh yes, I have. I forgot to tell you. I met him on the links yesterday. I’d gone out there alone, rather expecting to have a round with the pro., but, finding this lad there, I suggested that we might go round together. We did eighteen holes, and he licked the boots off me. Very hot stuff he was. And after the game he took me off to his cottage and gave me a drink. He lives at the cottage next door to Platt’s farm, so, you see, it was the identical chappie. We got extremely matey. Like brothers. Absolutely! So you can understand what a shock it gave me when I found what I took to be the same man serving bracers to the multitude the same evening. One of those nasty jars that cause a fellow’s head to swim a bit, don’t you know, and make him lose confidence in himself.”

Lord Belpher did not reply. His brain was whirling. So he had been right after all!

“You know,” pursued Reggie seriously, “I think you are making the bloomer of a lifetime over this hat-swatting chappie. You’ve misjudged him. He’s a first-rate sort. Take it from me! Nobody could have got out of the bunker at the fifteenth hole better than he did. If you’ll take my advice, you’ll conciliate the feller. A really first-class golfer is what you need in the family. Besides, even leaving out of the question the fact that he can do things with a niblick that I didn’t think anybody except the pro. could do, he’s a corking good sort. A stout fellow in every respect. I took to the chappie. He’s all right. Grab him, Boots, before he gets away. That’s my tip to you. You’ll never regret it! From first to last this lad didn’t foozle a single drive, and his approach-putting has to be seen to be believed. Well, got to dress, I suppose. Mustn’t waste life’s springtime sitting here talking to you. Toodle-oo, laddie! We shall meet anon!”

===Chinese===

“千真万确！当然是我认错了，可人就在那儿站着。那家伙简直就是他的化身。”

“可你从来没见过那个人。”

“哦，见过的。忘了跟你说了。昨天我在球场碰见他的。我独自出门，本指望跟职业球手打上一场，一瞧这位老兄在那儿，便提议两个人结伴打一轮。我们打了十八个洞，他把我打得一败涂地。好生了得。打完球他把我领去他的小屋，请我喝了一杯。他住在普拉特农场隔壁那幢小屋里，所以你瞧，正是同一位仁兄本尊。我们处得热络极了。情同手足。千真万确！所以你能体会，当天晚上我发现被我认作是同一个人的那位，正在给一干人等斟醒神的烈酒，这一下给了我多大的震动。就是那号叫人脑袋微微发晕、连自信心都保不住的恶心打击，你明白吧。”

贝尔弗勋爵没有答话。他的脑子嗡嗡打转。这么说他压根儿就没有错！

“要知道，”雷吉一本正经地接着说，“我看你对这位拍帽子的仁兄，可是犯了天字第一号的大错。你把他看屈了。他是个第一流的好人。这话听我的！第十五洞那个沙坑，谁也别想比他脱身脱得更漂亮。你要肯听我的劝，就该跟这小子拉拢拉拢。家里正缺一位真正头等的高尔夫球手。再说，撇开他那手挖起杆的功夫不提——除了职业球手，我原以为谁也使不出那本事——他还是个顶呱呱的汉子。方方面面都够得上一条好汉。我喜欢上了这位仁兄。他没得挑。靴子，趁他还没溜走，赶紧把他抓到手。这是我给你的忠告。你绝不会后悔！这位老兄从头到尾没有一个开球失手，他那近洞推杆，非亲眼看一看不能相信。好了，该换衣服了吧。总不能把人生的春天浪费在坐这儿跟你说话上。回见，小老弟！咱们后会有期！”

===Original===

Lord Belpher leaped from his bed. He was feeling worse than ever now, and a glance into the mirror told him that he looked rather worse than he felt. Late nights and insufficient sleep, added to the need of a shave, always made him look like something that should have been swept up and taken away to the ash-bin. And as for his physical condition, talking to Reggie Byng never tended to make you feel better when you had a headache. Reggie’s manner was not soothing, and on this particular morning his choice of a topic had been unusually irritating. Lord Belpher told himself that he could not understand Reggie. He had never been able to make his mind quite clear as to the exact relations between the latter and his sister Maud, but he had always been under the impression that, if they were not actually engaged, they were on the verge of becoming so; and it was maddening to have to listen to Reggie advocating the claims of a rival as if he had no personal interest in the affair at all. Percy felt for his complaisant friend something of the annoyance which a householder feels for the watchdog whom he finds fraternizing with the burglar. Why, Reggie, more than anyone else, ought to be foaming with rage at the insolence of this American fellow in coming down to Belpher and planting himself at the castle gates. Instead of which, on his own showing, he appeared to have adopted an attitude towards him which would have excited remark if adopted by David towards Jonathan. He seemed to spend all his spare time frolicking with the man on the golf-links and hobnobbing with him in his house.

Lord Belpher was thoroughly upset. It was impossible to prove it or to do anything about it now, but he was convinced that the fellow had wormed his way into the castle in the guise of a waiter. He had probably met Maud and plotted further meetings with her. This thing was becoming unendurable.

One thing was certain. The family honour was in his hands. Anything that was to be done to keep Maud away from the intruder must be done by himself. Reggie was hopeless: he was capable, as far as Percy could see, of escorting Maud to the fellow’s door in his own car and leaving her on the threshold with his blessing. As for Lord Marshmoreton, roses and the family history took up so much of his time that he could not be counted on for anything but moral support. He, Percy, must do the active work.

He had just come to this decision, when, approaching the window and gazing down into the grounds, he perceived his sister Maud walking rapidly﻿—and, so it seemed to him, with a furtive air﻿—down the east drive. And it was to the east that Platt’s farm and the cottage next door to it lay.

===Chinese===

贝尔弗勋爵从床上一跃而起。这会儿他感觉比先前更糟，往镜子里一瞥，又告诉他模样比感觉还要糟上几分。熬夜加睡眠不足，再添上该刮没刮的胡子，向来使他看上去像件本该扫拢来倒进灰箱的东西。至于身体状况，头疼的时候跟雷吉·宾谈话，从来只会越谈越不好受。雷吉那副做派没有半点安抚功效，而今天早上他挑的题目又格外招人上火。贝尔弗勋爵对自己说，雷吉这个人他弄不懂。他从来没能把雷吉和自己妹妹莫德之间的确切关系想个明白，但他始终抱有一个印象：即便两人还没正式订婚，也已到了订婚的门槛上；如今听着雷吉替一个情敌摇旗呐喊，仿佛此事与他本人八竿子打不着，实在叫人按捺不住。珀西对自己这位好说话的朋友生出的恼火，好比一户人家发现自家的看门狗正跟窃贼称兄道弟。是啊，这个美国佬胆大包天，竟一路跑下贝尔弗，在城堡门口扎下营盘，雷吉比别人更该气得口吐白沫才是。可他倒好，照他自己的说法，他对那家伙摆出的姿态，若换作大卫对约拿单（David and Jonathan），也要惹人议论。他好像把全部闲暇都用来陪那家伙在高尔夫球场上嬉闹，还上人家屋里去套近乎。

贝尔弗勋爵心乱如麻。如今既无从证明，也无法有所作为，但他深信不疑：那家伙是扮成侍应生，千方百计钻进城堡来的。他说不定已经见到了莫德，还密谋着跟她再会。这件事越来越忍无可忍了。

有一点是肯定的。家族的名誉如今握在他手里。凡是要把莫德同那个闯入者隔开的事，都得他亲自出马。雷吉是指望不上的：照珀西看，他干得出开着自己的汽车把莫德送到那家伙门口，还在门槛上留下一句祝福再走人的事。至于马什莫顿勋爵，玫瑰和《家族史》占去了他那么多工夫，除了道义上的支援，别的什么也指望不上。他，珀西，必须亲自实干。

他刚打定这个主意，走到窗前朝园子里一望，就看见妹妹莫德正顺着东边车道快步走去——在他看来，还带着一副偷偷摸摸的神气。而普拉特农场和它隔壁那幢小屋，恰恰就在东边。

===Original===

At the moment of this discovery, Percy was in a costume ill adapted for the taking of country walks. Reggie’s remarks about his liver had struck home, and it had been his intention, by way of a corrective to his headache and a general feeling of swollen ill-health, to do a little work before his bath with a pair of Indian clubs. He had arrayed himself for this purpose in an old sweater, a pair of grey flannel trousers, and patent leather evening shoes. It was not the garb he would have chosen himself for a ramble, but time was flying: even to put on a pair of boots is a matter of minutes: and in another moment or two Maud would be out of sight. Percy ran downstairs, snatched up a soft shooting-hat, which proved, too late, to belong to a person with a head two sizes smaller than his own; and raced out into the grounds. He was just in time to see Maud disappearing round the corner of the drive.

Lord Belpher had never belonged to that virile class of the community which considers running a pleasure and a pastime. At Oxford, on those occasions when the members of his college had turned out on raw afternoons to trot along the riverbank encouraging the college eight with yelling and the swinging of police-rattles, Percy had always stayed prudently in his rooms with tea and buttered toast, thereby avoiding who knows what colds and coughs. When he ran, he ran reluctantly and with a definite object in view, such as the catching of a train. He was consequently not in the best of condition, and the sharp sprint which was imperative at this juncture if he was to keep his sister in view left him spent and panting. But he had the reward of reaching the gates of the drive not many seconds after Maud, and of seeing her walking﻿—more slowly now﻿—down the road that led to Platt’s. This confirmation of his suspicions enabled him momentarily to forget the blister which was forming on the heel of his left foot. He set out after her at a good pace.

The road, after the habit of country roads, wound and twisted. The quarry was frequently out of sight. And Percy’s anxiety was such that, every time Maud vanished, he broke into a gallop. Another hundred yards, and the blister no longer consented to be ignored. It cried for attention like a little child, and was rapidly insinuating itself into a position in the scheme of things where it threatened to become the centre of the world. By the time the third bend in the road was reached, it seemed to Percy that this blister had become the one great Fact in an unreal nightmare-like universe. He hobbled painfully: and when he stopped suddenly and darted back into the shelter of the hedge his foot seemed aflame. The only reason why the blister on his left heel did not at this juncture attract his entire attention was that he had become aware that there was another of equal proportions forming on his right heel.

===Chinese===

发现这一幕的当口，珀西身上那套装束根本不适合下乡走路。雷吉关于他肝脏的那番话戳中了痛处，他本打算在洗澡之前耍上一阵印度健身棒（Indian clubs），整治一下头疼和浑身肿胀不适的毛病。为了这个用途，他套上一件旧毛衣、一条灰法兰绒裤，脚上还是漆皮晚宴鞋。这身打扮他自己绝不会挑来散步，可时间飞逝：就连换双靴子也得费上几分钟；再过一两分钟，莫德就要没影了。珀西奔下楼梯，一把抓起一顶软猎帽——那顶帽子事后证明是给脑袋小他两号的人备的——冲进了园子。他恰巧赶上看见莫德拐过车道的弯角，不见了。

贝尔弗勋爵从来不属于社会上那类精力过剩、把跑步当作乐子和消遣的人。在牛津那阵子，每逢学院的人要在阴冷的下午倾巢而出，沿河岸小跑，又嚷又摇警察响板，给学院的八人赛艇鼓劲，珀西总是审慎地留在自己房里，就着茶和黄油吐司，天知道因此躲过了多少伤风咳嗽。他跑步，从来是迫不得已，而且目标明确，比如赶火车。所以他的竞技状态并不怎么样，此刻要想不让妹妹脱离视线，非得结结实实来一记冲刺不可，这一冲就冲得他筋疲力尽、气喘吁吁。不过报偿也是有的：只在莫德之后几秒钟，他便赶到了车道口，望见她正沿着通向普拉特家的大路走去——脚步已经慢了下来。疑心得到证实，他一时竟忘了左脚后跟正在磨起来的水泡。他迈开大步，紧追了上去。

乡间大路照乡间大路的脾气，弯弯曲曲，扭来扭去。猎物时常脱出视线。珀西焦灼万分，每回莫德一消失，他就撒腿狂奔。又跑出一百码，水泡可不答应再受冷落了。它像个小孩似的嚷着要人理会，而且正在飞快地往万物格局的中心钻，眼看就要当上世界的中枢。到第三个弯道的时候，珀西觉得这个水泡已经在一个恍如噩梦的非现实宇宙里，成了唯一了不起的重大事实。他一瘸一拐，疼得钻心；待他猛地刹住，闪身躲进树篱的掩蔽，那只脚简直像着了火。左脚后跟的水泡之所以没能在此时独占他的全部注意力，只因为他发觉右脚后跟上，另一个同等规格的水泡正在成形。

===Original===

Percy had stopped and sought cover in the hedge because, as he rounded the bend in the road, he perceived, before he had time to check his gallop, that Maud had also stopped. She was standing in the middle of the road, looking over her shoulder, not ten yards away. Had she seen him? It was a point that time alone could solve. No! She walked on again. She had not seen him. Lord Belpher, by means of a notable triumph of mind over matter, forgot the blisters and hurried after her.

They had now reached that point in the road where three choices offer themselves to the wayfarer. By going straight on he may win through to the village of Moresby-in-the-Vale, a charming little place with a Norman church; by turning to the left he may visit the equally seductive hamlet of Little Weeting; by turning to the right off the main road and going down a leafy lane he may find himself at the door of Platt’s farm. When Maud, reaching the crossroads, suddenly swung down the one to the left, Lord Belpher was for the moment completely baffled. Reason reasserted its way the next minute, telling him that this was but a ruse. Whether or no she had caught sight of him, there was no doubt that Maud intended to shake off any possible pursuit by taking this speciously innocent turning and making a detour. She could have no possible motive in going to Little Weeting. He had never been to Little Weeting in his life, and there was no reason to suppose that Maud had either.

The signpost informed him﻿—a statement strenuously denied by the twin-blisters﻿—that the distance to Little Weeting was one and a half miles. Lord Belpher’s view of it was that it was nearer fifty. He dragged himself along wearily. It was simpler now to keep Maud in sight, for the road ran straight: but, there being a catch in everything in this world, the process was also messier. In order to avoid being seen, it was necessary for Percy to leave the road and tramp along in the deep ditch which ran parallel to it. There is nothing halfhearted about these ditches which accompany English country roads. They know they are intended to be ditches, not mere furrows, and they behave as such. The one that sheltered Lord Belpher was so deep that only his head and neck protruded above the level of the road, and so dirty that a bare twenty yards of travel was sufficient to coat him with mud. Rain, once fallen, is reluctant to leave the English ditch. It nestles inside it for weeks, forming a rich, oatmeal-like substance which has to be stirred to be believed. Percy stirred it. He churned it. He ploughed and sloshed through it. The mud stuck to him like a brother.

Nevertheless, being a determined young man, he did not give in. Once he lost a shoe, but a little searching recovered that. On another occasion, a passing dog, seeing things going on in the ditch which in his opinion should not have been going on﻿—he was a high-strung dog, unused to coming upon heads moving along the road without bodies attached﻿—accompanied Percy for over a quarter of a mile, causing him exquisite discomfort by making sudden runs at his face. A well-aimed stone settled this little misunderstanding, and Percy proceeded on his journey alone. He had Maud well in view when, to his surprise, she left the road and turned into the gate of a house which stood not far from the church.

===Chinese===

珀西之所以急停并躲进树篱，是因为他转过路弯的那一瞬，还没来得及收住狂奔，就瞥见莫德也停住了脚。她站在大路当中，扭头朝背后张望，离他不到十码。她看见他了吗？这个问题只有时间能解答。没有！她又朝前走了。她没看见他。贝尔弗勋爵凭借精神战胜物质的赫赫战功，把水泡忘了个干净，急忙尾随上去。

走到这个地步，大路给行路人摆出了三条去路。照直往前，可以到达谷中莫尔斯比村（Moresby-in-the-Vale），一座迷人的小村，有座诺曼式教堂；往左拐，可以造访同样诱人的小威廷（Little Weeting）小村；离开大路往右拐，顺一条绿荫夹道的小径下去，便能走到普拉特农场的门口。莫德走到岔路口，冷不丁一头折进了左边那条，贝尔弗勋爵一时间彻底懵了。过了片刻，理性重又夺回了地盘，告诉他这不过是个障眼法。且不论她有没有瞧见他，毫无疑问，莫德是想借这条看似清白的岔道兜个圈子，甩脱一切可能的追踪。她去小威廷不可能有任何由头。他这辈子从未踏进过小威廷，也没理由认为莫德去过。

路牌告诉他——这一说法遭到双水泡的极力否认——到小威廷的距离是一英里半。贝尔弗勋爵自己的估算是，足有五十英里。他拖着步子，疲惫不堪地往前挪。如今要盯住莫德倒简单了，因为大路笔直：可世上万事总藏着圈套，这么一来，跟踪的营生也邋遢得多。为了不叫人看见，珀西只能离开路面，在与路平行的深沟里跋涉。英国乡间大路旁的这些水沟，做起沟来从来不打半点折扣。它们心里有数，自己生来是沟，不是浅浅的犁沟，行事也就一是一二是二。庇护贝尔弗勋爵的这条沟，深到只有脑袋和脖子露出路面之上，又脏到只消走上区区二十码，就能给他糊上满身烂泥。雨水一旦落进英国的水沟，是绝不肯走的。它在沟里一窝就是几个星期，沤出一层浓稠的、燕麦糊似的东西，不搅一搅你不会相信。珀西搅了。他搅了个底朝天。他在里头又犁又蹚。烂泥粘住他，亲如手足。

然而，他是个有决心的年轻人，不肯服输。有一回他丢了一只鞋，搜寻了一小会儿，又找了回来。另一回，一条过路的狗瞧见沟里出了在它看来不该出的状况——那是一条神经兮兮的狗，不习惯迎面碰上一颗沿着大路移动却没有身子跟着的脑袋——竟陪了珀西四分之一英里还多，还几次冷不丁朝他脸上扑，弄得他难受得无以复加。一颗瞄得极准的石子了结了这场小小的误会，珀西这才独自继续赶路。他正把莫德望得清清楚楚，不料她竟离开大路，拐进了离教堂不远的一座房子的院门，他吃了一惊。

===Original===

Lord Belpher regained the road, and remained there, a puzzled man. A dreadful thought came to him that he might have had all this trouble and anguish for no reason. This house bore the unmistakable stamp of a vicarage. Maud could have no reason that was not innocent for going there. Had he gone through all this, merely to see his sister paying a visit to a clergyman? Too late it occurred to him that she might quite easily be on visiting terms with the clergy of Little Weeting. He had forgotten that he had been away at Oxford for many weeks, a period of time in which Maud, finding life in the country weigh upon her, might easily have interested herself charitably in the life of this village. He paused irresolutely. He was baffled.

Maud, meanwhile, had rung the bell. Ever since, looking over her shoulder, she had perceived her brother Percy dodging about in the background, her active young mind had been busying itself with schemes for throwing him off the trail. She must see George that morning. She could not wait another day before establishing communication between herself and Geoffrey. But it was not till she reached Little Weeting that there occurred to her any plan that promised success.

A trim maid opened the door.

“Is the vicar in?”

“No, miss. He went out half an hour back.”

Maud was as baffled for the moment as her brother Percy, now leaning against the vicarage wall in a state of advanced exhaustion.

===Chinese===

贝尔弗勋爵回到大路上，站在原地，一头雾水。一个可怕的念头向他袭来：这一路的辛苦和苦楚，说不定全是白受。这座房子一望便知是座牧师宅。莫德上那儿去，不可能有什么不清不白的缘由。难道他受尽这一切，就为看妹妹拜访一位牧师？她跟小威廷的教士们有来往，原是再容易不过的事——这个他想起得太迟了。他忘了自己去牛津已有好几个星期，这段日子里，莫德觉得乡下的日子压得人慌，满可能发了慈善心肠，关心起这个村庄的生活来。他迟疑不定地站住了。他被弄糊涂了。

这工夫，莫德已经按响了门铃。打从回头一瞥、发现哥哥珀西在后面躲躲闪闪起，她那机灵的小脑瓜就一直在忙着盘算怎么甩掉他。那天上午她是一定要见到乔治的。在自己和杰弗里之间建立起联系，她一天也等不下去了。可是一直到进了小威廷，她才想出一条有望成功的计策。

一位利利落落的女仆开了门。

“牧师在家吗？”

“不在，小姐。他半个钟头前出门了。”

莫德这会儿跟她哥哥珀西一样没了主意——后者正倚在牧师宅的墙上，疲惫已入晚期。

===Original===

“Oh, dear!” she said.

The maid was sympathetic.

“Mr. Ferguson, the curate, miss, he’s here, if he would do.”

Maud brightened.

===Chinese===

“哎哟，糟糕！”她说。

女仆满脸同情。

“副牧师弗格森先生在家，小姐，要是他能顶事儿的话。”

莫德眼睛一亮。

===Original===

“He would do splendidly. Will you ask him if I can see him for a moment?”

“Very well, miss. What name, please?”

“He won’t know my name. Will you please tell him that a lady wishes to see him?”

“Yes, miss. Won’t you step in?”

The front door closed behind Maud. She followed the maid into the drawing-room. Presently a young small curate entered. He had a willing, benevolent face. He looked alert and helpful.

===Chinese===

“他顶事儿顶得再好没有了。劳驾你去问一声，我能不能见他一小会儿？”

“好的，小姐。请问怎么通报？”

“他不知道我的名字。就请告诉他，有一位小姐想见他。”

“是，小姐。请进来等可好？”

前门在莫德身后关上了。她跟着女仆走进客厅。不多时，进来一位年轻瘦小的副牧师。他有一张热心肠的和善面孔，看上去机灵而乐于助人。

===Original===

“You wished to see me?”

“I am so sorry to trouble you,” said Maud, rocking the young man in his tracks with a smile of dazzling brilliancy﻿—(“No trouble, I assure you,” said the curate dizzily)﻿—“but there is a man following me!”

The curate clicked his tongue indignantly.

“A rough sort of a tramp kind of man. He has been following me for miles, and I’m frightened.”

“Brute!”

“I think he’s outside now. I can’t think what he wants. Would you﻿—would you mind being kind enough to go and send him away?”

===Chinese===

“您要见我？”

“实在过意不去，要麻烦您了，”莫德说，一个光华夺目的微笑把年轻人当场震得钉在原地（“不麻烦，我向您担保，”副牧师晕晕乎乎地说）——“可是有个人在跟踪我！”

副牧师愤愤地啧了一声。

“一个粗野的、流浪汉一样的人。他跟着我走了好几英里了，我害怕。”

“畜生！”

“我想他现在就在外头。我猜不透他想干什么。您能不能——能不能劳驾走一趟，把他打发走？”

===Original===

The eyes that had settled George’s fate for all eternity flashed upon the curate, who blinked. He squared his shoulders and drew himself up. He was perfectly willing to die for her.

“If you will wait here,” he said, “I will go and send him about his business. It is disgraceful that the public highways should be rendered unsafe in this manner.”

“Thank you ever so much,” said Maud gratefully. “I can’t help thinking the poor fellow may be a little crazy. It seems so odd of him to follow me all that way. Walking in the ditch too!”

“Walking in the ditch!”

“Yes. He walked most of the way in the ditch at the side of the road. He seemed to prefer it. I can’t think why.”

===Chinese===

那双曾经一锤定音、决定了乔治终身命运的眼睛朝副牧师一闪，闪得他直眨眼。他挺起胸膛，把身子站得笔直。为了她，他就是去死也心甘情愿。

“请您在此稍候，”他说，“我去把他打发走。公共大路竟叫人闹得这般不安全，实在不成体统。”

“多谢多谢，”莫德感激地说。“我总忍不住想，那可怜的家伙没准儿有点疯。跟了我那么远的路，也真够怪的。还专挑沟里走呢！”

“走沟里！”

“是啊。一路上有大半程他都走在路旁的沟里。他好像还挺情愿。我实在想不通。”

===Original===

Lord Belpher, leaning against the wall and trying to decide whether his right or left foot hurt him the more excruciatingly, became aware that a curate was standing before him, regarding him through a pair of gold-rimmed *pince-nez* with a disapproving and hostile expression. Lord Belpher returned his gaze. Neither was favourably impressed by the other. Percy thought he had seen nicer-looking curates, and the curate thought he had seen more prepossessing tramps.

“Come, come!” said the curate. “This won’t do, my man!” A few hours earlier Lord Belpher had been startled when addressed by George as “sir.” To be called “my man” took his breath away completely.

The gift of seeing ourselves as others see us is, as the poet indicates, vouchsafed to few men. Lord Belpher, not being one of these fortunates, had not the slightest conception how intensely revolting his personal appearance was at that moment. The red-rimmed eyes, the growth of stubble on the cheeks, and the thick coating of mud which had resulted from his rambles in the ditch combined to render him a horrifying object.

“How dare you follow that young lady? I’ve a good mind to give you in charge!”

===Chinese===

贝尔弗勋爵正倚着墙，拿不定哪只脚疼得更钻心，忽然察觉一位副牧师站在面前，隔着一副金丝边夹鼻眼镜（pince-nez）打量他，一脸不以为然和敌意。贝尔弗勋爵也回瞪过去。两人彼此都没留下好印象。珀西心想，相貌更体面的副牧师他见过；副牧师心想，长相更顺眼的流浪汉他也见过。

“喂，喂！”副牧师说，“这样可不行，伙计！”几个钟头以前，乔治称他一声“先生”，贝尔弗勋爵已经吃了一惊；这一声“伙计”，更是叫他连气都喘不上来了。

如诗人所言，能照别人的眼睛看见我们自己，这份天赋世间罕有。贝尔弗勋爵既然不在这些幸运儿之列，对自己此刻的尊容有多叫人作呕，全无半点概念。眼圈通红，两腮胡茬丛生，再加上沟里跋涉糊上的厚厚一层烂泥，合起来把他造就成一幅骇人的景象。

“你竟敢跟踪那位小姐？我真想把你送官究办！”

===Original===

Percy was outraged.

“I’m her brother!” He was about to substantiate the statement by giving his name, but stopped himself. He had had enough of letting his name come out on occasions like the present. When the policeman had arrested him in the Haymarket, his first act had been to thunder his identity at the man: and the policeman, without saying in so many words that he disbelieved him, had hinted scepticism by replying that he himself was the king of Brixton. “I’m her brother!” he repeated thickly.

The curate’s disapproval deepened. In a sense, we are all brothers; but that did not prevent him from considering that this mud-stained derelict had made an impudent and abominable misstatement of fact. Not unnaturally he came to the conclusion that he had to do with a victim of the Demon Rum.

===Chinese===

珀西火冒三丈。

“我是她哥哥！”他本想报出姓名来给这句话作证，又缩住了。这类场合亮出自己的名号，他已经领教够了。在干草市场街被警察逮住的时候，他干的头一件事就是冲那人轰雷似的报出自己的身份；那警察倒没有明说不信，只是回了一句他自己乃是布里克斯顿（Brixton）之王，把怀疑给暗示了出来。“我是她哥哥！”他含混不清地又说了一遍。

副牧师的不以为然又深了一层。广义上说，我们人人皆是兄弟；可这不妨碍他认定，这个浑身烂泥的落魄汉厚颜无耻地撒了一个可憎的弥天大谎。他自然而然断定，自己面对的是一个中了酒魔（Demon Rum）之毒的不幸者。

===Original===

“You ought to be ashamed of yourself,” he said severely. “Sad piece of human wreckage as you are, you speak like an educated man. Have you no self-respect? Do you never search your heart and shudder at the horrible degradation which you have brought on yourself by sheer weakness of will?”

He raised his voice. The subject of Temperance was one very near to the curate’s heart. The vicar himself had complimented him only yesterday on the good his sermons against the drink evil were doing in the village, and the landlord of the Three Pigeons down the road had on several occasions spoken bitter things about blighters who came taking the living away from honest folks.

“It is easy enough to stop if you will but use a little resolution. You say to yourself, ‘Just one won’t hurt me!’ Perhaps not. But can you be content with just one? Ah! No, my man, there is no middle way for such as you. It must be all or nothing. Stop it now﻿—now, while you still retain some semblance of humanity. Soon it will be too late! Kill that craving! Stifle it! Strangle it! Make up your mind now﻿—now, that not another drop of the accursed stuff shall pass your lips.﻿ ﻿…”

The curate paused. He perceived that enthusiasm was leading him away from the main issue. “A little perseverance,” he concluded rapidly, “and you will soon find that cocoa gives you exactly the same pleasure. And now will you please be getting along. You have frightened the young lady, and she cannot continue her walk unless I assure her that you have gone away.”

===Chinese===

“你该为自己害臊，”他厉声说。“你虽是人间一件凄惨的残骸，谈吐倒像个受过教育的人。你就没有一点自尊吗？你就从不扪心自问，为自己单凭意志软弱弄到这般可怕的堕落而不寒而栗吗？”

他提高了嗓门。戒酒这个题目，正是副牧师的心头大事。就在昨天，牧师本人还当面夸奖他，说他在布道里抨击酒害，给村里带来了好处；而前头路上的“三鸽子”酒馆（the Three Pigeons）的老板，也早不止一回咬牙切齿，咒骂那些抢走老实人饭碗的讨厌鬼。

“只要你肯拿出一点儿决心，戒掉并不难。你对自己说：‘就喝一口，碍不了事！’也许真碍不了事。可你能甘心只喝一口吗？啊！不能，伙计，像你这样的人没有中间道路可走。要么全戒，要么全完。现在就戒——就现在，趁你身上还剩几分人样。用不了多久就太迟了！掐灭那个瘾！把它闷死！把它勒死！现在就下定决心——就在此刻，叫那该死的东西一滴也过不了你的嘴唇。……”

副牧师停了停。他发觉自己热情过头，扯离了正题。“只要稍稍坚持，”他赶紧收尾，“你很快就会发现，可可带给你的乐趣分毫不差。现在就请你上路吧。你把那位小姐吓着了，除非我向她担保你已经走掉，她没法接着散步。”

===Original===

Fatigue, pain and the annoyance of having to listen to this man’s well-meant but ill-judged utterances had combined to induce in Percy a condition bordering on hysteria. He stamped his foot, and uttered a howl as the blister warned him with a sharp twinge that this sort of behaviour could not be permitted.

“Stop talking!” he bellowed. “Stop talking like an idiot! I’m going to stay here till that girl comes out, if have to wait all day!”

The curate regarded Percy thoughtfully. Percy was no Hercules: but then, neither was the curate. And in any case, though no Hercules, Percy was undeniably an ugly-looking brute. Strategy, rather than force, seemed to the curate to be indicated. He paused a while, as one who weighs pros and cons, then spoke briskly, with the air of the man who has decided to yield a point with a good grace.

===Chinese===

疲惫、疼痛，再加上不得不听这个人一番用心虽好、判断却糟的高论，合起来把珀西逼到了近乎歇斯底里的地步。他跺了一下脚，随即嚎了一嗓子——水泡以一阵钻心的刺痛警告他，这种行为是不被允许的。

“住口！”他吼道。“别再像傻子一样讲话！我就守在这儿，等那位姑娘出来，哪怕等上一整天！”

副牧师若有所思地打量珀西。珀西算不上赫拉克勒斯（Hercules）；可转念一想，副牧师自己也算不上。而且无论如何，纵然不是赫拉克勒斯，珀西无疑是一副凶相毕露的蛮相。在副牧师看来，眼下该用计，不该用武。他踌躇片刻，像个权衡利弊的人，随即干脆利落地开了口，一派识趣让步还让得漂漂亮亮的气度。

===Original===

“Dear, dear!” he said. “That won’t do! You say you are this young lady’s brother?”

“Yes, I do!”

“Then perhaps you had better come with me into the house and we will speak to her.”

“All right.”

“Follow me.”

===Chinese===

“哎呀，哎呀！”他说，“这可不行！你说你是这位小姐的哥哥？”

“我说了，没错！”

“那么你最好随我进屋去，咱们当面同她讲。”

“行。”

“跟我来。”

===Original===

Percy followed him. Down the trim gravel walk they passed, and up the neat stone steps. Maud, peeping through the curtains, thought herself the victim of a monstrous betrayal or equally monstrous blunder. But she did not know the Rev. Cyril Ferguson. No general, adroitly leading the enemy on by strategic retreat, ever had a situation more thoroughly in hand. Passing with his companion through the open door, he crossed the hall to another door, discreetly closed.

“Wait in here,” he said. Lord Belpher moved unsuspectingly forward. A hand pressed sharply against the small of his back. Behind him a door slammed and a key clicked. He was trapped. Groping in Egyptian darkness, his hands met a coat, then a hat, then an umbrella. Then he stumbled over a golf-club and fell against a wall. It was too dark to see anything, but his sense of touch told him all he needed to know. He had been added to the vicar’s collection of odds and ends in the closet reserved for that purpose.

He groped his way to the door and kicked it. He did not repeat the performance. His feet were in no shape for kicking things.

Percy’s gallant soul abandoned the struggle. With a feeble oath, he sat down on a box containing croquet implements, and gave himself up to thought.

===Chinese===

珀西跟着他去了。两人走过修剪齐整的砾石小径，踏上干干净净的石阶。莫德隔着窗帘偷看，只道自己成了天大背叛的受害者，要不就是天大误会的受害者。可她不认识西里尔·弗格森牧师（the Rev. Cyril Ferguson）。从没有哪位将军，靠战略后撤把敌人诱得团团转，能把局面捏得比他更牢。他带着同伴穿过敞开的门，越过门厅，走向另一扇门——一扇谨慎关着的门。

“在这里头等一等。”他说。贝尔弗勋爵毫无戒心地往前走。一只手猛地抵住他的腰眼。身后一扇门砰地关上，钥匙咔哒一拧。他被困住了。他伸手在一片埃及般的漆黑里摸索，手碰到一件外套，又一顶帽子，再一把伞。接着他绊上一根高尔夫球杆，跌在墙上。黑得什么也看不见，可触觉把该知道的统统告诉了他：牧师专辟那间壁橱收存零星什物，如今这份收藏里又添了他一件。

他摸索到门口，抬脚踢门。这一手他没有再来第二回。他那双脚的现状，不适合用来踢东西。

珀西那颗英武的灵魂放弃了挣扎。他低低咒了一声，在一口装着槌球器具的木箱上坐下，听凭思想驰骋。

===Original===

“You’ll be quite safe now,” the curate was saying in the adjoining room, not without a touch of complacent self-approval such as becomes the victor in a battle of wits. “I have locked him in the cupboard. He will be quite happy there.” An incorrect statement this. “You may now continue your walk in perfect safety.”

“Thank you ever so much,” said Maud. “But I do hope he won’t be violent when you let him out.”

“I shall not let him out,” replied the curate, who, though brave, was not rash. “I shall depute the task to a worthy fellow named Willis, in whom I shall have every confidence. He﻿—he is, in fact, our local blacksmith!”

And so it came about that when, after a vigil that seemed to last for a lifetime, Percy heard the key turn in the lock and burst forth seeking whom he might devour, he experienced an almost instant quieting of his excited nervous system. Confronting him was a vast man whose muscles, like those of that other and more celebrated village blacksmith, were plainly as strong as iron bands.

===Chinese===

“现在您只管放心，”副牧师在隔壁屋里说，话音里带着几分洋洋自得的自我嘉许——斗智的赢家，理当如此。“我把他锁进壁橱了。他在那里头会非常舒坦。”此言差矣。“您现在可以接着散步，包您万无一失。”

“多谢多谢，”莫德说。“不过我真心盼望，您放他出来的时候，他别动粗。”

“我不放他出来，”副牧师答道。他虽然勇敢，却并不鲁莽。“我要把这桩差事托付给一位可敬的汉子，名叫威利斯（Willis），此人我一万个放心。他——说起来，他就是我们本地的铁匠！”

于是便有了这样一幕：珀西熬过了一场形同半生的守夜，听见钥匙在锁孔里一转，破橱而出，正欲寻人吞噬，他的兴奋神经却在几乎一瞬间平复了下来。迎面站着的是个魁梧大汉，一身腱子肉，同那位更有名气的乡村铁匠一模一样，分明结实得像铁箍一般。

===Original===

This man eyed Percy with a chilly eye.

“Well,” he said. “What’s troublin’ you?”

Percy gulped. The man’s mere appearance was a sedative.

“Er﻿—nothing!” he replied. “Nothing!”

“There better hadn’t be!” said the man darkly. “Mr. Ferguson give me this to give to you. Take it!”

===Chinese===

这人冷冰冰地打量着珀西。

“怎么着，”他说，“你有啥烦心的事？”

珀西倒抽一口冷气。这人的外表本身就是一剂镇静药。

“呃——没什么！”他答道。“没什么！”

“最好没什么！”这人阴沉沉地说。“弗格森先生交给我，叫我转交给你。拿着！”

===Original===

Percy took it. It was a shilling.

“And this.”

The second gift was a small paper pamphlet. It was entitled “Now’s the Time!” and seemed to be a story of some kind. At any rate, Percy’s eyes, before they began to swim in a manner that prevented steady reading, caught the words “Job Roberts had always been a hard-drinking man, but one day, as he was coming out of the bar-parlour﻿ ﻿…” He was about to hurl it from him, when he met the other’s eye and desisted. Rarely had Lord Belpher encountered a man with a more speaking eye.

===Chinese===

珀西接了过来。是一枚一先令。

“还有这个。”

第二件赠品是一本小册子。册子题为《现在正是时候！》（“Now’s the Time!”），像是讲的一个故事。反正，珀西的眼睛在开始发花打晃、没法稳稳当当读书之前，捞到了这么几句：“乔布·罗伯茨向来是个贪杯的汉子，可是有一天，他正从酒吧间出来……”他正要把它甩出去，一抬眼撞上那人的目光，只得作罢。贝尔弗勋爵平生少遇见一双比这更会说话的眼睛。

===Original===

“And now you get along,” said the man. “You pop off. And I’m going to watch you do it, too. And, if I find you sneakin’ off to the Three Pigeons﻿ ﻿…”

His pause was more eloquent than his speech and nearly as eloquent as his eye. Lord Belpher tucked the tract into his sweater, pocketed the shilling, and left the house. For nearly a mile down the well-remembered highway he was aware of a Presence in his rear, but he continued on his way without a glance behind.

===Chinese===

“现在你走吧，”那人说。“你就拔脚开溜。我还偏要看着你溜。要叫我瞅见你偷偷溜去‘三鸽子’……”

他这一顿，比他的话更有雄辩之力，几乎赶得上他那双眼睛。贝尔弗勋爵把小册子掖进毛衣，先令揣进兜里，出了那座房子。在那条记忆犹新的大路上走出去将近一英里，他始终觉得背后有个“什么”跟着，可他头也不回，只顾赶自己的路。

===Original===

> Like one that on a lonely road
 

 Doth walk in fear and dread;
 

 And, having once looked back, walks on
 

 And turns no more his head!
 

 Because he knows a frightful fiend
 

 Doth close behind him tread!

===Chinese===

> 恰似有人在荒凉的路上
> 满心恐惧惊惶；
> 回头望了一眼，便只顾朝前走，
> 再不敢回头张望！
> 因为他知道，有个可怕的魔怪
> 正紧紧踏步跟在他身后！

===Original===

Maud made her way across the fields to the cottage down by Platt’s. Her heart was as light as the breeze that ruffled the green hedges. Gaily she tripped towards the cottage door. Her hand was just raised to knock, when from within came the sound of a well-known voice.

She had reached her goal, but her father had anticipated her. Lord Marshmoreton had selected the same moment as herself for paying a call upon George Bevan.

Maud tiptoed away, and hurried back to the castle. Never before had she so clearly realized what a handicap an adhesive family can be to a young girl.

===Chinese===

莫德穿过田野，朝普拉特家隔壁那座小屋走去。她的心轻快极了，像拂乱绿篱的那阵微风。她欢欢喜喜，一路小碎步奔向小屋门前。手刚抬起来要敲门，屋里忽然传出一个熟悉的声音。

她的目的地到了，可她父亲抢在了她前头。马什莫顿勋爵挑中登门拜访乔治·贝文的时刻，同她分毫不差。

莫德蹑手蹑脚地退走，匆匆赶回城堡。一个如影随形的家庭对年轻姑娘能有多大妨碍，她从前从没有体会得这么清楚过。

## XVI / XVI

===Original===

At the moment of Lord Marshmoreton’s arrival, George was reading a letter from Billie Dore, which had come by that morning’s post. It dealt mainly with the vicissitudes experienced by Miss Dore’s friend, Miss Sinclair, in her relations with the man Spenser Gray. Spenser Gray, it seemed, had been behaving oddly. Ardent towards Miss Sinclair almost to an embarrassing point in the early stages of their acquaintance, he had suddenly cooled; at a recent lunch had behaved with a strange aloofness; and now, at this writing, had vanished altogether, leaving nothing behind him but an abrupt note to the effect that he had been compelled to go abroad and that, much as it was to be regretted, he and she would probably never meet again.

“And if,” wrote Miss Dore, justifiably annoyed, “after saying all those things to the poor kid and telling her she was the only thing in sight, he thinks he can just slide off with a ‘Goodbye! Good luck! and God bless you!’ he’s got another guess coming. And that’s not all. He hasn’t gone abroad! I saw him in Piccadilly this afternoon. He saw me, too, and what do you think he did? Ducked down a side-street, if you please. He must have run like a rabbit, at that, because, when I got there, he was nowhere to be seen. I tell you, George, there’s something funny about all this.”

Having been made once or twice before the confidant of the tempestuous romances of Billie’s friends, which always seemed to go wrong somewhere in the middle and to die a natural death before arriving at any definite point, George was not particularly interested, except in so far as the letter afforded rather comforting evidence that he was not the only person in the world who was having trouble of the kind. He skimmed through the rest of it, and had just finished when there was a sharp rap at the front door.

===Chinese===

马什莫顿勋爵上门的那一刻，乔治正在读一封比莉·多尔写来的信，信是当天早晨的邮班送到的。信里主要讲的是多尔小姐的朋友辛克莱小姐，同那个斯宾塞·格雷之间经历的种种变故。斯宾塞·格雷的行止，看来颇为古怪。相识之初，他对辛克莱小姐一片火热，几乎热得叫人难为情；忽然之间他又冷了下来；不久前一次午餐上，态度冷淡得蹊跷；而到了落笔的此刻，他已全然没了踪影，只留下一张措辞突兀的短简，大意说他是不得已要出国，他同她多半往后不会再见面了——虽然此事委实令人遗憾。

“要是那可怜的孩子把他说的那些话都听尽了——他说她是他眼里独一份儿的宝贝——到头来他还以为撂下一句‘再见！祝你好运！愿上帝保佑你！’就能溜之大吉，”多尔小姐写道，恼得颇有道理，“那他可是打错了算盘。这还不算完。他压根儿没出国！今天下午我在皮卡迪利看见他了。他也看见我了，你猜他怎么样？他居然一头扎进一条小巷子里去了，你说说看。就他那个跑法，准是跟兔子似的撒腿飞奔，因为我赶到那儿，人早没影了。我跟你说，乔治，这事情处处透着古怪。”

比莉那些朋友们的暴风骤雨般的罗曼史，前前后后已有一两回拿他做过知心人；那些罗曼史总是进行到半途就走了样，还没走到什么明确的地步便寿终正寝。所以乔治兴趣不大，只有一处例外：这封信倒是提供了颇为宽心的证据，足见这世界上碰上同类麻烦的并非他一人。他把信的其余部分匆匆浏览一过，刚读完，前门上响起了一阵急促的敲门声。

===Original===

“Come in!” called George.

There entered a sturdy little man of middle age whom at first sight George could not place. And yet he had the impression that he had seen him before. Then he recognized him as the gardener to whom he had given the note for Maud that day at the castle. The alteration in the man’s costume was what had momentarily baffled George. When they had met in the rose-garden, the other had been arrayed in untidy gardening clothes. Now, presumably in his Sunday suit, it was amusing to observe how almost dapper he had become. Really, you might have passed him in the lane and taken him for some neighbouring squire.

George’s heart raced. Your lover is ever optimistic, and he could conceive of no errand that could have brought this man to his cottage unless he was charged with the delivery of a note from Maud. He spared a moment from his happiness to congratulate himself on having picked such an admirable go-between. Here evidently, was one of those trusty old retainers you read about, faithful, willing, discreet, ready to do anything for “the little missy” (bless her heart!). Probably he had danced Maud on his knee in her infancy, and with a doglike affection had watched her at her childish sports. George beamed at the honest fellow, and felt in his pocket to make sure that a suitable tip lay safely therein.

===Chinese===

“进来！”乔治喊道。

进来的是个壮实的小个子中年人，乍一看，乔治认不出他是谁。可他又有种印象，觉得从前见过此人。随即他认出来了：正是那天在城堡里，他托他给莫德捎字条的那位园丁。一时叫乔治发懵的，是此人装束上的变化。玫瑰园里相遇那回，对方穿的是一身邋遢的园艺旧衣；此刻想必换上了上教堂的体面行头，那份近乎翩翩的利落劲儿，看着着实有趣。说真的，你在乡间小道上同他擦肩而过，多半会把他当成左近的哪一位乡绅。

乔治的心怦怦狂跳起来。做情人的一向乐观，他想不出这还能有什么差事把此人引到他的小屋来，除非是受托捎来了莫德的一封书信。他从幸福里匀出一霎，庆幸自己选中了这么一位可敬可佩的牵线人。眼前这位显然就是说部戏文里常见的那类忠心耿耿的老家人：忠实、勤快、口风紧，肯为“小小姐”（老天保佑她！）赴汤蹈火。多半莫德还在孩提的时候，就在他膝头上蹦跳过，他也像忠犬恋主一般看着她做孩童的游戏。乔治朝这位老实人满脸堆笑，一面伸手摸了摸口袋，确认里面稳稳当当搁着一笔像样的小费。

===Original===

“Good morning,” he said.

“Good morning,” replied the man.

A purist might have said he spoke gruffly and without geniality. But that is the beauty of these old retainers. They make a point of deliberately trying to deceive strangers as to the goldenness of their hearts by adopting a forbidding manner. And “Good morning!” Not “Good morning, sir!” Sturdy independence, you observe, as befits a free man. George closed the door carefully. He glanced into the kitchen. Mrs. Platt was not there. All was well.

===Chinese===

“早上好。”他说。

“早上好。”那人应道。

讲究的人或许要说，他答话粗声粗气，不见和气。可这正是这类老家人的妙处。他们存心板起一副拒人千里的面孔，好叫外人错认了他们那颗金子般的心。还有，他说的是“早上好！”不是“早上好，先生！”独立不羁，你瞧，这才配得上一个自由人。乔治小心地把门关上。他朝厨房里瞥了一眼。普拉特太太不在那儿。万事顺遂。

===Original===

“You have brought a note from Lady Maud?”

The honest fellow’s rather dour expression seemed to grow a shade bleaker.

“If you are alluding to Lady Maud Marsh, my daughter,” he replied frostily, “I have not!”

===Chinese===

“您捎来了莫德小姐的字条？”

老实人那副执拗的面色，似乎又添了几分灰暗。

“倘若您指的是莫德·马什小姐——小女的——”他冷冰冰地答道，“那我可没有！”

===Original===

For the past few days George had been no stranger to shocks, and had indeed come almost to regard them as part of the normal everyday life; but this latest one had a stumbling effect.

“I beg your pardon?” he said.

“So you ought to,” replied the earl.

===Chinese===

这几天来，打击之于乔治早已不是稀客，他甚至几乎已经把它们看作了日常安生日子的一部分；可这最新的一下还是叫他绊了个趔趄。

“您说什么？”他问。

“你也该问才对。”伯爵回道。

===Original===

George swallowed once or twice to relieve a curious dryness of the mouth.

“Are you Lord Marshmoreton?”

“I am.”

“Good Lord!”

===Chinese===

乔治咽了一两回唾沫，好解一解嘴里那股说不出的干渴。

“您就是马什莫顿勋爵？”

“正是。”

“我的老天！”

===Original===

“You seem surprised.”

“It’s nothing!” muttered George. “At least, you﻿—I mean to say﻿ ﻿… It’s only that there’s a curious resemblance between you and one of your gardeners at the castle. I﻿—I daresay you have noticed it yourself.”

“My hobby is gardening.”

Light broke upon George. “Then was it really you﻿—?”

“It was!”

===Chinese===

“你好像很吃惊。”

“没什么！”乔治咕哝道，“至少，您——我是说……不过是您同城堡里的一位园丁长得出奇地像罢了。我——我想您自己也留意到了。”

“我的爱好就是园艺。”

乔治恍然大悟。“这么说，那天果真是您——？”

“正是我！”

===Original===

George sat down. “This opens up a new line of thought!” he said.

Lord Marshmoreton remained standing. He shook his head sternly.

“It won’t do, Mr.﻿—I have never heard your name.”

“Bevan,” replied George, rather relieved at being able to remember it in the midst of his mental turmoil.

“It won’t do, Mr. Bevan. It must stop. I allude to this absurd entanglement between yourself and my daughter. It must stop at once.”

===Chinese===

乔治坐了下来。“这可开辟出一条崭新的思路！”他说。

马什莫顿勋爵站着没动。他板起脸摇了摇头。

“这可不行，先生——我还没听说过你的名字呢。”

“贝文，”乔治答道。心绪纷乱之际居然还记得起自己的名字，他暗自颇觉宽慰。

“这不行，贝文先生。必须就此打住。我说的是你同我小女之间这桩荒唐的纠葛。必须立刻了断。”

===Original===

It seemed to George that such an entanglement could hardly be said to have begun, but he did not say so.

Lord Marshmoreton resumed his remarks. Lady Caroline had sent him to the cottage to be stern, and his firm resolve to be stern lent his style of speech something of the measured solemnity and careful phrasing of his occasional orations in the House of Lords.

“I have no wish to be unduly hard upon the indiscretions of Youth. Youth is the period of Romance, when the heart rules the head. I myself was once a young man.”

===Chinese===

在乔治听来，这桩纠葛恐怕还谈不上开了头，不过他没有把这话说出口。

马什莫顿勋爵接着往下讲。这趟上小屋来扮严父，是卡罗琳夫人的吩咐；而这副严父非做成不可的决心，也给他言语间添了几分他偶尔登上上议院讲坛时那种一板一眼的庄严、字斟句酌的腔调。

“对于青年人的失于检点，我并无苛责过甚之意。青年是浪漫的时期，是心灵支配头脑的时期。我本人也曾经做过一回年轻人。”

===Original===

“Well, you’re practically that now,” said George.

“Eh?” cried Lord Marshmoreton, forgetting the thread of his discourse in the shock of pleased surprise.

“You don’t look a day over forty.”

“Oh, come, come, my boy!﻿ ﻿… I mean, Mr. Bevan.”

===Chinese===

“嗐，您如今差不多也正是嘛。”乔治说。

“嗯？”马什莫顿勋爵叫出了声，又惊又喜，把话头忘了个精光。

“您看上去连一天都不超过四十岁。”

“哦，得了，得了，小伙子！……我是说，贝文先生。”

===Original===

“You don’t honestly.”

“I’m forty-eight.”

“The Prime of Life.”

“And you don’t think I look it?”

“You certainly don’t.”

“Well, well, well! By the way, have you tobacco, my boy? I came without my pouch.”

===Chinese===

“您当真一点儿也不显。”

“我今年四十八。”

“人生的盛年。”

“你真觉得我看上去不像这个岁数？”

“您确确实实不像。”

“嘿，嘿，嘿！对了，小伙子，你这儿有烟丝吗？我的烟袋忘带了。”

===Original===

“Just at your elbow. Pretty good stuff. I bought it in the village.”

“The same I smoke myself.”

“Quite a coincidence.”

“Distinctly.”

“Match?”

“Thank you, I have one.”

===Chinese===

“就在您手边。烟丝还不赖，是在村里买的。”

“我自己抽的正是这一种。”

“可真巧了。”

“可不是嘛。”

“火柴？”

“谢谢，我有。”

===Original===

George filled his own pipe. The thing was becoming a love-feast.

“What was I saying?” said Lord Marshmoreton, blowing a comfortable cloud. “Oh, yes.” He removed his pipe from his mouth with a touch of embarrassment. “Yes, yes, to be sure!”

There was an awkward silence.

===Chinese===

乔治装上自己的烟斗。这场面眼看要办成一场交心联欢会了。

“我方才说到哪儿了？”马什莫顿勋爵喷出一口受用的烟云。“哦，对了。”他略带窘意地把烟斗从嘴边拿开。“对，对，一点不错！”

接着是一阵尴尬的沉默。

===Original===

“You must see for yourself,” said the earl, “how impossible it is.”

George shook his head.

“I may be slow at grasping a thing, but I’m bound to say I can’t see that.”

Lord Marshmoreton recalled some of the things his sister had told him to say. “For one thing, what do we know of you? You are a perfect stranger.”

===Chinese===

“你自己总该看得明白，”伯爵说，“这事情是万难成真的。”

乔治摇了摇头。

“我这个人领悟事情兴许迟钝，可我不得不说，这一层我看不出来。”

马什莫顿勋爵回想起妹妹嘱咐他要讲的其中几条。“首先，我们对你的底细知道些什么？你完全是个陌生人。”

===Original===

“Well, we’re all getting acquainted pretty quick, don’t you think? I met your son in Piccadilly and had a long talk with him, and now you are paying me a neighbourly visit.”

“This was not intended to be a social call.”

“But it has become one.”

“And then, that is one point I wish to make, you know. Ours is an old family, I would like to remind you that there were Marshmoretons in Belpher before the War of the Roses.”

===Chinese===

“可不是，咱们大伙儿熟络得都快得很，您说是不是？我在皮卡迪利碰上了您儿子，同他长谈了一场，如今您又来串街坊的门了。”

“我这一趟可不是登门拜客来的。”

“可这一趟如今已经成了拜客啦。”

“再者说了，有一点我想请你明白。我们家是古老的门第，我不妨提醒你一句，远在玫瑰战争之前，贝尔弗就有马什莫顿家的人了。”

===Original===

“There were Bevans in Brooklyn before the B.R.T.”

“I beg your pardon?”

“I was only pointing out that I can trace my ancestry a long way. You have to trace things a long way in Brooklyn, if you want to find them.”

“I have never heard of Brooklyn.”

===Chinese===

“布鲁克林捷运铁路（B.R.T.）还没影儿的时候，布鲁克林就有贝文家了。”

“你说什么？”

“我只是想指出，我的祖先一样能往上追出老远。在布鲁克林，想找什么东西，本来就得一路追出老远才找得着。”

“布鲁克林，没听说过。”

===Original===

“You’ve heard of New York?”

“Certainly.”

“New York’s one of the outlying suburbs.”

===Chinese===

“纽约总听说过吧？”

“那当然。”

“纽约算是它外围的一个郊区。”

===Original===

Lord Marshmoreton relit his pipe. He had a feeling that they were wandering from the point.

“It is quite impossible.”

“I can’t see it.”

“Maud is so young.”

“Your daughter could be nothing else.”

===Chinese===

马什莫顿勋爵重新点着了烟斗。他隐隐觉得，两人已经离题跑野了。

“此事万万不能。”

“我看不出怎么不能。”

“莫德还这样年轻。”

“您的女儿，除了年轻还能是别的吗。”

===Original===

“Too young to know her own mind,” pursued Lord Marshmoreton, resolutely crushing down a flutter of pleasure. There was no doubt that this singularly agreeable man was making things very difficult for him. It was disarming to discover that he was really capital company﻿—the best, indeed, that the earl could remember to have discovered in the more recent period of his rather lonely life. “At present, of course, she fancies that she is very much in love with you﻿ ﻿… It is absurd!”

“You needn’t tell me that,” said George. Really, it was only the fact that people seemed to go out of their way to call at his cottage and tell him that Maud loved him that kept him from feeling his cause perfectly hopeless. “It’s incredible. It’s a miracle.”

“You are a romantic young man, and you no doubt for the moment suppose that you are in love with her.”

“No!” George was not going to allow a remark like that to pass unchallenged. “You are wrong there. As far as I am concerned, there is no question of its being momentary or supposititious or anything of that kind. I am in love with your daughter. I was from the first moment I saw her. I always shall be. She is the only girl in the world!”

===Chinese===

“年纪轻得还不知道自己的心思呢，”马什莫顿勋爵接着说，一面把心头掠起的一阵欢喜狠狠按了下去。无可怀疑，这位妙不可言的人物存心叫他事情难办。更叫他无从招架的是，他发觉此人实在是个顶好的伴儿——说实在的，在他那相当孤寂的生活的近年以来，他记忆里再没发现过这么好的伴儿。“眼下，她自然自以为同你爱得死去活来……真是荒唐！”

“这倒不劳您告诉我，”乔治说。说实在的，也全亏人们仿佛不辞辛苦、特地上他的小屋来告知他莫德爱着他，他这才没有觉得自己的指望已全然无望。“真是难以置信。简直是奇迹。”

“你是个浪漫的年轻人，此刻无疑自以为是在爱着她。”

“不对！”乔治决不肯让这样的议论就这么混过去。“这一点您可说错了。就我而论，根本不存在什么一时冲动、想入非非之类的说法。我爱上的是您的女儿。打见到她的头一刻起我就爱上了她。今后也永远爱下去。她是这世界上独一无二的姑娘！”

===Original===

“Stuff and nonsense!”

“Not at all. Absolute, cold fact.”

“You have known her so little time.”

“Long enough.”

===Chinese===

“浑话！一派胡言！”

“绝非胡言。是千真万确、冷冰冰的事实。”

“你认识她统共才多少日子。”

“尽够了。”

===Original===

Lord Marshmoreton sighed. “You are upsetting things terribly.”

“Things are upsetting me terribly.”

“You are causing a great deal of trouble and annoyance.”

“So did Romeo.”

===Chinese===

马什莫顿勋爵叹了口气。“你把事情搅得一团糟。”

“是事情把我搅得一团糟。”

“你惹下了好大的麻烦，招来好大的气恼。”

“罗密欧也是这样。”

===Original===

“Eh?”

“I said﻿—So did Romeo.”

“I don’t know anything about Romeo.”

“As far as love is concerned, I begin where he left off.”

===Chinese===

“嗯？”

“我说——罗密欧也是这样。”

“罗密欧的事我一概不知。”

“论谈恋爱这件事，我正是从他止步的地方接着往前走的。”

===Original===

“I wish I could persuade you to be sensible.”

“That’s just what I think I am.”

“I wish I could get you to see my point of view.”

“I do see your point of view. But dimly. You see, my own takes up such a lot of the foreground.”

===Chinese===

“但愿我能劝你明白事理。”

“我恰恰觉得自己明白得很。”

“但愿我能叫你看到我的立场。”

“您的立场我是看见了的。不过模模糊糊。您要知道，我自己的立场把前景占得太满了。”

===Original===

There was a pause.

“Then I am afraid,” said Lord Marshmoreton, “that we must leave matters as they stand.”

“Until they can be altered for the better.”

“We will say no more about it now.”

“Very well.”

===Chinese===

接着停了一停。

“那么恐怕，”马什莫顿勋爵说，“我们只好让事情暂且照老样子搁着了。”

“等到日后能把它们往好处变一变再说。”

“眼下这话咱们就不再多说了。”

“很好。”

===Original===

“But I must ask you to understand clearly that I shall have to do everything in my power to stop what I look on as an unfortunate entanglement.”

“I understand.”

“Very well.”

Lord Marshmoreton coughed. George looked at him with some surprise. He had supposed the interview to be at an end, but the other made no move to go. There seemed to be something on the earl’s mind.

“There is﻿—ah﻿—just one other thing,” said Lord Marshmoreton. He coughed again. He felt embarrassed. “Just﻿—just one other thing,” he repeated.

===Chinese===

“不过我必须请你明白无误地了解：凡我力所能及，我势必要设法阻止这桩我视为不幸的纠葛。”

“我明白。”

“很好。”

马什莫顿勋爵咳嗽了一声。乔治略带诧异地望着他。他原以为这场谈话已经收场，可对方半点没有起身要走的意思。伯爵心里似乎还压着一桩事。

“还有——呃——还有一件小事，”马什莫顿勋爵说。他又咳嗽了一声。他觉得不好意思。“就——就一件小事，”他重复道。

---

===Original===

The reason for Lord Marshmoreton’s visit to George had been twofold. In the first place, Lady Caroline had told him to go. That would have been reason enough. But what made the visit imperative was an unfortunate accident of which he had only that morning been made aware.

It will be remembered that Billie Dore had told George that the gardener with whom she had become so friendly had taken her name and address with a view later on to send her some of his roses. The scrap of paper on which this information had been written was now lost. Lord Marshmoreton had been hunting for it since breakfast without avail.

===Chinese===

马什莫顿勋爵这趟登乔治的门，缘由有二。头一条，是卡罗琳夫人叫他来的。这一条原也尽够了。可叫这一趟势在必行的，是一桩倒霉的意外——这桩意外，直到那天早晨他才风闻。

想必读者还记得，比莉·多尔曾告诉乔治，那位同她处得极投机的园丁抄下了她的姓名住址，预备日后寄几枝自己种的玫瑰给她。写着这一讯息的那张纸片，如今找不到了。打早饭起，马什莫顿勋爵就翻箱倒柜地寻它，至今一无所获。

===Original===

Billie Dore had made a decided impression upon Lord Marshmoreton. She belonged to a type which he had never before encountered, and it was one which he had found more than agreeable. Her knowledge of roses and the proper feeling which she manifested towards rose-growing as a lifework consolidated the earl’s liking for her. Never, in his memory, had he come across so sensible and charming a girl; and he had looked forward with a singular intensity to meeting her again. And now some too zealous housemaid, tidying up after the irritating manner of her species, had destroyed the only clue to her identity.

It was not for some time after this discovery that hope dawned again for Lord Marshmoreton. Only after he had given up the search for the missing paper as fruitless did he recall that it was in George’s company that Billie had first come into his life. Between her, then, and himself George was the only link.

It was primarily for the purpose of getting Billie’s name and address from George that he had come to the cottage. And now that the moment had arrived for touching upon the subject, he felt a little embarrassed.

===Chinese===

比莉·多尔给马什莫顿勋爵留下了决然不浅的印象。她属于他平生从未遇过的一路人，而这一路人他发觉实在可爱得很。她懂玫瑰，又把种玫瑰当作终身事业，对这份事业所表露的那份恰如其分的热情，叫伯爵对她的喜爱愈发笃实。平生记忆所及，他从没遇上过这么明白事理、这么招人喜欢的姑娘；他心心念念，急切盼着同她重逢。可如今，某个热心过火的女仆，照她那一族类恼人的老规矩归置屋子，竟把标识她身份的唯一线索毁了个干净。

打从有了这个发现，又过了好一阵，马什莫顿勋爵心头才重新透出光来。直到他把寻找纸片的事断念死心，他才记起：比莉头一回闯进他的生活，正是在乔治的陪伴之下。那么，在她同他自己之间，乔治是仅存的纽带。

他这趟来小屋，头一条本意便是要从乔治口里问出比莉的姓名住址。可眼下话真要说到题上了，他倒有几分局促起来。

---

===Original===

“When you visited the castle,” he said, “when you visited the castle﻿ ﻿…”

“Last Thursday,” said George helpfully.

“Exactly. When you visited the castle last Thursday, there was a young lady with you.”

Not realizing that the subject had been changed, George was under the impression that the other had shifted his front and was about to attack him from another angle. He countered what seemed to him an insinuation stoutly.

===Chinese===

“你上城堡来的那天，”他说，“你上城堡来的那天……”

“上星期四，”乔治好意地提了个头。

“正是。你上星期四来城堡的时候，有一位年轻小姐同行。”

乔治没听出话题已经换了，只当对方是挪动了阵线，预备从另一个角度向他发起攻势。他把这句在他听来语带影射的话顶了回去，寸步不让。

===Original===

“We merely happened to meet at the castle. She came there quite independently of me.”

Lord Marshmoreton looked alarmed. “You didn’t know her?” he said anxiously.

“Certainly I knew her. She is an old friend of mine. But if you are hinting﻿ ﻿…”

“Not at all,” rejoined the earl, profoundly relieved. “Not at all. I ask merely because this young lady, with whom I had some conversation, was good enough to give me her name and address. She, too, happened to mistake me for a gardener.”

“It’s those corduroy trousers,” murmured George in extenuation.

===Chinese===

“我们不过是在城堡上偶然碰头的。她到那儿去，同我毫不相干。”

马什莫顿勋爵神色一慌。“你不认识她？”他急急地问。

“我当然认识她。她是我多年的老朋友。可您这话要是暗指……”

“全无此意，”伯爵连忙接话，如释重负。“全无此意。我问一声，只因这位小姐同我攀谈过几句，蒙她惠然把姓名住址见告。她也恰巧错把我当成了园丁。”

“都怪那条灯芯绒裤子，”乔治咕哝了一句，替他开脱。

===Original===

“I have unfortunately lost them.”

“You can always get another pair.”

“Eh?”

“I say you can always get another pair of corduroy trousers.”

“I have not lost my trousers. I have lost the young lady’s name and address.”

===Chinese===

“不巧，我把它们弄丢了。”

“这个总还能再买一条。”

“嗯？”

“我是说，灯芯绒裤子总还能再买一条的。”

“我丢的不是裤子。我丢的是那位小姐的姓名住址。”

===Original===

“Oh!”

“I promised to send her some roses. She will be expecting them.”

“That’s odd. I was just reading a letter from her when you came in. That must be what she’s referring to when she says, ‘If you see dadda, the old dear, tell him not to forget my roses.’ I read it three times and couldn’t make any sense out of it. Are you Dadda?”

The earl smirked. “She did address me in the course of our conversation as dadda.”

“Then the message is for you.”

===Chinese===

“哦！”

“我答应过给她寄些玫瑰去。她一定正等着呢。”

“巧了。您进来的时候，我正读她的信呢。怪不得她信里有一句——‘你要是见着老爹，那位老宝贝，跟他说别忘了我那些玫瑰。’这句我念了三遍，愣是没念出个所以然来。您就是老爹吗？”

伯爵的嘴角漾出得色。“攀谈中间，她确实管我叫过老爹。”

“那这口信就是捎给您的了。”

===Original===

“A very quaint and charming girl. What is her name? And where can I find her?”

“Her name’s Billie Dore.”

“Billie?”

“Billie.”

“Billie!” said Lord Marshmoreton softly. “I had better write it down. And her address?”

===Chinese===

“一位顶古怪又顶可爱的姑娘。她叫什么名字？我上哪儿能寻着她？”

“她的名字叫比莉·多尔。”

“比莉？”

“比莉。”

“比莉！”马什莫顿勋爵轻声念道。“我最好把它记下来。她的住址呢？”

===Original===

“I don’t know her private address. But you could always reach her at the Regal Theatre.”

“Ah! She is on the stage?”

“Yes. She’s in my piece, *Follow the Girl*.”

“Indeed! Are you a playwright, Mr. Bevan?”

“Good Lord, no!” said George, shocked. “I’m a composer.”

===Chinese===

“她私下住哪儿我不知道。不过您随时可以由雷加尔剧院同她接上头。”

“哦！她是登台演戏的？”

“正是。她演的是我这出《跟着姑娘走》（Follow the Girl）。”

“原来如此！你是写戏的，贝文先生？”

“老天爷，不是！”乔治吓了一跳，“我是作曲的。”

===Original===

“Very interesting. And you met Miss Dore through her being in this play of yours?”

“Oh, no. I knew her before she went on the stage. She was a stenographer in a music-publisher’s office when we first met.”

“Good gracious! Was she really a stenographer?”

“Yes. Why?”

“Oh﻿—ah﻿—nothing, nothing. Something just happened to come to my mind.”

===Chinese===

“很有意思。那么你认识多尔小姐，是因为她参演了你这出戏？”

“哦，不是。她上台之前我就认识她了。我们头一回见面，她还在一家乐谱出版行的写字间里当速记员呢。”

“我的老天！她真当过速记员？”

“是啊。怎么了？”

“哦——呃——没什么，没什么。恰巧想起一点事情罢了。”

===Original===

What happened to come into Lord Marshmoreton’s mind was a fleeting vision of Billie installed in Miss Alice Faraday’s place as his secretary. With such a helper it would be a pleasure to work on that infernal Family History which was now such a bitter toil. But the daydream passed. He knew perfectly well that he had not the courage to dismiss Alice. In the hands of that calm-eyed girl he was as putty. She exercised over him the hypnotic spell a lion-tamer exercises over his little playmates.

“We have been pals for years,” said George. “Billie is one of the best fellows in the world.”

“A charming girl.”

“She would give her last nickel to anyone that asked for it.”

“Delightful!”

===Chinese===

马什莫顿勋爵恰巧想起的，是比莉接替爱丽丝·法拉第小姐、坐上他的秘书席位的匆匆一景。有这样一位帮手，编纂那部如今苦不堪言的遭瘟的《家族史》，倒要成了一桩乐事。可是白日梦转瞬即逝。他心里清楚得很，自己绝没有辞退爱丽丝的胆量。在那位眼神沉静的姑娘手里，他软得就像一团油灰。她对他施的魔力，恰似驯狮人施在他那些小搭档身上的催眠咒。

“我们是多年的老哥们儿了，”乔治说。“比莉是这世上顶好的人儿里的一个。”

“一位招人喜欢的姑娘。”

“谁开口要，她把兜里最后一枚五分镍币也掏给人家。”

“难得呀！”

===Original===

“And as straight as a string. No one ever said a word against Billie.”

“No?”

“She may go out to lunch and supper and all that kind of thing, but there’s nothing to that.”

“Nothing!” agreed the earl warmly. “Girls must eat!”

“They do. You ought to see them.”

===Chinese===

“而且为人正派，直得像根琴弦。比莉身上，从来没人嚼出过半个字的舌头。”

“是吗？”

“她有时出门吃个午饭、赴个晚饭，诸如此类，可那都是没什么的。”

“没什么！”伯爵热切地附和。“姑娘家总得吃饭嘛！”

“是得吃。您真该见识见识她们吃饭。”

===Original===

“A little harmless relaxation after the fatigue of the day!”

“Exactly. Nothing more.”

Lord Marshmoreton felt more drawn than ever to this sensible young man﻿—sensible, at least, on all points but one. It was a pity they could not see eye to eye on what was and what was not suitable in the matter of the love-affairs of the aristocracy.

“So you are a composer, Mr. Bevan?” he said affably.

===Chinese===

“劳累了一天，来点儿无伤大雅的消遣！”

“正是。仅此而已。”

马什莫顿勋爵愈发觉得这位明理的年轻人可亲——明理，至少除了一桩以外桩桩明理。可惜的是，在贵族的恋爱究竟什么相宜、什么不相宜这一桩上，两人怎么也看不到一处去。

“这么说，你是位作曲家，贝文先生？”他亲切地问。

===Original===

“Yes.”

Lord Marshmoreton gave a little sigh. “It’s a long time since I went to see a musical performance. More than twenty years. When I was up at Oxford, and for some years afterwards, I was a great theatregoer. Never used to miss a first night at the Gaiety. Those were the days of Nellie Farren and Kate Vaughan. Florence St. John, too. How excellent she was in *Faust Up to Date*! But we missed Nellie Farren. Meyer Lutz was the Gaiety composer then. But a good deal of water has flowed under the bridge since those days. I don’t suppose you have ever heard of Meyer Lutz?”

“I don’t think I have.”

“Johnnie Toole was playing a piece called *Partners*. Not a good play. And *The Yeoman of the Guard* had just been produced at the Savoy. That makes it seem a long time ago, doesn’t it? Well, I mustn’t take up all your time. Goodbye, Mr. Bevan. I am glad to have had the opportunity of this little talk. The Regal Theatre, I think you said, is where your piece is playing? I shall probably be going to London shortly. I hope to see it.” Lord Marshmoreton rose. “As regards the other matter, there is no hope of inducing you to see the matter in the right light?”

===Chinese===

“是。”

马什莫顿勋爵轻轻叹了口气。“我去看音乐喜剧，可是有年头了。不止二十年了。我在牛津念书那阵子，往后好几年，都是个热心的戏迷。盖蒂剧院（the Gaiety）的首演夜，一场也没落下过。那是内莉·法伦（Nellie Farren）和凯特·沃恩（Kate Vaughan）的年代。还有弗洛伦斯·圣约翰（Florence St. John）。她在《摩登浮士德》（Faust Up to Date）里演得有多出色！可惜后来内莉·法伦就不在了。那时节盖蒂剧院的作曲是迈耶·卢茨（Meyer Lutz）。不过打那些日子起，桥底下也不知流走了多少水。我想你多半没听说过迈耶·卢茨吧？”

“我想我没听说过。”

“约翰尼·图尔（Johnnie Toole）那时节正演着一出叫《搭档》（Partners）的戏。不算好戏。而《皇家卫队》（The Yeoman of the Guard）也刚在萨沃伊剧院（the Savoy）搬演。这么一算，那些日子可就隔得远了，是不是？好了，我不该把你的工夫都占掉。再会，贝文先生。有机会作这一场小小的交谈，我很高兴。雷加尔剧院——我想你是说了——你那出戏就在那儿演？我近日多半要上伦敦去。我希望去看一看。”马什莫顿勋爵站起身。“至于另一件事，要劝你从正确的眼光来看问题，还有指望吗？”

===Original===

“We seem to disagree as to which is the right light.”

“Then there is nothing more to be said. I will be perfectly frank with you, Mr. Bevan. I like you﻿ ﻿…”

“The feeling is quite mutual.”

“But I don’t want you as a son-in-law. And, dammit,” exploded Lord Marshmoreton, “I won’t have you as a son-in-law! Good God! do you think that you can harry and assault my son Percy in the heart of Piccadilly and generally make yourself a damned nuisance and then settle down here without an invitation at my very gates and expect to be welcomed into the bosom of the family? If I were a young man﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“至于哪一边才算正确的眼光，咱们俩看来自有分歧。”

“那就没什么可说的了。我跟你打开天窗说亮话，贝文先生。我喜欢你……”

“这份好感，我这边也分毫不差。”

“可我不想让你做我的女婿。而且，见鬼，”马什莫顿勋爵爆发了，“我也决计不让你做我的女婿！天老爷！你以为你可以在皮卡迪利正当中追逐、殴辱我的儿子珀西，横竖闹得人不得安生，然后不请自来，径直在我家大门口住下，还指望一家人张开双臂把你搂进怀抱？我要是个年轻人……”

===Original===

“I thought we had agreed that you were a young man.”

“Don’t interrupt me!”

“I only said﻿ ﻿…”

“I heard what you said. Flattery!”

“Nothing of the kind. Truth.”

===Chinese===

“我记得咱们才议定，您是个年轻人来着。”

“别打岔！”

“我就说了句……”

“你说的话我听见了。奉承！”

“压根儿不是。是实话。”

===Original===

Lord Marshmoreton melted. He smiled. “Young idiot!”

“We agree there all right.”

Lord Marshmoreton hesitated. Then with a rush he unbosomed himself, and made his own position on the matter clear.

“I know what you’ll be saying to yourself the moment my back is turned. You’ll be calling me a stage heavy father and an old snob and a number of other things. Don’t interrupt me, dammit! You will, I tell you! And you’ll be wrong. I don’t think the Marshmoretons are fenced off from the rest of the world by some sort of divinity. My sister does. Percy does. But Percy’s an ass! If ever you find yourself thinking differently from my son Percy, on any subject, congratulate yourself. You’ll be right.”

“But﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

马什莫顿勋爵心软了。他笑了。“傻小子！”

“这一点咱们倒全无异议。”

马什莫顿勋爵踌躇了一下。跟着便一口气把心窝里的话全掏了出来，摆明了自己在这桩事上的立场。

“我知道我一转身你会对自己怎么说。你要骂我是戏台上那种专横的老太爷，骂我是个老势利鬼，还要骂上别的许许多多名堂。别打岔，见鬼！你就是要骂，我告诉你！可你骂错了。我可不认为我们马什莫顿家是被什么神力圈起来、同世人隔开的。我妹妹是这么想。珀西是这么想。可珀西是个蠢货！哪一天你发觉自己在随便哪个问题上同我儿子珀西想的不一样，你就该恭喜自己。准是你对。”

“可是……”

===Original===

“I know what you’re going to say. Let me finish. If I were the only person concerned, I wouldn’t stand in Maud’s way, whoever she wanted to marry, provided he was a good fellow and likely to make her happy. But I’m not. There’s my sister Caroline. There’s a whole crowd of silly, cackling fools﻿—my sisters﻿—my sons-in-law﻿—all the whole pack of them! If I didn’t oppose Maud in this damned infatuation she’s got for you﻿—if I stood by and let her marry you﻿—what do you think would happen to me?﻿—I’d never have a moment’s peace! The whole gabbling pack of them would be at me, saying I was to blame. There would be arguments, discussions, family councils! I hate arguments! I loathe discussions! Family councils make me sick! I’m a peaceable man, and I like a quiet life! And, damme, I’m going to have it. So there’s the thing for you in letters of one syllable. I don’t object to you personally, but I’m not going to have you bothering me like this. I’ll admit freely that, since I have made your acquaintance, I have altered the unfavourable opinion I had formed of you from﻿—from hearsay﻿ ﻿…”

“Exactly the same with me,” said George. “You ought never to believe what people tell you. Everyone told me your middle name was Nero, and that﻿ ﻿…”

“Don’t interrupt me!”

“I wasn’t. I was just pointing out﻿ ﻿…”

“Be quiet! I say I have changed my opinion of you to a great extent. I mention this unofficially, as a matter that has no bearing on the main issue; for, as regards any idea you may have of inducing me to agree to your marrying my daughter, let me tell you that I am unalterably opposed to any such thing!”

===Chinese===

“我知道你要说什么。让我说完。倘若这桩事只牵涉我一个人，莫德要嫁谁我都不拦，只要那是个好小伙子，又能叫她幸福。可这桩事不光牵涉我一个人。还有我妹妹卡罗琳。还有一大帮叽叽呱呱的蠢货——我的妹妹们——我的女婿们——整整一窝子！倘若我不反对莫德对你这场该死的痴迷——倘若我站在一旁由着她嫁给你——你猜我落个什么下场？——我一辈子也别想有片刻安生！那一大帮呱噪家伙全会缠上我，说我不是。少不了要争，要辩，要开家庭会议！我恨透了争辩！我厌恶透了讨论！家庭会议叫我恶心！我是个爱好安宁的人，我就爱过清静日子！而且，见鬼，这清静日子我是要定了的。事情就这样给你交个底，一个字一个字，说得再浅白不过。你这个人本身我并不讨厌，可我不能再让你这么搅得我不安生。我爽爽快快承认，自打认识你以来，我原先从——从道听途说得来的对你的坏印象，已经改了……”

“我跟您一模一样，”乔治说。“人家嘴里的话您千万信不得。人人都对我说，您名字中间那一个词是尼禄，还说……”

“别打岔！”

“我没打岔。我不过是想指出……”

“不许做声！我说的是，我对你的看法已经大大改观了。这一点我只算私下说说，同本题并不相干；至于你心里要是还盘算着劝我同意你娶我的女儿，那我不妨明告，此种念头我绝无更改的余地，坚决反对到底！”

===Original===

“Don’t say that.”

“What the devil do you mean﻿—don’t say that! I do say that! It is out of the question. Do you understand? Very well, then. Good morning.”

The door closed. Lord Marshmoreton walked away feeling that he had been commendably stern. George filled his pipe and sat smoking thoughtfully. He wondered what Maud was doing at that moment.

===Chinese===

“别说这话。”

“你搞的什么鬼——还叫我不说这话！我偏要说！此事根本无从谈起。明白不明白？好，那就这样。再会。”

门关上了。马什莫顿勋爵走远了，自觉这一趟严责得恰到好处，颇堪嘉许。乔治装满烟斗，坐着抽烟出神。他心里在想，这会儿莫德在做什么呢。

---

===Original===

Maud at that moment was greeting her brother with a bright smile, as he limped downstairs after a belated shave and change of costume.

“Oh, Percy, dear,” she was saying, “I had quite an adventure this morning. An awful tramp followed me for miles! Such a horrible-looking brute. I was so frightened that I had to ask a curate in the next village to drive him away. I did wish I had had you there to protect me. Why don’t you come out with me sometimes when I take a country walk? It really isn’t safe for me to be alone!”

===Chinese===

此刻，莫德正满面笑容迎接她的哥哥；哥哥刮罢胡子、换好衣裳，误了时候，正一瘸一拐地下楼来。

“哦，珀西，亲爱的，”她说，“今天早上我可真遇上了一桩险事。一个吓人的流浪汉跟了我好几英里！那副尊容要多凶有多凶。我吓坏了，只好请邻村的一位副牧师把他轰走。我那时候真巴不得你在跟前保护我。我下乡散步的时候，你怎么不时常陪我一块儿出来走走呢？我一个人走，实在不安全啊！”

## XVII / XVII

===Original===
The gift of hiding private emotion and keeping up appearances before strangers is not, as many suppose, entirely a product of our modern civilization. Centuries before we were born or thought of there was a widely press-agented boy in Sparta who even went so far as to let a fox gnaw his tender young stomach without permitting the discomfort inseparable from such a proceeding to interfere with either his facial expression or his flow of small talk. Historians have handed it down that, even in the later stages of the meal, the polite lad continued to be the life and soul of the party. But, while this feat may be said to have established a record never subsequently lowered, there is no doubt that almost every day in modern times men and women are performing similar and scarcely less impressive miracles of self-restraint. Of all the qualities which belong exclusively to Man and are not shared by the lower animals, this surely is the one which marks him off most sharply from the beasts of the field. Animals care nothing about keeping up appearances. Observe Bertram the Bull when things are not going just as he could wish. He stamps. He snorts. He paws the ground. He throws back his head and bellows. He is upset, and he doesn’t care who knows it. Instances could be readily multiplied. Deposit a charge of shot in some outlying section of Thomas the Tiger, and note the effect. Irritate Wilfred the Wasp, or stand behind Maud the Mule and prod her with a pin. There is not an animal on the list who has even a rudimentary sense of the social amenities; and it is this more than anything else which should make us proud that we are human beings on a loftier plane of development.

===Chinese===
在陌生人面前藏起私情、撑住体面，这份天赋可不像许多人以为的那样，全然是现代文明的产物。早在你我出生、乃至被人想到之前的许多个世纪，斯巴达就有过一位被宣传得沸沸扬扬的男孩，他甚至听凭一只狐狸啃咬自己柔嫩的少年肚腹，也不肯让这类行径必然伴随的不适，妨碍他脸上的神情分毫，或是搅扰他的谈兴。据史家相传，甚至在那顿“饭”吃到后半程，这位有礼有节的少年依旧是全场欢乐的灵魂。不过，这项壮举固然可以说创下了一桩此后无人能破的纪录，可毫无疑问，如今几乎每一天都有男男女女在成就与此相仿、令人叹服之处也差不了多少的自制奇迹。在人这个万物之灵独有、低等动物无从分享的种种品质之中，这无疑是最能把人同田野间的群兽截然区分开来的一样。动物压根儿不在乎撑不撑场面。且看公牛伯特兰（Bertram the Bull），但凡事情稍不如它的意：它跺蹄。它喷鼻。它刨地。它仰天狂吼。它心烦意乱，而且满不在乎叫谁知道。这样的例子俯拾即是。往老虎托马斯（Thomas the Tiger）身上不碍事的偏远部位送进一枪铁砂，再瞧瞧效果。招惹一下黄蜂威尔弗雷德（Wilfred the Wasp），或是站到骡子莫德（Maud the Mule）背后拿别针戳它一下。名单上的动物，没有哪一只对社交客套哪怕有一点雏形的观念；而正是这一点，胜过一切，叫我们自豪自己是人，是站在更高发展阶段上的人。

---

===Original===
In the days which followed Lord Marshmoreton’s visit to George at the cottage, not a few of the occupants of Belpher Castle had their mettle sternly tested in this respect; and it is a pleasure to be able to record that not one of them failed to come through the ordeal with success. The general public, as represented by the uncles, cousins, and aunts who had descended on the place to help Lord Belpher celebrate his coming-of-age, had not a notion that turmoil lurked behind the smooth fronts of at least half a dozen of those whom they met in the course of the daily round.
Lord Belpher, for example, though he limped rather painfully, showed nothing of the baffled fury which was reducing his weight at the rate of ounces a day. His uncle Francis, the Bishop, when he tackled him in the garden on the subject of Intemperance﻿—for Uncle Francis, like thousands of others, had taken it for granted, on reading the report of the encounter with the policeman and Percy’s subsequent arrest, that the affair had been the result of a drunken outburst﻿—had no inkling of the volcanic emotions that seethed in his nephew’s bosom. He came away from the interview, indeed, feeling that the boy had listened attentively and with a becoming regret, and that there was hope for him after all, provided that he fought the impulse. He little knew that, but for the conventions (which frown on the practice of murdering bishops), Percy would gladly have strangled him with his bare hands and jumped upon the remains.

===Chinese===
自马什莫顿勋爵亲临小屋探望乔治之后的那些天里，贝尔弗城堡的住客当中，颇有不少人就在这个方面经受了严格的考验；而令人欣然的是，我们可以记上一笔：他们中间没有一个人在严酷的考验面前败下阵来。至于寻常公众——就是那些涌来帮贝尔弗勋爵庆贺成人礼的叔伯、姑姨和表亲们——对于自己日常碰面的人当中，至少有半打光鲜脸面底下潜藏着骚动一事，竟一无所知。

比如说贝尔弗勋爵，虽然他一瘸一拐、疼得不轻，却丝毫没有露出那股受挫的怒火——这股怒火正以一天一盎司的速度削减着他的体重。他的叔叔、当主教的弗朗西斯（Francis）在花园里揪住他大谈节制饮酒——原来弗朗西斯叔叔同千百读者一样，读到那场同警察的冲突和珀西随后被捕的报道，便想当然认定事情是酒后撒疯闯出来的祸——谈话之间，他对于侄儿胸中翻腾的火山般的激情竟毫无察觉。确实，谈完话走开的时候，他满心觉得这孩子听得专心，悔意也得体，只要他同那股冲动搏斗下去，终究还是有指望的。他万万没有想到，若非世俗礼法从中作梗（这礼法可皱着眉头不许人干谋杀主教的行径），珀西巴不得赤手空拳把他掐死，再朝那遗骸猛踩几脚。

===Original===
Lord Belpher’s case, inasmuch as he took himself extremely seriously and was not one of those who can extract humour even from their own misfortunes, was perhaps the hardest which comes under our notice; but his sister Maud was also experiencing mental disquietude of no mean order. Everything had gone wrong with Maud. Barely a mile separated her from George, that essential link in her chain of communication with Geoffrey Raymond; but so thickly did it bristle with obstacles and dangers that it might have been a mile of No Man’s Land. Twice, since the occasion when the discovery of Lord Marshmoreton at the cottage had caused her to abandon her purpose of going in and explaining everything to George, had she attempted to make the journey; and each time some trifling, maddening accident had brought about failure. Once, just as she was starting, her aunt Augusta had insisted on joining her for what she described as “a nice long walk”; and the second time, when she was within a bare hundred yards of her objective, some sort of a cousin popped out from nowhere and forced his loathsome company on her.
Foiled in this fashion, she had fallen back in desperation on her second line of attack. She had written a note to George, explaining the whole situation in good, clear phrases and begging him as a man of proved chivalry to help her. It had taken up much of one afternoon, this note, for it was not easy to write; and it had resulted in nothing. She had given it to Albert to deliver and Albert had returned empty-handed.

===Chinese===
贝尔弗勋爵的情形，大概要算我们所见诸人中最苦的一桩，因为他把自己看得万分要紧，又不是那种能从自身灾祸里也搜刮出一点幽默来的人；不过他妹妹莫德内心的烦乱，也决计不在小处。莫德是事事不顺。她同乔治相距不足一英里，而乔治正是她那副与杰弗里·雷蒙德联通的链条上顶要紧的一环；可这一英里路上荆棘密布、险象环生，简直相当于一英里宽的无人地带。自从那回在小屋撞见马什莫顿勋爵、不得不打消进屋去向乔治把一切解释明白的打算以来，她已两度试着上路，而每一回，都有点微不足道又叫人发疯的意外从中作祟，害她功败垂成。头一回，她刚要动身，奥古斯塔姑妈（aunt Augusta）执意要陪她走一趟她自己所说的“舒舒服服的长路”；第二回，她离目的地已不足一百码，也不知是哪一房的表哥从天而降，硬把他那叫人腻烦的陪伴塞给了她。

一连这般受挫之后，她万般无奈，只好退而动用第二路进兵。她给乔治写了一张便条，用明白晓畅的好词好句把整个局面交代清楚，恳求他看在自己那条经受过考验的侠义心肠的分上，拉她一把。这张便条花去了她大半个下午，因为实在不好下笔；而结果是一场空。她把条子交给阿尔伯特送去，阿尔伯特却两手空空地回来了。

===Original===
“The gentleman said there was no answer, m’lady!”
“No answer! But there must be an answer!”
“No answer, m’lady. Those was his very words,” stoutly maintained the black-souled boy, who had destroyed the letter within two minutes after it had been handed to him. He had not even bothered to read it. A deep, dangerous, dastardly stripling this, who fought to win and only to win. The ticket marked “R. Byng” was in his pocket, and in his ruthless heart a firm resolve that R. Byng and no other should have the benefit of his assistance.

===Chinese===
“那位先生说没有回信，小姐！”

“没有回信！可是一定得有回信呀！”

“没有回信，小姐。他就是这么说的，一个字儿不差。”那黑心烂肺的小子斩钉截铁地一口咬定——其实那张条子交到他手里还不到两分钟，就叫他毁掉了。他甚至懒得看上一眼。好一个城府深沉、心狠手辣、卑鄙无耻的少年郎，他打仗只为求胜，而且一心只许求胜。那张写着“R. 宾”的彩票就揣在他的口袋里，他那颗冷酷的心里也立定了主意：他这份帮忙的好处，只许落到 R. 宾一个人头上，别人休想。

---

===Original===
Maud could not understand it. That is to say, she resolutely kept herself from accepting the only explanation of the episode that seemed possible. In black and white she had asked George to go to London and see Geoffrey and arrange for the passage﻿—through himself as a sort of clearing-house﻿—of letters between Geoffrey and herself. She had felt from the first that such a request should be made by her in person and not through the medium of writing, but surely it was incredible that a man like George, who had been through so much for her and whose only reason for being in the neighbourhood was to help her, could have coldly refused without even a word. And yet what else was she to think? Now, more than ever, she felt alone in a hostile world.
Yet, to her guests she was bright and entertaining. Not one of them had a suspicion that her life was not one of pure sunshine.

===Chinese===
莫德想不通。也就是说，这件事唯一像样的解释明明摆在那儿，她却咬紧牙关不肯认账。她白纸黑字地请乔治去伦敦见杰弗里，安排杰弗里和她两下里的书信往还——请他本人充当转递的枢纽，好比票据交换所那样的机关。打一开始她就觉得，这样的请求理应当面提出，而不该假手笔墨；可是，像乔治这样的一个人，为她受过那么多的累，如今待在这左近的唯一缘由就是要帮她，这样的人竟然冷冰冰一口回绝，连一句话也不留，这总归叫人难以置信。可不然她又该怎么想呢？此刻她比什么时候都更真切地觉得，自己孤身一人，落在一个与她为敌的世界上。

然而招待宾客时，她照样明艳照人、妙趣横生。他们中间没有一个人起过疑心，想到她的日子并非一片纯粹的阳光。

===Original===
Albert, I am happy to say, was thoroughly miserable. The little brute was suffering torments. He was showering anonymous Advice to the Lovelorn on Reggie Byng﻿—excellent stuff, culled from the pages of weekly papers, of which there was a pile in the housekeeper’s room, the property of a sentimental lady’s maid﻿—and nothing seemed to come of it. Every day, sometimes twice and thrice a day, he would leave on Reggie’s dressing-table significant notes similar in tone to the one which he had placed there on the night of the ball; but, for all the effect they appeared to exercise on their recipient, they might have been blank pages.
The choicest quotations from the works of such established writers as “Aunt Charlotte” of Forget-Me-Not and “Doctor Cupid,” the heart-expert of Home Chat, expended themselves fruitlessly on Reggie. As far as Albert could ascertain﻿—and he was one of those boys who ascertain practically everything within a radius of miles﻿—Reggie positively avoided Maud’s society.

===Chinese===
阿尔伯特，说来可喜，是惨透了。这小坏蛋正受着煎熬。他把一则则不知出处的“失恋者忠告”（Advice to the Lovelorn）连珠炮似的砸向雷吉·宾——都是好货色，从周刊上摘下来的；女管家房里就堆着一摞，本是一位多愁善感的贴身女仆的私藏——可看样子全无着落。他天天往雷吉的梳妆台上留条子，有时候一天两趟三趟，条子意味深长，腔调跟舞会那天夜里留下的那一张如出一辙；可是单看它们在那位收条人身上起的作用，还不如说是几张白纸。

《勿忘我》（Forget-Me-Not）杂志的“夏洛特婶婶”（“Aunt Charlotte”）、《家常话》（Home Chat）的爱情问题专家“丘比特大夫”（“Doctor Cupid”）——这些成名作家笔下的锦言妙语，掇其精华，浇到雷吉身上，尽数付诸东流。据阿尔伯特查考——他是那号孩子，方圆几英里之内的事几乎没有他查考不出的——雷吉分明是有意躲着莫德，不跟她作伴。

===Original===
And this after reading “Doctor Cupid’s” invaluable tip about “Seeking her company on all occasions” and the dictum of “Aunt Charlotte” to the effect that “Many a wooer has won his lady by being persistent”﻿—Albert spelled it “persistuent” but the effect is the same﻿—“and rendering himself indispensable by constant little attentions.” So far from rendering himself indispensable to Maud by constant little attentions, Reggie, to the disgust of his backer and supporter, seemed to spend most of his time with Alice Faraday. On three separate occasions had Albert been revolted by the sight of his protégé in close association with the Faraday girl﻿—once in a boat on the lake and twice in his grey car. It was enough to break a boy’s heart; and it completely spoiled Albert’s appetite﻿—a phenomenon attributed, I am glad to say, in the Servants’ Hall to reaction from recent excesses. The moment when Keggs, the butler, called him a greedy little pig and hoped it would be a lesson to him not to stuff himself at all hours with stolen cakes was a bitter moment for Albert.

===Chinese===
而这一套，全是在读过“丘比特大夫”那条无价锦囊——“不分场合，时时刻刻都要求得同她作伴”——以及“夏洛特婶婶”的金言之后发生的；那金言大意说：“许多求婚人凭着一股契而不舍——”阿尔伯特把它拼成了白字“契而不舍”，不过效果反正相同——“加上小殷勤不断，把自己变成她离不得的人。”可是雷吉呢，且莫说什么小殷勤不断、叫莫德离不得他，竟叫他背后这位后台兼军师又是恼火又是恶心：他大把的工夫，看样子都消磨在爱丽丝·法拉第身边。阿尔伯特已经三度亲眼撞见他那被保护人同法拉第家的姑娘厮守在一处，每一回都叫他直反胃——一回是在湖上一条小船里，两回是在他那辆灰色汽车里。这足够叫一个孩子心碎；也把阿尔伯特的胃口败了个干净——说来可喜，仆役大厅里把这一现象归因于近日放纵口腹之后的失调。管家凯格斯骂他是一只嘴馋的小肥猪，还指望这一回能给他个教训，叫他别再成天价偷了糕点就往肚里塞——那一刻，对阿尔伯特来说，是苦涩的一刻。

===Original===
It is a relief to turn from the contemplation of these tortured souls to the pleasanter picture presented by Lord Marshmoreton. Here, undeniably, we have a man without a secret sorrow, a man at peace with this best of all possible worlds. Since his visit to George a second youth seems to have come upon Lord Marshmoreton. He works in his rose-garden with a new vim, whistling or even singing to himself stray gay snatches of melodies popular in the ’eighties.
Hear him now as he toils. He has a long garden-implement in his hand, and he is sending up the death-rate in slug circles with a devastating rapidity.

===Chinese===
从这些受尽熬煎的灵魂身上移开目光，转而看看马什莫顿勋爵呈现的那幅较为可喜的画面，实在是一桩快事。这一位嘛，无可否认，是个胸中没有隐痛的人，是个与这个一切可能世界里最好的世界相安无事的人。自从那趟小屋之行，马什莫顿勋爵身上像是又来了一回青春。他在玫瑰园里操持活计，劲头焕然一新，嘴里又是打呼哨，又是哼唱，东一句西一句，全是十九世纪八十年代风行的欢快调子。

现在就且听他一面劳作一面唱吧。他手里握着一杆长柄园艺家什，正以摧枯拉朽之势，推高蛞蝓界里的死亡率。

===Original===
> Ta-ra-ra boom-de-ay
 

 Ta-ra-ra **boom**﻿—

===Chinese===
> 塔拉拉，嘣——嘀——埃
> 塔拉拉，**嘣**——

===Original===
And the boom is a death-knell. As it rings softly out on the pleasant spring air, another stout slug has made the Great Change.
It is peculiar, this gaiety. It gives one to think. Others have noticed it, his lordship’s valet amongst them.

===Chinese===
而那声“嘣”，便是丧钟。当它随着宜人的春风悠悠荡开，又一条肥硕的蛞蝓已经与世长辞。

这份欢畅有点儿蹊跷，颇发人深省。已经有人留意到了，勋爵大人的贴身男仆便是其中一位。

===Original===
“I give you my honest word, Mr. Keggs,” says the valet, awed, “this very morning I ’eard the old devil a-singing in ’is barth! Chirruping away like a blooming linnet!”
“Lor!” says Keggs, properly impressed.
“And only last night ’e gave me ’arf a box of cigars and said I was a good, faithful feller! I tell you, there’s somethin’ happened to the old buster﻿—you mark my words!”

===Chinese===
“我跟您说句掏心窝子的实话，凯格斯先生，”贴身男仆说，一脸肃然起敬，“就在今儿一大早，我听见那位老魔王在澡塘子里唱上了！叽叽喳喳，活像一只欢天喜地的红雀！”

“哎呀，老天爷！”凯格斯说，着实给震住了。

“还有昨儿夜里，他赏了我半匣雪茄，还夸我是个又好又忠心的老实人！我跟您说，老家伙身上准是出了一档子事儿——您就记着这几句话吧！”

## XVIII / XVIII

===Original===

Over this complex situation the mind of Keggs, the butler, played like a searchlight. Keggs was a man of discernment and sagacity. He had instinct and reasoning power. Instinct told him that Maud, all unsuspecting the change that had taken place in Albert’s attitude toward her romance, would have continued to use the boy as a link between herself and George: and reason, added to an intimate knowledge of Albert, enabled him to see that the latter must inevitably have betrayed her trust. He was prepared to bet a hundred pounds that Albert had been given letters to deliver and had destroyed them. So much was clear to Keggs. It only remained to settle on some plan of action which would reestablish the broken connection. Keggs did not conceal a tender heart beneath a rugged exterior: he did not mourn over the picture of two loving fellow human beings separated by a misunderstanding; but he did want to win that sweepstake.
His position, of course, was delicate. He could not go to Maud and beg her to confide in him. Maud would not understand his motives, and might leap to the not unjustifiable conclusion that he had been at the sherry. No! Men were easier to handle than women. As soon as his duties would permit﻿—and in the present crowded condition of the house they were arduous﻿—he set out for George’s cottage.
“I trust I do not disturb or interrupt you, sir,” he said, beaming in the doorway like a benevolent high priest. He had doffed his professional manner of austere disapproval, as was his custom in moments of leisure.
“Not at all,” replied George, puzzled. “Was there anything﻿ ﻿… ?”
“There was, sir.”

===Chinese===

在这盘错综复杂的局面上，管家凯格斯的头脑像一盏探照灯来回扫射。凯格斯是个有眼力、有见识的人，直觉与推理的本事兼备。直觉告诉他：莫德丝毫没有察觉阿尔伯特对她的这桩罗曼史已换了态度，必定还会照老样子拿那孩子当自己同乔治之间的联络线；而推理，再加上他对阿尔伯特的知根知底，使他看得分明：那小子必然已经把她托付的事出卖了。他敢押一百英镑打赌：信交到了阿尔伯特手上，叫他去送，而他把它毁了。至此，凯格斯心里已然透亮。剩下的只是拿定一个行动方案，把断了的那条线重新接上。凯格斯并没有把一副软心肠藏在粗粝的外表底下：他并没有为双双相爱的痴男怨女被一场误会生生拆散的图景而哀伤；可他确实想赢下那场斯威普斯泰克。
他的处境自然微妙。他不能走到莫德跟前，求她对自己推心置腹。莫德不会明白他的用意，说不定还会贸然下一个倒也不算冤枉人的结论：这位管家多喝了几盅雪利酒。不成！男人总比女人好对付。待到差事容得他脱身——眼下宅里宾客盈门，他的差事着实繁重——他便动身往乔治的小屋去了。
“但愿我没有搅扰您、妨碍您，先生。”他说着，在门口堆起满脸笑容，活像一位慈眉善目的大祭司。那副严峻不悦的职业面孔他已经卸了下来——每逢闲暇时刻，他向来如此。
“一点也不，”乔治纳闷地答道，“有什么事……？”
“有的，先生。”

===Original===

“Come along in and sit down.”
“I would not take the liberty, if it is all the same to you, sir. I would prefer to remain standing.”
There was a moment of uncomfortable silence. Uncomfortable, that is to say, on the part of George, who was wondering if the butler remembered having engaged him as a waiter only a few nights back. Keggs himself was at his ease. Few things ruffled this man.
“Fine day,” said George.
“Extremely, sir, but for the rain.”
“Oh, is it raining?”

===Chinese===

“进来坐吧。”
“若是您不介意，先生，在下不敢放肆。我宁可站着。”
一时之间沉默得有些不自在。所谓不自在，是单指乔治这一头——他正心里嘀咕，不知道这位管家还记不记得，短短几夜之前，雇他充当侍者的正是此人。凯格斯自己倒是安之若素。这世上能搅得此人心绪不宁的事屈指可数。
“天气不坏。”乔治说。
“好得很，先生，只是下雨。”
“哦，下雨了吗？”

===Original===

“Sharp downpour, sir.”
“Good for the crops,” said George.
“So one would be disposed to imagine, sir.”
Silence fell again. The rain dripped from the eaves.
“If I might speak freely, sir﻿ ﻿… ?” said Keggs.
“Sure. Shoot!”

===Chinese===

“雨下得又急又猛，先生。”
“对庄稼有好处。”乔治说。
“想来该是如此，先生。”
沉默重新落下。雨水从屋檐上滴滴答答。
“倘若我可以放胆直言，先生……？”凯格斯说。
“行啊。开火吧！”

===Original===

“I beg your pardon, sir?”
“I mean, yes. Go ahead!”
The butler cleared his throat.
“Might I begin by remarking that your little affair of the ’eart, if I may use the expression, is no secret in the Servants’ ’all? I ’ave no wish to seem to be taking a liberty or presuming, but I should like to intimate that the Servants’ ’all is aware of the facts.”
“You don’t have to tell me that,” said George coldly. “I know all about the sweepstake.”

===Chinese===

“您说什么，先生？”
“我是说，行。您讲吧！”
管家清了清嗓子。
“容我先说一句：您那桩小心事儿——恕我斗胆用这个字眼——在仆役大厅里早已不是什么秘密。在下绝无意显得放肆或者僭越，只是想知会您一声：仆役大厅对其中底细一清二楚。”
“这可劳不着您告诉我，”乔治冷冷地说，“那场斯威普斯泰克的底细我全知道。”

===Original===

A flicker of embarrassment passed over the butler’s large, smooth face﻿—passed, and was gone.
“I did not know that you ’ad been apprised of that little matter, sir. But you will doubtless understand and appreciate our point of view. A little sporting flutter﻿—nothing more﻿—designed to halleviate the monotony of life in the country.”
“Oh, don’t apologize,” said George, and was reminded of a point which had exercised him a little from time to time since his vigil on the balcony. “By the way, if it isn’t giving away secrets, who drew Plummer?”
“Sir?”
“Which of you drew a man named Plummer in the sweep?”

===Chinese===

管家那张光滑的大脸上掠过一丝窘迫——掠过去，便没了踪影。
“我原不知道那件小事已经传进您的耳朵，先生。不过您想必能够体谅我们的立场。不过是小小赌个彩头取个乐——仅此而已——为的是给乡下生活的单调日子添点调剂。”
“哦，不必道歉，”乔治说。这句话倒提醒了他一桩心事——自打那晚在阳台上守夜以来，这事便不时在他心里盘桓。“对了，倘若这不算泄露秘密——抽到普卢默的是哪一位？”
“先生？”
“你们哪一位在斯威普斯泰克里抽中了一个名叫普卢默的人？”

===Original===

“I rather fancy, sir,” Keggs’ brow wrinkled in thought, “I rather fancy it was one of the visiting gentlemen’s gentlemen. I gave the point but slight attention at the time. I did not fancy Mr. Plummer’s chances. It seemed to me that Mr. Plummer was a negligible quantity.”
“Your knowledge of form was sound. Plummer’s out!”
“Indeed, sir! An amiable young gentleman, but lacking in many of the essential qualities. Perhaps he struck you that way, sir?”
“I never met him. Nearly, but not quite!”
“It entered my mind that you might possibly have encountered Mr. Plummer on the night of the ball, sir.”

===Chinese===

“我依稀记得，先生，”凯格斯思忖着，额头皱了起来，“我依稀记得，是哪位来做客的贵宾的贴身跟班抽中的。当时我对这一注没怎么留心。我本就不看好普卢默先生的赢面。在我看来，普卢默先生是个可以忽略不计的量。”
“您的行情看得很准。普卢默出局了！”
“可不是嘛，先生！一位和蔼可亲的年轻绅士，只是许多必不可少的素质都有所欠缺。想必他在您眼里也是这么个人，先生？”
“我从没碰见过他。差一点儿，可惜没碰上！”
“我心里琢磨，舞会那天晚上，您或许同普卢默先生打过照面，先生。”

===Original===

“Ah, I was wondering if you remembered me!”
