“I remember you perfectly, sir, and it was the fact that we had already met in what one might almost term a social way that emboldened me to come ’ere today and offer you my services as a hintermediary, should you feel disposed to avail yourself of them.”
George was puzzled.
“Your services?”
“Precisely, sir. I fancy I am in a position to lend you what might be termed an ’elping ’and.”

===Chinese===

“啊，我正纳闷您还记不记得我呢！”
“我记得一清二楚，先生。我们毕竟已经有过一面之缘，几乎称得上是社交意义上的相识；正因如此，我才壮起胆子，今天上这儿来自荐效劳：倘若您有意垂青，在下愿充当一名居间斡旋之人（hintermediary）。”
乔治听得一头雾水。
“你的效劳？”
“正是，先生。我想我有条件助您一臂之力——借个说法，给您搭一把手。”

===Original===

“But that’s remarkably altruistic of you, isn’t it?”
“Sir?”
“I say that is very generous of you. Aren’t you forgetting that you drew Mr. Byng?”
The butler smiled indulgently.
“You are not quite abreast of the progress of events, sir. Since the original drawing of names, there ’as been a trifling hadjustment. The boy Albert now ’as Mr. Byng and I ’ave you, sir. A little amicable arrangement informally conducted in the scullery on the night of the ball.”

===Chinese===

“您这份利他精神可真了不起，不是吗？”
“先生？”
“我是说您真是慷慨大方。您莫不是忘了，抽中宾先生的正是您自己？”
管家宽厚地微微一笑。
“您对事态的进展还没有跟得很紧，先生。自头一回抽签之后，出了一点小小的调整。如今那孩子阿尔伯特手里是宾先生，我手里是您，先生。舞会当晚在洗碗间里非正式地达成了一项小小的友好安排。”

===Original===

“Amicable?”
“On my part, entirely so.”
George began to understand certain things that had been perplexing to him.
“Then all this while﻿ ﻿… ?”
“Precisely, sir. All this while ’er ladyship, under the impression that the boy Albert was devoted to ’er cause, has no doubt been placing a misguided confidence in ’im﻿ ﻿… The little blighter!” said Keggs, abandoning for a moment his company manners and permitting vehemence to take the place of polish. “I beg your pardon for the expression, sir,” he added gracefully. “It escaped me inadvertently.”

===Chinese===

“友好安排？”
“就我这一面而言，完全是友好的。”
乔治开始明白了几件一直叫他纳闷的事。
“那么这一向……？”
“正是，先生。这一向，小姐大人只道那孩子阿尔伯特一心一意为她效力，无疑一直把信任错付给了他……这个小倒霉蛋！”凯格斯说，一刹那间把应酬客套尽数抛开，任凭激愤顶替了温文。“这个字眼失礼了，恕罪，先生，”他从容补上一句，“是一时没留神溜出来的。”

===Original===

“You think that Lady Maud gave Albert a letter to give to me, and that he destroyed it?”
“Such, I should imagine, must undoubtedly have been the case. The boy ’as no scruples, no scruples whatsoever.”
“Good Lord!”
“I appreciate your consternation, sir.”
“That must be exactly what has happened.”

===Chinese===

“您认为莫德小姐交过一封信，托阿尔伯特转交给我，而他把它毁了？”
“依我想来，实情必然如此，断无可疑。那孩子毫无顾忌，半点顾忌也没有。”
“我的老天爷！”
“您这般惊骇，在下完全领会，先生。”
“事情准是这样，分毫不差。”

===Original===

“To my way of thinking there is no doubt of it. It was for that reason that I ventured to come ’ere. In the ’ope that I might be hinstrumental in arranging a meeting.”
The strong distaste which George had had for plotting with this overfed menial began to wane. It might be undignified, he told himself, but it was undeniably practical. And, after all, a man who has plotted with pageboys has little dignity to lose by plotting with butlers. He brightened up. If it meant seeing Maud again he was prepared to waive the decencies.
“What do you suggest?” he said.
“It being a rainy evening and everyone indoors playing games and whatnot,”﻿—Keggs was amiably tolerant of the recreations of the aristocracy﻿—“you would experience little chance of a hinterruption, were you to proceed to the lane outside the heast entrance of the castle grounds and wait there. You will find in the field at the roadside a small disused barn only a short way from the gates, where you would be sheltered from the rain. In the meantime, I would hinform ’er ladyship of your movements, and no doubt it would be possible for ’er to slip off.”
“It sounds all right.”

===Chinese===

“依我之见，此事绝无疑义。正因如此，我才斗胆到这儿来。盼着我能从中襄助（hinstrumental），安排二位见上一面。”
乔治起初对同这个吃得脑满肠肥的仆役合谋深恶痛绝，这股强烈的反感开始消退。他对自己说，这或许有失体面，可无疑切合实际。何况，一个同小堂馆合谋过的人，再同管家合谋，还能折损多少体面呢？他精神一振。只要能再见到莫德，体面不体面的，他都甘愿撇在一边。
“依您的高见，该怎么办？”他说。
“今晚落雨，人人都待在屋里打牌消遣，诸如此类，”——凯格斯对贵族的娱乐一向和气宽容——“您倘若动身到城堡庄园东门外面那条小巷里去等着，叫人搅扰的机会微乎其微。离大门不远，路边的地里有一座废弃不用的小谷仓，可以避雨。这期间，我会把您的行止知会给小姐大人，她要悄悄脱身，想必不难。”
“听起来倒不坏。”

===Original===

“It is all right, sir. The chances of a hinterruption may be said to be reduced to a minimum. Shall we say in one hour’s time?”
“Very well.”
“Then I will wish you good evening, sir. Thank you, sir. I am glad to ’ave been of assistance.”
He withdrew as he had come, with a large impressiveness. The room seemed very empty without him. George, with trembling fingers, began to put on a pair of thick boots.

===Chinese===

“是不坏，先生。叫人搅扰的机会可以说已经减到了最小。就这么说定：一个钟头之后？”
“很好。”
“那么就容我道一声晚安，先生。谢谢您，先生。能帮上一点忙，在下深感欣慰。”
他告退了，来时何等气派，去时也何等气派。他一走，屋里显得空荡荡的。乔治手指发颤，开始穿一双厚靴子。

===Original===

For some minutes after he had set foot outside the door of the cottage, George was inclined to revile the weather for having played him false. On this evening of all evenings, he felt, the elements should, so to speak, have rallied round and done their bit. The air should have been soft and clear and scented: there should have been an afterglow of sunset in the sky to light him on his way. Instead, the air was full of that peculiar smell of hopeless dampness which comes at the end of a wet English day. The sky was leaden. The rain hissed down in a steady flow, whispering of mud and desolation, making a dreary morass of the lane through which he tramped. A curious sense of foreboding came upon George. It was as if some voice of the night had murmured maliciously in his ear a hint of troubles to come. He felt oddly nervous, as he entered the barn.
The barn was both dark and dismal. In one of the dark corners an intermittent dripping betrayed the presence of a gap in its ancient roof. A rat scurried across the floor. The dripping stopped and began again. George struck a match and looked at his watch. He was early. Another ten minutes must elapse before he could hope for her arrival. He sat down on a broken wagon which lay on its side against one of the walls.

===Chinese===

迈出小屋门之后的头几分钟，乔治满心要把这鬼天气痛骂一顿，骂它存心捉弄了他。他觉得，单单在今晚这个晚上，天地风雨诸元素，可以说是理应齐心协力、各尽一份力的。空气本该柔软、清朗、带着香气：天上本该有一抹落日余晖，照亮他的去路。可眼下，空气里满是那种英格兰湿天到头来特有的、叫人绝望的潮气。天色铅灰。雨不紧不慢、嘶嘶地落个不停，低语着泥泞与荒凉，把他跋涉的那条小巷泡成了一片凄凉的烂泥塘。一股说不出的不祥之感攫住了乔治。仿佛夜里有谁在他耳边恶毒地低语，暗示着将临头的麻烦。走进谷仓时，他只觉心神不宁得厉害，说不出的古怪。
谷仓又黑又凄凉。在一个黑暗的角落里，断断续续的滴水声泄露了那古老屋顶上的一处破洞。一只老鼠窜过地板。滴水声停了，又响起来。乔治划亮一根火柴，看了看表。他来得早了。还得再等上十分钟，才谈得上指望她到来。他在一辆侧翻在墙根的破大车上坐了下来。

===Original===

Depression returned. It was impossible to fight against it in this beast of a barn. The place was like a sepulchre. No one but a fool of a butler would have suggested it as a trysting-place. He wondered irritably why places like this were allowed to get into this condition. If people wanted a barn earnestly enough to take the trouble of building one, why was it not worth while to keep the thing in proper repair? Waste and futility! That was what it was. That was what everything was, if you came down to it. Sitting here, for instance, was a futile waste of time. She wouldn’t come. There were a dozen reasons why she should not come. So what was the use of his courting rheumatism by waiting in this morgue of dead agricultural ambitions? None whatever﻿—George went on waiting.
And what an awful place to expect her to come to, if by some miracle she did come﻿—where she would be stifled by the smell of mouldy hay, damped by raindrops and﻿—reflected George gloomily as there was another scurry and scutter along the unseen floor﻿—gnawed by rats. You could not expect a delicately-nurtured girl, accustomed to all the comforts of a home, to be bright and sunny with a platoon of rats crawling all over her.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

颓丧卷土重来。在这座讨厌透顶的谷仓里，人没法同它较劲。这地方活像一座陵墓。除了一个傻瓜透顶的管家，谁也不会提议拿这里当幽会的场所。他心烦意乱地纳闷，为什么竟容许这样的地方糟蹋成这副模样。倘若人们当初真心想要一座谷仓，肯下力气把它盖起来，怎么就不值得把它好好修缮保全？浪费！徒劳！就是这么回事。说到底，样样事情都是这么回事。比方说，坐在这儿干等，就是白白浪费时间。她是不会来的。她不来的理由能数出一打。那么，在这座埋葬了农业雄心的陈尸所里干等，讨一身风湿病，又有什么用处？毫无用处——乔治照等不误。
再说，就算她当真奇迹般地来了，叫她上这么个可怕的地方来——霉干草的气味闷得人透不过气，雨点子浇得人蔫头耷脑，还有——看不见的地板上又是一阵窸窸窣窣的急窜乱跑，乔治阴沉沉地想着——叫老鼠咬。你总不能指望一位娇生惯养的姑娘，享惯了家中种种舒适的姑娘，在一整队老鼠满身乱爬的时候还阳光满面吧……

===Original===

The grey oblong that was the doorway suddenly darkened.
“Mr. Bevan!”
George sprang up. At the sound of her voice every nerve in his body danced in mad exhilaration. He was another man. Depression fell from him like a garment. He perceived that he had misjudged all sorts of things. The evening, for instance, was a splendid evening﻿—not one of those awful dry, baking evenings which make you feel you can’t breathe, but pleasantly moist and full of a delightfully musical patter of rain. And the barn! He had been all wrong about the barn. It was a great little place, comfortable, airy, and cheerful. What could be more invigorating than that smell of hay? Even the rats, he felt, must be pretty decent rats, when you came to know them.
“I’m here!”

===Chinese===

门洞那一方灰色的长方形忽然暗了下去。
“贝文先生！”
乔治一跃而起。一听见她的声音，他浑身的每一根神经都在癫狂的狂喜里手舞足蹈。他换了一个人。颓丧像一件外衣似的从他身上滑落。他发觉自己把各样事情都看走了眼。譬如说，今晚原是个美妙的夜晚——不是那种干燥闷热、叫人透不过气的糟糕夜晚，而是湿润宜人，雨点子淅淅沥沥，奏着一支悦耳的小曲。还有那谷仓！他先前把谷仓全看错了。这是个顶好的小地方，舒适，透气，叫人心里敞亮。还有什么比干草的气味更提神的？就连那些老鼠，他觉得，等你同它们处熟了，想必也是相当规矩的老鼠。
“我来了！”

===Original===

Maud advanced quickly. His eyes had grown accustomed to the murk, and he could see her dimly. The smell of her damp raincoat came to him like a breath of ozone. He could even see her eyes shining in the darkness, so close was she to him.
“I hope you’ve not been waiting long?”
George’s heart was thundering against his ribs. He could scarcely speak. He contrived to emit a No.
“I didn’t think at first I could get away. I had to﻿ ﻿…” She broke off with a cry. The rat, fond of exercise like all rats, had made another of its excitable sprints across the floor.
A hand clutched nervously at George’s arm, found it and held it. And at the touch the last small fragment of George’s self-control fled from him. The world became vague and unreal. There remained of it but one solid fact﻿—the fact that Maud was in his arms and that he was saying a number of things very rapidly in a voice that seemed to belong to somebody he had never met before.

===Chinese===

莫德快步走上前来。他的眼睛已经习惯了昏暗，能隐隐约约望见她。她那件淋湿的雨衣的气味朝他扑来，像一口清冽沁人的新鲜空气。他甚至能看见她的眼睛在黑暗里亮晶晶的——她同他挨得这样近。
“但愿你没有等得太久？”
乔治的心咚咚地撞着肋骨。他几乎说不出话来。他好歹挤出了一个“不”字。
“起初我还当自己脱不开身呢。我得……”她惊叫一声，话头断了。那只老鼠跟所有的老鼠一样热爱体育锻炼，又在地板上来了一次兴冲冲的冲刺。
一只手慌乱地抓向乔治的胳膊，抓到了，便紧紧攥住。这一触之下，乔治残存的最后一丝半点的自制力逃了个精光。世界变得影影绰绰，不再真实。万物之中只剩下一个结结实实的事实——莫德在他的怀里；而他正用一种飞快的语速说着一连串的话，那嗓音陌生得很，仿佛属于一个他从来不曾见过的人。

## XIX / XIX

===Original===
With a shock of dismay so abrupt and overwhelming that it was like a physical injury, George became aware that something was wrong. Even as he gripped her, Maud had stiffened with a sharp cry; and now she was struggling, trying to wrench herself free. She broke away from him. He could hear her breathing hard.

“You﻿—you﻿—” She gulped.

“Maud!”

“How dare you!”

===Chinese===
一阵错愕的震惊猛然袭来，势不可挡，简直像皮肉上添了一道伤——直到这时，乔治才觉察到出了岔子。就在他搂住她的当儿，莫德已经一声尖叫，浑身僵直；此刻她在挣扎，想把身子挣脱出去。她挣脱了他。他听得见她急促的喘息。

“你——你——”她的话哽在喉咙口。

“莫德！”

“你竟敢这样！”

===Original===
There was a pause that seemed to George to stretch on and on endlessly. The rain pattered on the leaky roof. Somewhere in the distance a dog howled dismally. The darkness pressed down like a blanket, stifling thought.

“Good night, Mr. Bevan.” Her voice was ice. “I didn’t think you were﻿—that kind of man.”

She was moving toward the door; and, as she reached it, George’s stupor left him. He came back to life with a jerk, shaking from head to foot. All his varied emotions had become one emotion﻿—a cold fury.

“Stop!”

Maud stopped. Her chin was tilted, and she was wasting a baleful glare on the darkness.

===Chinese===
一阵静默，在乔治的感觉里无休无止地拖长下去。雨点噼噼啪啪打在漏雨的屋顶上。远处不知什么地方，一条狗凄凄凉凉地嗥着。黑暗像一床毯子直压下来，把思想都闷住了。

“晚安，贝文先生。”她的声音冷得结冰。“我没想到你是——那种男人。”

她朝门口走去；就在她将到门边的时候，乔治的麻木劲儿忽然退了。他猛地一激灵，活了过来，从头到脚抖个不停。他的万般情绪已并作一种情绪——一团冰冷的狂怒。

“站住！”

莫德站住了。她下巴翘得高高的，把一道恶狠狠的目光白白地泼洒在黑暗里。

===Original===
“Well, what is it?”

Her tone increased George’s wrath. The injustice of it made him dizzy. At that moment he hated her. He was the injured party. It was he, not she, that had been deceived and made a fool of.

“I want to say something before you go.”

“I think we had better say no more about it!”

===Chinese===
“好，有什么话？”

她这腔调火上浇油，乔治的怒气烧得更旺。这股冤枉气冲得他头晕。这一刻，他恨她。受害的是他。受骗上当、让人当猴耍了的，是他，不是她。

“您走以前，我有话要说。”

“依我看，这件事咱们最好一句也别再提！”

---

===Original===
By the exercise of supreme self-control George kept himself from speaking until he could choose milder words than those that rushed to his lips.

“I think we will!” he said between his teeth.

Maud’s anger became tinged with surprise. Now that the first shock of the wretched episode was over, the calmer half of her mind was endeavouring to soothe the infuriated half by urging that George’s behaviour had been but a momentary lapse, and that a man may lose his head for one wild instant, and yet remain fundamentally a gentleman and a friend. She had begun to remind herself that this man had helped her once in trouble, and only a day or two before had actually risked his life to save her from embarrassment. When she heard him call to her to stop, she supposed that his better feelings had reasserted themselves; and she had prepared herself to receive with dignity a broken, stammered apology. But the voice that had just spoken with a crisp, biting intensity was not the voice of remorse. It was a very angry man, not a penitent one, who was commanding﻿—not begging﻿—her to stop and listen to him.

===Chinese===
乔治施展了高度的自我克制，硬是憋住没开口，直到能挑出比冲到嘴边的那些话温和些的词句。

“我看非提不可！”他从牙缝里挤出一声。

莫德的怒气渐渐掺进了诧异。那桩倒霉事的第一阵震惊既已过去，她头脑里较为冷静的那一半，正设法安抚怒火中烧的那一半，劝它说：乔治的失态不过是片刻的过失；一个人可以在狂乱的一瞬间昏了头，骨子里却依然是位绅士，依然是位朋友。她已经开始提醒自己：这个人曾在她为难的时候帮过她的忙，而且就在一两天以前，还实实在在拿性命去冒了险，救她脱离了窘境。方才听见他喊她站住，她还当他的良知重新抬了头；她已经端好了架子，预备受用一番吞吞吐吐、结结巴巴的道歉。可是方才那一句，吐字干脆、咬人刺骨、火气咄咄逼人，绝不是悔悟的声音。喝令她站住听他说话的，是一个怒气冲冲的男人，不是一个悔过的人。

===Original===
“Well?” she said again, more coldly this time. She was quite unable to understand this attitude of his. She was the injured party. It was she, not he who had trusted and been betrayed.

“I should like to explain.”

“Please do not apologize.”

George ground his teeth in the gloom.

“I haven’t the slightest intention of apologizing. I said I would like to explain. When I have finished explaining, you can go.”

===Chinese===
“怎么？”她又问了一声，这一回更冷了。他这种态度，她全然无法理解。受害的是她。信了人又吃了亏的，是她，不是他。

“我想解释一下。”

“请不要道歉。”

乔治在黑暗里咬牙切齿。

“我压根儿没打算道歉。我说的是我想解释。等我解释完了，您就可以走了。”

===Original===
“I shall go when I please,” flared Maud.

This man was intolerable.

“There is nothing to be afraid of. There will be no repetition of the﻿—incident.”

Maud was outraged by this monstrous misinterpretation of her words.

“I am not afraid!”

“Then, perhaps, you will be kind enough to listen. I won’t detain you long. My explanation is quite simple. I have been made a fool of. I seem to be in the position of the tinker in the play whom everybody conspired to delude into the belief that he was a king. First a friend of yours, Mr. Byng, came to me and told me that you had confided to him that you loved me.”

===Chinese===
“我高兴几时走就几时走。”莫德火冒三丈地说。

这个人叫人忍无可忍。

“没有什么可怕的。那桩——插曲，绝不会再重演。”

她的话竟遭这样骇人听闻的曲解，莫德给气坏了。

“我没有怕！”

“那么，也许您肯赏光听一听。不耽搁您多久。我的解释很简单。我让人当猴耍了。我如今的处境，就像戏文里那个补锅匠：人人串通一气，哄得他相信自己当真是个国王。起头是您的一位朋友，宾先生，跑来对我说，您向他吐露过，说您爱我。”

===Original===
Maud gasped. Either this man was mad, or Reggie Byng was. She choose the politer solution.

“Reggie Byng must have lost his senses.”

“So I supposed. At least, I imagined that he must be mistaken. But a man in love is an optimistic fool, of course, and I had loved you ever since you got into my cab that morning﻿ ﻿…”

“What!”

“So after a while,” proceeded George, ignoring the interruption, “I almost persuaded myself that miracles could still happen, and that what Byng said was true. And when your father called on me and told me the very same thing I was convinced. It seemed incredible, but I had to believe it. Now it seems that, for some inscrutable reason, both Byng and your father were making a fool of me. That’s all. Good night.”

===Chinese===
莫德倒抽了一口气。不是这个人疯了，就是雷吉·宾疯了。她挑了顶客气的那一种解释。

“雷吉·宾一定是疯了。”

“我也是这么想的。至少，我估摸他准是弄错了。不过，堕入情网的人照例是个乐观的傻子——打那天早上您跳上我的马车起，我就爱上您了……”

“什么！”

“于是过了一阵子，”乔治不理会她的打岔，接着说，“我便差不多说服了自己：奇迹终究是有的，宾说的话是真的。等到您父亲也登门来访，对我讲了同样的话，我便信了。这事听着难以置信，可我不能不信。如今看来呢，出于某种不可思议的缘故，宾和您父亲原来是串通一气耍弄我。就这样。晚安。”

===Original===
Maud’s reply was the last which George or any man would have expected. There was a moment’s silence, and then she burst into a peal of laughter. It was the laughter of overstrained nerves, but to George’s ears it had the ring of genuine amusement.

“I’m glad you find my story entertaining,” he said dryly. He was convinced now that he loathed this girl, and that all he desired was to see her go out of his life forever. “Later, no doubt, the funny side of it will hit me. Just at present my sense of humour is rather dormant.”

Maud gave a little cry.

“I’m sorry! I’m so sorry, Mr. Bevan. It wasn’t that. It wasn’t that at all. Oh, I am so sorry. I don’t know why I laughed. It certainly wasn’t because I thought it funny. It’s tragic. There’s been a dreadful mistake!”

===Chinese===
莫德的回答，是乔治也好、随便哪个男人也好，万万想不到的。静默了片刻，随后她突然爆发出一串响亮的笑声。这是神经绷得过紧才有的笑，可落到乔治的耳朵里，声声都透着货真价实的开心。

“很高兴您觉得我的故事有趣。”他干巴巴地说。他如今深信不疑：他厌恶这个姑娘；他唯一的愿望，就是看她永远走出他的生活。“往后呢，不用说，这里头好笑的一面自然会找上我。眼下么，我的幽默感睡得正沉。”

莫德轻轻惊叫了一声。

“对不起！真对不起，贝文先生。不是那么回事，完全不是那么回事。哦，我太对不起了。我不知道自己干吗要笑。那绝不是因为我觉得好笑。这事惨透了。这里头出了一场天大的误会！”

===Original===
“I noticed that,” said George bitterly. The darkness began to afflict his nerves. “I wish to God we had some light.”

The glare of a pocket-torch smote upon him.

“I brought it to see my way back with,” said Maud in a curious, small voice. “It’s very dark across the fields. I didn’t light it before, because I was afraid somebody might see.”

She came towards him, holding the torch over her head. The beam showed her face, troubled and sympathetic, and at the sight all George’s resentment left him. There were mysteries here beyond his unravelling, but of one thing he was certain: this girl was not to blame. She was a thoroughbred, as straight as a wand. She was pure gold.

===Chinese===
“这一点我注意到了。”乔治辛酸地说。黑暗开始折磨他的神经。“要是上帝肯赏咱们一点亮光就好了。”

一道袖珍手电的强光劈面照来。

“我带着它，是照着回路走的。”莫德说，声音细细的，有点异样。“穿过田地那边黑得很。方才我没敢点它，怕叫人瞧见。”

她朝他走来，手电举在头顶上。光柱照亮了她的脸：一脸的困扰，满怀的同情；一见这光景，乔治的满腔怨气烟消云散。这里头的重重谜团，他解不开；可有一件事他拿得准：这姑娘没有过错。她是个地道的纯血种，笔直得像一杆细杖。她是十足的赤金。

---

===Original===
“I came here to tell you everything,” she said. She placed the torch on the wagon-wheel so that its ray fell in a pool of light on the ground between them. “I’ll do it now. Only﻿—only it isn’t so easy now. Mr. Bevan, there’s a man﻿—there’s a man that father and Reggie Byng mistook﻿—they thought﻿ ﻿… You see, they knew it was you that I was with that day in the cab, and so they naturally thought, when you came down here, that you were the man I had gone to meet that day﻿—the man I﻿—I﻿—”

“The man you love?”

“Yes,” said Maud in a small voice; and there was silence again.

George could feel nothing but sympathy. It mastered every other emotion in him, even the grey despair that had come with her words. He could feel all that she was feeling.

“Tell me all about it,” he said.

===Chinese===
“我到这儿来，就是要什么都告诉您。”她说。她把手电搁在一只马车轮上，让那一束光在两人中间的地上淌成一汪光亮。“现在就讲。只是——只是如今不容易开口了。贝文先生，有一个男人——有一个男人，父亲和雷吉·宾错认了——他们以为……您瞧，他们知道那天在马车里和我在一起的是您，所以等您下到这地方来，他们自然认定，您就是我那天要去见的那个人——那个我——我——”

“您爱的那个人？”

“是的。”莫德细声细气地说；接着又是一片静默。

乔治心里只剩了同情。这同情压倒了他其余的一切情绪，连她那番话带来的灰色绝望也一并压服。她心里的一切感受，他都能感同身受。

“把一切都讲给我听吧。”他说。

===Original===
“I met him in Wales last year.” Maud’s voice was a whisper. “The family found out, and I was hurried back here, and have been here ever since. That day when I met you I had managed to slip away from home. I had found out that he was in London, and I was going to meet him. Then I saw Percy, and got into your cab. It’s all been a horrible mistake. I’m sorry.”

“I see,” said George thoughtfully. “I see.”

His heart ached like a living wound. She had told so little, and he could guess so much. This unknown man who had triumphed seemed to sneer scornfully at him from the shadows.

“I’m sorry,” said Maud again.

===Chinese===
“我是去年在威尔士遇见他的。”莫德的声音低成了耳语。“家里发觉了，急急忙忙把我弄回这儿，打那以后我就一直关在这儿。那天遇见您，我好容易从家里溜了出来。我打听到了他在伦敦，正要去见他。后来我瞧见了珀西，就跳上了您的马车。这一场从头到尾都是可怕的误会。对不起。”

“原来如此。”乔治若有所思地说。“原来如此。”

他的心口疼得像一处活生生的伤口。她说得那么少，他能猜出的却那么多。那个得胜的无名男人，仿佛正躲在暗影里朝他讥诮地冷笑。

“对不起。”莫德又说了一遍。

===Original===
“You mustn’t feel like that. How can I help you? That’s the point. What is it you want me to do?”

“But I can’t ask you now.”

“Of course you can. Why not?”

“Why﻿—oh, I couldn’t!”

===Chinese===
“您千万别这么想。我怎样才能帮您？这才是要紧的。您要我做什么？”

“可如今我不能开口求您了。”

“当然能。怎么不能？”

“这——哦，我做不到！”

===Original===
George managed to laugh. It was a laugh that did not sound convincing even to himself, but it served.

“That’s morbid,” he said. “Be sensible. You need help, and I may be able to give it. Surely a man isn’t barred forever from doing you a service just because he happens to love you? Suppose you were drowning and Mr. Plummer was the only swimmer within call, wouldn’t you let him rescue you?”

“Mr. Plummer? What do you mean?”

“You’ve not forgotten that I was a reluctant ear-witness to his recent proposal of marriage?”

Maud uttered an exclamation.

===Chinese===
乔治勉强自己笑了一声。这笑声连他自己听着也缺乏说服力，可它顶了用。

“这么想就病态了。”他说。“讲点道理。您需要帮助，而我也许帮得上。一个人总不见得就因为碰巧爱上了您，就永远没有资格为您效劳了吧？假使您落了水，附近会水的只有普卢默先生一个，您难道不肯让他救？”

“普卢默先生？您这是什么意思？”

“您总没忘记吧，前些天他求婚，我是出于无奈拿耳朵旁听了一场？”

莫德惊呼了一声。

===Original===
“I never asked! How terrible of me. Were you much hurt?”

“Hurt?” George could not follow her.

“That night. When you were on the balcony, and﻿—”

“Oh!” George understood. “Oh, no, hardly at all. A few scratches. I scraped my hands a little.”

“It was a wonderful thing to do,” said Maud, her admiration glowing for a man who could treat such a leap so lightly. She had always had a private theory that Lord Leonard, after performing the same feat, had bragged about it for the rest of his life.

===Chinese===
“我一直没问过您！我真不像话。您伤得厉害吗？”

“伤？”乔治跟不上她的思路。

“那天夜里呀。您在阳台上的时候，还有——”

“哦！”乔治明白了。“哦，不，几乎没有。擦破了些皮。手蹭破了一点。”

“那可真是一桩了不起的壮举，”莫德说。一个人干了这么惊天动地的一跳，居然能把它说得这样轻描淡写，她的敬佩不由得灼灼发亮。她心里向来有个私下认定的想法：伦纳德勋爵当年立了同样一桩功绩之后，足足吹嘘了一辈子。

===Original===
“No, no, nothing,” said George, who had since wondered why he had ever made such a to-do about climbing up a perfectly stout sheet.

“It was splendid!”

George blushed.

“We are wandering from the main theme,” he said. “I want to help you. I came here at enormous expense to help you. How can I do it?”

===Chinese===
“不，不，没什么。”乔治说。打那以后他一直在纳闷：攀一条结结实实的床单罢了，自己当初何苦闹出偌大的动静。

“那真是棒极了！”

乔治红了脸。

“咱们岔到题外去了。”他说。“我要帮您。我不惜血本搬到这地方来，就是为了帮您。叫我怎么帮法？”

===Original===
Maud hesitated.

“I think you may be offended at my asking such a thing.”

“You needn’t.”

“You see, the whole trouble is that I can’t get in touch with Geoffrey. He’s in London, and I’m here. And any chance I might have of getting to London vanished that day I met you, when Percy saw me in Piccadilly.”

===Chinese===
莫德迟疑起来。

“我怕您听了我要请求的事会恼。”

“不会的。”

“您瞧，全部难处就在于我同杰弗里接不上头。他在伦敦，我在这儿。而我到伦敦去的一线指望，早在遇见您的那一天就断送干净了——那天珀西在皮卡迪利瞧见了我。”

===Original===
“How did your people find out it was you?”

“They asked me﻿—straight out.”

“And you owned up?”

“I had to. I couldn’t tell them a direct lie.”

George thrilled. This was the girl he had had doubts of.

===Chinese===
“您家里人是怎么查出来那是您的？”

“他们问我——单刀直入地问。”

“您就认了？”

“我只好认。直截了当的谎话，我说不出口。”

乔治心头一热。这正是那个他曾起过疑心的姑娘。

===Original===
“So than it was worse then ever,” continued Maud. “I daren’t risk writing to Geoffrey and having the letter intercepted. I was wondering﻿—I had the idea almost as soon as I found that you had come here﻿—”

“You want me to take a letter from you and see that it reaches him. And then he can write back to my address, and I can smuggle the letter to you?”

“That’s exactly what I do want. But I almost didn’t like to ask.”

“Why not? I’ll be delighted to do it.”

“I’m so grateful.”

===Chinese===
“这么一来，局面比先前更糟了，”莫德接着说，“我不敢冒险写信给杰弗里，怕信叫人半道截了去。我正盘算着——几乎是一打听到您来了这儿，主意就有了——”

“您要我捎您一封信，包管送到他手里。然后他把回信寄到我的地址，我再偷偷把信带给您，是不是？”

“这正是我一心想求的事。可我差一点就不好意思开口。”

“这有什么？这事我乐意办。”

“我感激极了。”

===Original===
“Why, it’s nothing. I thought you were going to ask me to look in on your brother and smash another of his hats.”

Maud laughed delightedly. The whole tension of the situation had been eased for her. More and more she found herself liking George. Yet, deep down in her, she realized with a pang that for him there had been no easing of the situation. She was sad for George. The Plummers of this world she had consigned to what they declared would be perpetual sorrow with scarcely a twinge of regret. But George was different.

“Poor Percy!” she said. “I don’t suppose he’ll ever get over it. He will have other hats, but it won’t be the same.” She came back to the subject nearest her heart. “Mr. Bevan, I wonder if you would do just a little more for me?”

“If it isn’t criminal. Or, for that matter, if it is.”

“Could you go to Geoffrey, and see him, and tell him all about me and﻿—and come back and tell me how he looks, and what he said and﻿—and so on?”

===Chinese===
“嗐，这算什么。我还当您要请我去瞧瞧令兄，再砸烂他一顶帽子呢。”

莫德欢天喜地地笑了。整个局面的紧张，在她这一头已经化开了。她越来越觉得自己喜欢乔治。然而在她心底深处，她带着一阵刺痛意识到：在他那一头，局面并没有化开分毫。她替乔治难过。世上普卢默那一号人，被打发去领受他们自己扬言要抱憾终身的痛苦时，她心里连半点刺痛都不曾有。可乔治不一样。

“可怜的珀西！”她说。“我想他这辈子缓不过劲儿来了。帽子他还会再有的，可终归不是原来那顶了。”她转回她心坎上顶要紧的话题。“贝文先生，不知道您肯不肯再为我多办一点点事？”

“只要不犯法就成。再不然，犯法也成。”

“您能不能去见杰弗里，见见他，把关于我的一切都讲给他听，还有——还有回来告诉我他模样如何，说了些什么，还有——等等这一类？”

===Original===
“Certainly. What is his name, and where do I find him?”

“I never told you. How stupid of me. His name is Geoffrey Raymond, and he lives with his uncle, Mr. Wilbur Raymond, at 11a, Belgrave Square.”

“I’ll go to him tomorrow.”

“Thank you ever so much.”

===Chinese===
“没问题。他叫什么名字？我上哪儿找他？”

“我一直没告诉过您。瞧我多糊涂。他叫杰弗里·雷蒙德，同他叔叔威尔伯·雷蒙德先生一起住在贝尔格雷夫广场 11a 号。”

“我明天就去找他。”

“太谢谢您了。”

---

===Original===
George got up. The movement seemed to put him in touch with the outer world. He noticed that the rain had stopped, and that stars had climbed into the oblong of the doorway. He had an impression that he had been in the barn a very long time; and confirmed this with a glance at his watch, though the watch, he felt, understated the facts by the length of several centuries. He was abstaining from too close an examination of his emotions from a prudent feeling that he was going to suffer soon enough without assistance from himself.

“I think you had better be going back,” he said. “It’s rather late. They may be missing you.”

Maud laughed happily.

“I don’t mind now what they do. But I suppose dinners must be dressed for, whatever happens.” They moved together to the door. “What a lovely night after all! I never thought the rain would stop in this world. It’s like when you’re unhappy and think it’s going on forever.”

“Yes,” said George.

===Chinese===
乔治站起身来。这一起身，仿佛把他重新接回了外面的世界。他注意到雨已经停了，星星已经爬进门洞那一方长方形里。他有一种印象，觉得自己在谷仓里已经待了很久很久；看一眼表，证实了这个印象——只是照他感觉，这块表把事实少报了好几个世纪。他有意不去细察自己的情绪，出于一种精明的审慎：不等自己来帮忙，他很快就要够受的了。

“我看您顶好回去了。”他说。“时候不早了。他们说不定在找您。”

莫德欢畅地笑了。

“如今他们爱把我怎么样就怎么样，我全不在乎。不过我想，不管出了什么事，吃晚饭总得换上晚礼服。”两人一齐朝门口走去。“夜色多可爱啊，这场雨到底停了！我方才还当它在这世上永生永世也下不完呢。就跟人伤心的时候一样，总觉得这伤心要没完没了地继续下去。”

“是啊。”乔治说。

===Original===
Maud held out her hand.

“Good night, Mr. Bevan.”

“Good night.”

He wondered if there would be any allusion to the earlier passages of their interview. There was none. Maud was of the class whose education consists mainly of a training in the delicate ignoring of delicate situations.

===Chinese===
莫德伸出手来。

“晚安，贝文先生。”

“晚安。”

他心里嘀咕，不知她会不会提一提这次会面前半场的种种过节。她没有提。莫德出身的那个阶层，教养主要是一门功课：教你如何得体地不理会难堪的处境。

===Original===
“Then you will go and see Geoffrey?”

“Tomorrow.”

“Thank you ever so much.”

“Not at all.”

George admired her. The little touch of formality which she had contrived to impart to the conversation struck just the right note, created just the atmosphere which would enable them to part without weighing too heavily on the deeper aspect of that parting.

===Chinese===
“那么您会上杰弗里那儿去？”

“明天。”

“太谢谢您了。”

“不谢。”

乔治打心眼里佩服她。她设法给这场谈话罩上的那一层小小客套，调门敲得恰到好处，造成的气氛恰到火候，让他们能够就此分手，不至于把分手底下更深的一层分量压上心头。

===Original===
“You’re a real friend, Mr. Bevan.”

“Watch me prove it.”

“Well, I must rush, I suppose. Good night!”

“Good night!”

She moved off quickly across the field. Darkness covered her. The dog in the distance had begun to howl again. He had his troubles, too.

===Chinese===
“您是个真正的朋友，贝文先生。”

“看我证明给您看。”

“那么，我想我得赶紧跑了。晚安！”

“晚安！”

她快步穿过田野去了。黑暗吞没了她。远处那条狗又开始嗥起来。它也有它的烦恼。

## XX / XX


===Original===

Trouble sharpens the vision. In our moments of distress we can see clearly that what is wrong with this world of ours is the fact that Misery loves company and seldom gets it. Toothache is an unpleasant ailment; but, if toothache were a natural condition of life, if all mankind were afflicted with toothache at birth, we should not notice it. It is the freedom from aching teeth of all those with whom we come in contact that emphasizes the agony. And, as with toothache, so with trouble. Until our private affairs go wrong, we never realize how bubbling over with happiness the bulk of mankind seems to be. Our aching heart is apparently nothing but a desert island in an ocean of joy.

George, waking next morning with a heavy heart, made this discovery before the day was an hour old. The sun was shining, and birds sang merrily, but this did not disturb him. Nature is ever callous to human woes, laughing while we weep; and we grow to take her callousness for granted. What jarred upon George was the infernal cheerfulness of his fellow men. They seemed to be doing it on purpose﻿—triumphing over him﻿—glorying in the fact that, however Fate might have shattered him, they were all right.

People were happy who had never been happy before. Mrs. Platt, for instance. A grey, depressed woman of middle age, she had seemed hitherto to have few pleasures beyond breaking dishes and relating the symptoms of sick neighbours who were not expected to live through the week. She now sang. George could hear her as she prepared his breakfast in the kitchen. At first he had had a hope that she was moaning with pain; but this was dispelled when he had finished his toilet and proceeded downstairs. The sounds she emitted suggested anguish, but the words, when he was able to distinguish them, told another story. Incredible as it might seem, on this particular morning Mrs. Platt had elected to be lighthearted. What she was singing sounded like a dirge, but actually it was “Stop your tickling, Jock!” And, later, when she brought George his coffee and eggs, she spent a full ten minutes prattling as he tried to read his paper, pointing out to him a number of merry murders and sprightly suicides which otherwise he might have missed. The woman went out of her way to show him that for her, if not for less fortunate people, God this morning was in His heaven and all was right in the world.

===Chinese===

苦难磨利人的眼睛。每逢身陷愁城，我们便看得清清楚楚：我们这个世界的不妥之处，就在于苦难爱找伴儿，却难得找着伴儿。牙疼是一桩不好受的毛病；可倘若牙疼是人生的常态，倘若全人类一出娘胎就害牙疼，我们便不会把它放在心上。叫我们痛上加痛的，恰恰是眼前接触到的这一位那位偏偏满口好牙、毫无痛楚。牙疼如此，苦难亦然。不到自家的私事出了岔子，我们从来想不到，世上芸芸众生看上去竟那样满心欢喜、快活得直冒泡。我们这颗作痛的心，分明不过是欢乐海洋当中的一座荒岛。

第二天早晨，乔治一觉醒来，心头沉甸甸的，这一天还不到一个钟头，他便有了上述发现。太阳亮堂堂，鸟儿唱得欢，这倒并不搅扰他。大自然对人间苦难从来无动于衷，我们流泪，她发笑；久而久之，我们也把她这副冷心肠视作理所当然。真正硌得乔治难受的，是同类们那股地狱般火辣的欢天喜地劲儿。他们像是故意来这一手——成心气他——存心夸耀：无论命运把他砸成了什么样子，他们自己反正安然无恙。

从前从来没有快活过的人，如今也快活了。譬如普拉特太太。她本是个人到中年、灰扑扑、郁郁寡欢的女人，迄今为止，乐趣仿佛除了摔碟子摔碗，就只有数落左邻右舍病人的病状——照她的说法，那些人没有一个能熬得过这个礼拜。如今她唱起歌来了。她在厨房里给乔治预备早饭，乔治听得见她的动静。起初他还抱着一线希望，只当她是在疼得呻吟；可等他梳洗完毕下了楼，这点指望便烟消云散。她发出的那些声音听上去倒像受着煎熬，可等到能分清歌词了，讲的却是另一码事。说来叫人难以置信，就在今天这个早晨，普拉特太太自选了一副轻快心肠。她唱的那调子听着像哀乐，实际上是《别再胳肢我了，乔克》（Stop Your Tickling, Jock）。后来她端来咖啡和鸡蛋，乔治想看报，她却絮絮叨叨足足讲了十分钟，向他指点出好多起轻快的凶杀案和活泼的自杀案，不然他兴许就错过了。这个女人特特地为这番奔忙，好叫他知道：就算那些没她走运的人未必如此，至少对她来说，今早上帝安稳坐在天堂，世间百事顺遂。


===Original===

Two tramps of supernatural exuberance called at the cottage shortly after breakfast to ask George, whom they had never even consulted about their marriages, to help support their wives and children. Nothing could have been more carefree and *debonair* than the demeanour of these men.

And then Reggie Byng arrived in his grey racing car, more cheerful than any of them.

Fate could not have mocked George more subtly. A sorrow’s crown of sorrow is remembering happier things, and the sight of Reggie in that room reminded him that on the last occasion when they had talked together across this same table it was he who had been in a Fool’s Paradise and Reggie who had borne a weight of care. Reggie this morning was brighter than the shining sun and gayer than the carolling birds.

===Chinese===

早饭过后不多时，又有两个精力充沛得近乎超自然的流浪汉上门来找乔治，请他帮着养活他们的老婆孩子——虽说他们当年成亲，可从来没征求过乔治的意见。这两位的一举一动，那份无忧无虑，那份风流自赏（*debonair*），普天之下再找不出更甚的了。

接着，雷吉·宾开着他那辆灰色赛车来了，比他们哪一个都欢天喜地。

命运嘲弄乔治，再也想不出比这更刁钻的花样了。悲哀的顶点仍是悲哀，那便是追怀往日的欢愉；一看见雷吉走进这间屋子，乔治就想起，上一回两人隔着这张桌子交谈，那时节做愚人乐园美梦的是他，满怀心事压肩的倒是雷吉。而今天早晨，雷吉比灿烂的太阳还要亮堂，比啭鸣的鸟儿还要欢畅。


===Original===

“Hullo-ullo-ullo-ullo-ullo-ullo-ul-lo! Topping morning, isn’t it!” observed Reggie. “The sunshine! The birds! The absolute what-do-you-call-it of everything and so forth, and all that sort of thing, if you know what I mean! I feel like a two-year-old!”

George, who felt older than this by some ninety-eight years, groaned in spirit. This was more than man was meant to bear.

“I say,” continued Reggie, absently reaching out for a slice of bread and smearing it with marmalade, “this business of marriage, now, and all that species of rot! What I mean to say is, what about it? Not a bad scheme, taking it by and large? Or don’t you think so?”

George writhed. The knife twisted in the wound. Surely it was bad enough to see a happy man eating bread and marmalade without having to listen to him talking about marriage.

===Chinese===

“哈罗——喽——喽——喽——喽——喽——喽——喽！早晨好得呱呱叫，是不是！”雷吉招呼道，“这阳光！这鸟儿！这万事万物彻头彻尾的那个——那个什么来着，诸如此类，等等等等，你明白我的意思！我觉得自己像个两岁娃！”

乔治自觉比这个岁数还要年长九十八岁上下，只在心里哀哼了一声。这可超出了血肉之躯所能忍受的限度。

“我说，”雷吉接着说，一面心不在焉地伸手拿了片面包，抹上橘子酱，“结婚这档子事，喏，还有那一整套无聊的滥调！我是说，你有什么高见？总起来说，这主意不赖吧？还是说你不这么想？”

乔治扭动了。刀子在伤口里又拧了一圈。眼睁睁看一个快活人大嚼抹着橘子酱的面包，已经够受了，还得听他大谈婚姻，未免赶尽杀绝。


===Original===

“Well, anyhow, be that as it may,” said Reggie, biting jovially and speaking in a thick but joyous voice. “I’m getting married today, and chance it. This morning, this very morning, I leap off the dock!”

George was startled out of his despondency.

“What!”

“Absolutely, laddie!”

George remembered the conventions.

“I congratulate you.”

===Chinese===

“哦，不管怎样，话又说回来，”雷吉说着，欢天喜地地咬下一大口，嗓音含混，却喜气洋洋，“我今天就要结婚了，豁出去了。就在今天早上，就是这个早上，我就要纵身从跳板上跳下去了！”

乔治从沮丧中惊了一惊。

“什么！”

“千真万确，小老弟！”

乔治想起了世间的规矩。

“恭喜你了。”


===Original===

“Thanks, old man. And not without reason. I’m the luckiest fellow alive. I hardly knew I was alive till now.”

“Isn’t this rather sudden?”

Reggie looked a trifle furtive. His manner became that of a conspirator.

“I should jolly well say it is sudden! It’s got to be sudden. Dashed sudden and deuced secret! If the mater were to hear of it, there’s no doubt whatever she would form a flying wedge and bust up the proceedings with no uncertain voice. You see, laddie, it’s Miss Faraday I’m marrying, and the mater﻿—dear old soul﻿—has other ideas for Reginald. Life’s a rummy thing, isn’t it! What I mean to say is, it’s rummy, don’t you know, and all that.”

===Chinese===

“多谢，老伙计。而且恭喜得有根有据。我是天底下头一号的幸运儿。直到今天我才知道自己原来是活着的。”

“这是不是来得太突然了些？”

雷吉的神色有了几分鬼祟，摆出的架势活像一个密谋家。

“可不是突然得没法说！这事就得突然。突然得要命，还得机密得见鬼！这消息要是传到老妈耳朵里，毫无疑问，她准会结成一个楔形冲锋阵，杀将进来，毫不含糊地把整个仪式砸个稀烂。你瞧，小老弟，我要娶的是法拉第小姐，而老妈——那位亲爱的老人家——给雷金纳德另作了安排。人生真是件怪事，是不是！我是说，它就是怪，你明白，诸如此类。”


===Original===

“Very,” agreed George.

“Who’d have thought, a week ago, that I’d be sitting in this jolly old chair asking you to be my best man? Why, a week ago I didn’t know you, and, if anybody had told me Alice Faraday was going to marry me, I’d have given one of those hollow, mirthless laughs.”

“Do you want me to be your best man?”

“Absolutely, if you don’t mind. You see,” said Reggie confidentially, “it’s like this. I’ve got lots of pals, of course, buzzing about all over London and its outskirts, who’d be glad enough to rally round and join the execution-squad; but you know how it is. Their maters are all pals of my mater, and I don’t want to get them into trouble for aiding and abetting my little show, if you understand what I mean. Now, you’re different. You don’t know the mater, so it doesn’t matter to you if she rolls around and puts the Curse of the Byngs on you, and all that sort of thing. Besides, I don’t know.” Reggie mused. “Of course, this is the happiest day of my life,” he proceeded, “and I’m not saying it isn’t, but you know how it is﻿—there’s absolutely no doubt that a chappie does not show at his best when he’s being married. What I mean to say is, he’s more or less bound to look a fearful ass. And I’m perfectly certain it would put me right off my stroke if I felt that some chump like Jack Ferris or Ronnie Fitzgerald was trying not to giggle in the background. So, if you will be a sportsman and come and hold my hand till the thing’s over, I shall be eternally grateful.”

===Chinese===

“确实怪。”乔治附和道。

“换在一礼拜前，谁能想到我会坐在这把快活的旧椅子上，请你来当我的伴郎？喏，一礼拜前我还根本不认识你；那时候谁要是告诉我，说爱丽丝·法拉第要嫁给我，我准会干笑一声，又空洞又无趣。”

“你是想请我当你的伴郎？”

“千真万确，只要你不嫌烦。是这么回事，”雷吉推心置腹地说，“我自然有一大帮朋友，满伦敦四郊嗡嗡乱飞，个个都巴不得聚拢来，加入行刑队；可你知道，这里头有个讲究。他们的老妈全是我老妈的闺蜜，我可不愿意叫他们因为帮衬了我这点儿小把戏，落个助纣为虐的罪名，惹上麻烦，你明白我的意思吧。你就不一样了。你不认识老妈，就算她气得满地打滚，冲你降下宾家的诅咒（the Curse of the Byngs），诸如此类，也碍不着你什么。再说，我也说不上来。”雷吉沉吟了一下。“当然，这是我一辈子最快活的日子，”他接着说，“我也不说它不是，可你知道是怎么回事——有一条道理铁板钉钉：一个哥们儿结婚的当口，风采决计到不了最佳。我是说，他多多少少准得活像一头吓人的蠢驴。而我百分之百地肯定，倘若我察觉背后有个傻瓜蛋，比如杰克·费里斯或者罗尼·菲茨杰拉德（Ronnie Fitzgerald）之流，正憋着不敢咯咯笑出声来，我这杆非走样不可。所以，你若肯行个好，做条爽快的汉子，过来握着我的手，撑到事情完事大吉，我感激你一辈子。”


===Original===

“Where are you going to be married?”

“In London. Alice sneaked off there last night. It was easy, as it happened, because by a bit of luck old Marshmoreton had gone to town yesterday morning﻿—nobody knows why: he doesn’t go up to London more than a couple of times a year. She’s going to meet me at the Savoy, and then the scheme was to toddle round to the nearest registrar and request the lad to unleash the marriage service. I’m whizzing up in the car, and I’m hoping to be able to persuade you to come with me. Say the word, laddie!”

George reflected. He liked Reggie, and there was no particular reason in the world why he should not give him aid and comfort in this crisis. True, in his present frame of mind, it would be torture to witness a wedding ceremony; but he ought not to let that stand in the way of helping a friend.

“All right,” he said.

===Chinese===

“你们打算在哪儿结婚？”

“在伦敦。爱丽丝昨晚就先溜过去了。说来也巧，事情还真不难，因为托点儿福，老马什莫顿昨天早上进城去了——谁也不知道为什么：他一年里上伦敦的趟数超不过两三回。她要在萨沃伊饭店同我碰头，然后按计划溜溜达达走到最近的婚姻登记处，请那位老兄把结婚这项业务放闸开办。我这就开车嗖嗖直上，只盼能说动你跟我一块儿去。只要你说句话，小老弟！”

乔治盘算了一番。他喜欢雷吉，况且在这场危局里帮他一把、给他些安慰，天底下也找不出什么特别的理由说不行。诚然，照他眼下这副心境，亲眼看一场婚礼无异于受刑；可他总不该拿这个作挡箭牌，不去援手一位朋友。

“行。”他说。


===Original===

“Stout fellow! I don’t know how to thank you. It isn’t putting you out or upsetting your plans, I hope, or anything on those lines?”

“Not at all. I had to go up to London today, anyway.”

“Well, you can’t get there quicker than in my car. She’s a hummer. By the way, I forgot to ask. How is your little affair coming along? Everything going all right?”

“In a way,” said George. He was not equal to confiding his troubles to Reggie.

===Chinese===

“好汉子！我都不知道怎么谢你才好。这没有搅了你的安排、打乱你的计划吧，或者诸如此类的情况？”

“完全没有。反正我今天本来就要上伦敦去。”

“那好，坐我的车，你决计找不出更快的路子。这车是个呱呱叫的宝贝。哦对了，我忘了问。你那桩小事儿进展如何？一切都还顺当吧？”

“勉强算是吧。”乔治说。他没法打起精神，把自己的烦恼向雷吉倾吐。


===Original===

“Of course, your trouble isn’t like mine was. What I mean is, Maud loves you, and all that, and all you’ve got to think out is a scheme for laying the jolly old family a stymie. It’s a pity﻿—almost﻿—that yours isn’t a case of having to win the girl, like me; because by Jove, laddie,” said Reggie with solemn emphasis, “I could help you there. I’ve got the thing down fine. I’ve got the infallible dope.”

George smiled bleakly.

“You have? You’re a useful fellow to have around. I wish you would tell me what it is.”

“But you don’t need it.”

“No, of course not. I was forgetting.”

===Chinese===

“当然啦，你的难处跟我那阵子的不一样。我是说，莫德爱你，诸如此类，你只消琢磨出一个法子，把他那快乐的老一家子来一个‘斯泰米’（stymie）堵死就行。几乎可以说可惜的是——你这桩事，竟不是像我这样，非得自个儿去赢姑娘的心；因为，哎哟老天，小老弟，”雷吉一脸郑重地强调，“这一层我倒真能帮你。这门学问我吃得透透的。我手里握着百试百灵的秘笈。”

乔治惨然一笑。

“你有？有你这么个人在身边真有用。但愿你能把那秘笈讲给我听听。”

“可你用不上啊。”

“对，当然用不上。我倒把这茬忘了。”


===Original===

Reggie looked at his watch.

“We ought to be shifting in a quarter of an hour or so. I don’t want to be late. It appears that there’s a catch of some sort in this business of getting married. As far as I can make out, if you roll in after a certain hour, the Johnnie in charge of the proceedings gives you the miss-in-baulk, and you have to turn up again next day. However, we shall be all right unless we have a breakdown, and there’s not much chance of that. I’ve been tuning up the old car since seven this morning, and she’s sound in wind and limb, absolutely. Oil﻿—petrol﻿—water﻿—air﻿—nuts﻿—bolts﻿—sprockets﻿—carburetor﻿—all present and correct. I’ve been looking after them like a lot of baby sisters. Well, as I was saying, I’ve got the dope. A week ago I was just one of the mugs﻿—didn’t know a thing about it﻿—but now! Gaze on me, laddie! You see before you old Colonel Romeo, the Man who Knows! It all started on the night of the ball. There was the dickens of a big ball, you know, to celebrate old Boots’ coming-of-age﻿—to which, poor devil, he contributed nothing but the sunshine of his smile, never having learned to dance. On that occasion a most rummy and extraordinary thing happened. I got pickled to the eyebrows!” He laughed happily. “I don’t mean that that was a unique occurrence and so forth, because, when I was a bachelor, it was rather a habit of mine to get a trifle submerged every now and again on occasions of decent mirth and festivity. But the rummy thing that night was that I showed it. Up till then, I’ve been told by experts, I was a chappie in whom it was absolutely impossible to detect the symptoms. You might get a bit suspicious if you found I couldn’t move, but you could never be certain. On the night of the ball, however, I suppose I had been filling the radiator a trifle too enthusiastically. You see, I had deliberately tried to shove myself more or less below the surface in order to get enough nerve to propose to Alice. I don’t know what your experience has been, but mine is that proposing’s a thing that simply isn’t within the scope of a man who isn’t moderately woozled. I’ve often wondered how marriages ever occur in the dry States of America. Well, as I was saying, on the night of the ball a most rummy thing happened. I thought one of the waiters was you!”

===Chinese===

雷吉看了看表。

“咱们一刻钟上下就该动身了。我可不想迟到。听说结婚这档子事里头藏着个什么机关。依我看，要是过了钟点才滚进门，主持其事的那位老兄就判你一个‘误局’（miss-in-baulk），你只好第二天再跑一趟。不过，只要车不趴窝，咱们就不碍事，而趴窝的机会也微乎其微。打今天早上七点起，我就在调弄这辆老车，她如今气息绵长、筋骨结实，绝对没得挑。机油——汽油——水——气——螺帽——螺栓——链轮——汽化器——样样在册，全部合格。我一直拿它们当一群小妹妹似的照应着。嗯，话说回来，我手里是有秘笈的。一礼拜前，我还只是个呆瓜——这里头的事一窍不通——可如今呢！瞧瞧我吧，小老弟！站在你面前的，是老罗密欧上校，深谙此道的那一位！一切得从舞会那晚说起。你知道，那是一场大得吓人的舞会，替老靴子庆贺成人礼——可怜虫自己，除了笑得阳光灿烂，别的什么也没出，因为他压根儿没学过跳舞。就在那个场合，出了一桩顶顶古怪、顶顶出奇的事。我醉到了眉毛根！”他开怀大笑。“我并不是说这是什么破天荒的事，诸如此类，因为单身汉那些年，碰上像样的欢宴庆贺，时不时灌个半沉半浮，本是我的老习惯。可那晚的怪处在于，我露了相。据行家们告诉我，在那之前，我是个无论怎样也查不出症状的家伙。你兴许会起些疑心，倘若发现我动弹不得；可你永远吃不准。然而舞会那晚，我想是我往散热器里灌水灌得略略起过了头。是这样的，为了攒足胆量向爱丽丝求婚，我存心把自己多多少少按到水面底下去。不知道你的经验如何，我的经验是：一个人要是没有几分醺醺然的底气，求婚这桩事压根儿不在他力所能及的范围之内。我常纳闷，美国那些禁酒州的人，婚事都是怎么办成的。好，闲话休提，舞会那晚出了一件顶古怪的事。我把一个侍者当成是你了！”


===Original===

He paused impressively to allow this startling statement to sink in.

“And was he?” said George.

“Absolutely not! That was the rummy part of it. He looked as like you as your twin brother.”

“I haven’t a twin brother.”

“No, I know what you mean, but what I mean to say is he looked just like your twin brother would have looked if you had had a twin brother. Well, I had a word or two with this chappie, and after a brief conversation it was borne in upon me that I was up to the gills. Alice was with me at the time, and noticed it too. Now you’d have thought that that would have put a girl off a fellow, and all that. But no. Nobody could have been more sympathetic. And she has confided to me since that it was seeing me in my oiled condition that really turned the scale. What I mean is, she made up her mind to save me from myself. You know how some girls are. Angels absolutely! Always on the look out to pluck brands from the burning, and whatnot. You may take it from me that the good seed was definitely sown that night.”

===Chinese===

他煞有介事地顿住，好叫这句惊人的话沉淀到听者心里去。

“那他是吗？”乔治问。

“压根儿不是！怪就怪在这里。他跟你长得一般无二，活像你的孪生兄弟。”

“我没有孪生兄弟。”

“对，我明白你的意思，可我是说，他看上去就跟你那位孪生兄弟一模一样——倘若你有孪生兄弟的话。喏，我跟这位仁兄搭了几句话，短短一谈之后，我心里就明白了：我已经醉到了腮帮子。当时爱丽丝就在我身边，她也看了出来。你兴许会说，这种事女孩子见了准得倒胃口，诸如此类。可偏不。她那份体恤，谁也不能更多了。后来她向我吐露，正是看见我这副‘上了油’的醉态，才真正叫她拿定了主意。我是说，她打定主意，要把我从我自己手里搭救出来。你知道有些姑娘就是那路人。天使，彻头彻尾的天使！成天守在那里，专爱从火中往外抽柴，诸如此类。你可以信我的话，那晚好种子是确确实实播下去了。”


===Original===

“Is that your recipe, then? You would advise the would-be bridegroom to buy a case of champagne and a wedding licence and get to work? After that it would be all over except sending out the invitations?”

Reggie shook his head.

“Not at all. You need a lot more than that. That’s only the start. You’ve got to follow up the good work, you see. That’s where a number of chappies would slip up, and I’m pretty certain I should have slipped up myself, but for another singularly rummy occurrence. Have you ever had a what-do-you-call it? What’s the word I want? One of those things fellows get sometimes.”

===Chinese===

“这么说，这就是你的秘方喽？照你的高见，预备做新郎的人只消买上一箱香槟、领一张结婚许可证，放手开干就是？再往后，就万事俱备，只欠印发喜柬了？”

雷吉摇了摇头。

“差得远。你需要的远不止这个。那只是个开头。你得趁热打铁，把好事追下去，你明白。多少人就在这一步失了手；我敢说我自己也照失不误，若不是又出了一桩怪得出奇的事。你可曾碰见过一种——那个词儿怎么说来着？我要找的是哪个词儿？就是爷们儿有时候会得上的那种东西。”


===Original===

“Headaches?” hazarded George.

“No, no. Nothing like that. I don’t mean anything you get﻿—I mean something you get, if you know what I mean.”

“Measles?”

“Anonymous letter. That’s what I was trying to say. It’s a most extraordinary thing, and I can’t understand even now where the deuce they came from, but just about then I started to get a whole bunch of anonymous letters from some chappie unknown who didn’t sign his name.”

“What you mean is that the letters were anonymous,” said George.

“Absolutely. I used to get two or three a day sometimes. Whenever I went up to my room, I’d find another waiting for me on the dressing-table.”

===Chinese===

“头疼？”乔治试着猜道。

“不，不。全不是那回事。我不是说你身上招来的那种东西——我是说你收到的、拿在手里能看的那种东西，你明白我的意思吧。”

“麻疹？”

“匿名信。我要说的就是这个。这事顶顶不可思议，直到今天我也闹不明白，那些信到底是哪个见鬼的寄来的，可就在那前后，我开始收到一大摞匿名信，寄信的是个无名之辈，从来不署名。”

“你的意思是，那些信本身是匿名的。”乔治说。

“一点不错。有时候一天能来上两三封。每逢我回房，梳妆台上总又候着一封。”


===Original===

“Offensive?”

“Eh?”

“Were the letters offensive? Anonymous letters usually are.”

“These weren’t. Not at all, and quite the reverse. They contained a series of perfectly topping tips on how a fellow should proceed who wants to get hold of a girl.”

“It sounds as though somebody had been teaching you jujitsu by post.”

===Chinese===

“写得难听吗？”

“嗯？”

“那些信写得难听吗？匿名信通常都难听。”

“这几封不难听。一点儿也不，恰恰相反。里头是一整套妙不可言的锦囊，教一个想把姑娘追到手的爷们儿该怎样行事。”

“听起来倒像是有人开了个函授班，用邮包教你柔道。”


===Original===

“They were great! Real red-hot stuff straight from the stable. Priceless tips like ‘Make yourself indispensable to her in little ways,’ ‘Study her tastes,’ and so on and so forth. I tell you, laddie, I pretty soon stopped worrying about who was sending them to me, and concentrated the old bean on acting on them. They worked like magic. The last one came yesterday morning, and it was a topper! It was all about how a chappie who was nervous should proceed. Technical stuff, you know, about holding her hand and telling her you’re lonely and being sincere and straightforward and letting your heart dictate the rest. Have you ever asked for one card when you wanted to fill a royal flush and happened to pick out the necessary ace? I did once, when I was up at Oxford, and, by Jove, this letter gave me just the same thrill. I didn’t hesitate. I just sailed in. I was cold sober, but I didn’t worry about that. Something told me I couldn’t lose. It was like having to hole out a three-inch putt. And﻿—well, there you are, don’t you know.” Reggie became thoughtful. “Dash it all! I’d like to know who the fellow was who sent me those letters. I’d like to send him a wedding-present or a bit of the cake or something. Though I suppose there won’t be any cake, seeing the thing’s taking place at a registrar’s.”

“You could buy a bun,” suggested George.

===Chinese===

“那些信妙极了！地地道道是从马厩里直接递出来的滚烫情报。绝妙的锦囊妙计，比如‘在小事上叫她离不得你’，‘揣摩她的喜好’，诸如此类，不一而足。我跟你说，小老弟，没用多久，我就不再操心信是谁寄的了，一门心思用我这颗老脑袋瓜琢磨照办。那些招数灵得像施了魔法。最后一封是昨天早上来的，更是锦囊里的压卷之作！通篇讲的是一个心里发怵的哥们儿该怎样行事。全是技术要领，你知道，什么握住她的手啦，告诉她说你心里孤单啦，待人要诚恳直率啦，剩下的一切都交给你自己的心去指点啦。你可曾有过这样的经历：一手牌只差一张就凑成同花大顺，伸手摸来一张，偏偏正是缺的那张爱司？我碰过一回，那还是在牛津念书的时候，哎哟老天，这封信给我的那份激动，跟当年一模一样。我没有踌躇。我干脆放手大干了一场。我当时滴酒未沾，可这一点我并不担心。冥冥中有个声音告诉我，这一把输不了。就跟一记三英寸的推杆，非进洞不可，是一个道理。还有——喏，事情就这么成了，你知道。”雷吉陷入了沉思。“真见鬼！我倒想知道那些信是哪位仁兄寄的。我想送他一件结婚贺礼，或是分他一角喜糕什么的。不过我想喜糕是不会有喽，谁叫婚礼是在登记处办的呢。”

“你可以去买个小圆甜面包。”乔治提议。


===Original===

“Well, I shall never know, I suppose. And now how about trickling forth? I say, laddie, you don’t object if I sing slightly from time to time during the journey? I’m so dashed happy, you know.”

“Not at all, if it’s not against the traffic regulations.”

===Chinese===

“唉，我想我永远也不会知道了。这会儿，咱们悠悠地淌出门去怎么样？我说，小老弟，一路上有我时不时稍稍哼上几句歌，你不反对吧？你知道，我欢天喜地得没治了。”

“绝不反对，只要不违反交通规则就行。”


===Original===

Reggie wandered aimlessly about the room in an ecstasy.

“It’s a rummy thing,” he said meditatively, “I’ve just remembered that, when I was at school, I used to sing a thing called the what’s-it’s-name’s wedding song. At house-suppers, don’t you know, and whatnot. Jolly little thing. I daresay you know it. It starts ‘Ding dong! Ding dong!’ or words to that effect, ‘Hurry along! For it is my wedding-morning!’ I remember you had to stretch out the ‘mor’ a bit. Deuced awkward, if you hadn’t laid in enough breath. ‘The Yeoman’s Wedding-Song.’ That was it. I knew it was some chappie or other’s. And it went on ‘And the bride in something or other is doing something I can’t recollect.’ Well, what I mean is, now it’s my wedding-morning! Rummy, when you come to think of it, what? Well, as it’s getting tolerable late, what about it? Shift ho?”

===Chinese===

雷吉在屋里飘飘然地团团乱转，脚底下没有定向。

“说来也怪，”他若有所思地说，“我刚刚想起来，上学那阵子，我常唱一支歌，叫什么名字来着的婚礼歌。在宿舍晚宴上唱，你明白，诸如此类的场合。一支快活的小曲。我敢说你也会。开头是‘叮——咚！叮——咚！’，大意如此，‘快快赶路！因为今天是我的婚礼之晨！’我记得那个‘mor’音还得拖上一拖。要是气没憋够，可就狼狈死了。《自耕农婚礼歌》（The Yeoman’s Wedding-Song）。就是它。我就说嘛，是哪位仁兄的大作。底下还有一句‘而新娘如何如何，正在做一件我记不得的事’。喏，我的意思是，如今轮到我的婚礼之晨了！细想起来真怪，是不是？唔，时候已经相当不早了，怎么样？拔锚开路？”


===Original===

“I’m ready. Would you like me to bring some rice?”

“Thank you, laddie, no. Dashed dangerous stuff, rice! Worse than shrapnel. Got your hat? All set?”

“I’m waiting.”

“Then let the revels commence,” said Reggie. “Ding dong! Ding Dong! Hurry along! For it is my wedding-morning! And the bride﻿—Dash it, I wish I could remember what the bride was doing!”

===Chinese===

“我好了。要不要我带点儿米去？”

“多谢，小老弟，不必了。米这东西危险得要命！比榴霰弹还凶。帽子带了吗？都齐了？”

“我候着呢。”

“那么，狂欢大典开始，”雷吉说，“叮——咚！叮——咚！快快赶路！因为今天是我的婚礼之晨！还有新娘——真见鬼，但愿我想得起新娘当时在干什么！”


===Original===

“Probably writing you a note to say that she’s changed her mind, and it’s all off.”

“Oh, my God!” exclaimed Reggie. “Come on!”

===Chinese===

“多半在给你写条子，说她改了主意，这门亲事作罢了。”

“哦，我的老天爷！”雷吉大叫。“快走！”

## XXI / XXI

===Original===

Mr. and Mrs. Reginald Byng, seated at a table in the corner of the Regent Grillroom, gazed fondly into each other’s eyes. George, seated at the same table, but feeling many miles away, watched them moodily, fighting to hold off a depression which, cured for a while by the exhilaration of the ride in Reggie’s racing-car (it had beaten its previous record for the trip to London by nearly twenty minutes), now threatened to return. The gay scene, the ecstasy of Reggie, the more restrained but equally manifest happiness of his bride﻿—these things induced melancholy in George. He had not wished to attend the wedding-lunch, but the happy pair seemed to be revolted at the idea that he should stroll off and get a bite to eat somewhere else.

“Stick by us, laddie,” Reggie had said pleadingly, “for there is much to discuss, and we need the counsel of a man of the world. We are married all right﻿—”

“Though it didn’t seem legal in that little registrar’s office,” put in Alice.

“﻿—But that, as the blighters say in books, is but a beginning, not an end. We have now to think out the most tactful way of letting the news seep through, as it were, to the mater.”

===Chinese===

雷金纳德·宾先生和太太坐在摄政烤肉馆（the Regent Grillroom）角落的一张桌旁，情意绵绵地彼此凝望。乔治坐在同一张桌上，却只觉得自己远在许多英里之外，闷闷地看着这一对，苦苦抵挡着一阵抑郁——那抑郁方才叫乘雷吉赛车兜风的兴奋压下去了一阵（这一趟去伦敦，把先前自己创下的纪录缩短了将近二十分钟），这会儿眼看又要卷土重来。满堂的欢畅景象，雷吉的心醉神迷，他新娘那有所节制却同样藏不住的幸福——这一切都在乔治心里酿出了愁绪。他本不想来吃这顿婚宴午餐，可这对新人一想到他要自己溜达出去、另找地方随便吃一口，就仿佛打心眼里反感这个主意。

“陪着我们，老弟，”雷吉方才央求道，“有好多事要商量，我们得请一位通晓世故的人给拿拿主意。我们总算是结成婚了——”

“不过在那间小小的登记处里，这事瞧着倒不大像真的合法结了婚似的。”爱丽丝插了一句。

“——可那呀，照书里那帮家伙的说法，不过是个开头，不是终点。眼下我们得想出一个最得体的法子，让消息——可以这么说吧——一点一点渗透到老妈那里去。”

===Original===

“And Lord Marshmoreton,” said Alice. “Don’t forget he has lost his secretary.”

“And Lord Marshmoreton,” amended Reggie. “And about a million other people who’ll be most frightfully peeved at my doing the Wedding Glide without consulting them. Stick by us, old top. Join our simple meal. And over the old coronas we will discuss many things.”

The arrival of a waiter with dishes broke up the silent communion between husband and wife, and lowered Reggie to a more earthly plane. He refilled the glasses from the stout bottle that nestled in the ice-bucket﻿—(“Only this one, dear!” murmured the bride in a warning undertone, and “All right darling!” replied the dutiful groom)﻿—and raised his own to his lips.

“Cheerio! Here’s to us all! Maddest, merriest day of all the glad New Year and so forth. And now,” he continued, becoming sternly practical, “about the good old sequel and aftermath, so to speak, of this little binge of ours. What’s to be done. You’re a brainy sort of feller, Bevan, old man, and we look to you for suggestions. How would you set about breaking the news to mother?”

===Chinese===

“还有马什莫顿勋爵，”爱丽丝说，“别忘了，他的秘书没了。”

“还有马什莫顿勋爵，”雷吉修正道。“还有大约一百万个别的人——我不等请示、不等商量就滑了这一步‘婚礼滑步’（Wedding Glide），他们准得气个半死。陪着我们，老伙计。来跟我们便饭一顿。回头抽着上好的科罗娜雪茄，咱们要谈的事情多着呢。”

堂倌端着菜碟子到来，打破了夫妻俩默默传情的境界，也把雷吉降回到比较接地气的层面上。他从偎在冰桶里的那瓶胖乎乎的酒里把各人的杯子重新斟满（“只这一杯哦，亲爱的！”新娘压低嗓子警告道，“好嘞，亲爱的！”尽职的新郎应道），然后举起自己那杯凑到唇边。

“干杯！祝咱们大伙儿！‘快乐新年里顶疯顶乐的一天’，云云。眼下呢，”他接着说下去，摆出一副严肃务实的架势，“就该谈谈咱们这顿小酌的老一套续篇和余波了，姑且这么说吧。该怎么办呢。贝文，老兄，你是个有脑筋的家伙，我们全指望你出主意。要是换你去把消息捅给母亲，你会怎么个下手法？”

===Original===

“Write her a letter,” said George.

Reggie was profoundly impressed.

“Didn’t I tell you he would have some devilish shrewd scheme?” he said enthusiastically to Alice. “Write her a letter! What could be better? Poetry, by Gad!” His face clouded. “But what would you say in it? That’s a pretty knotty point.”

“Not at all. Be perfectly frank and straightforward. Say you are sorry to go against her wishes﻿—”

“Wishes,” murmured Reggie, scribbling industrially on the back of the marriage licence.

===Chinese===

“给她写封信。”乔治说。

雷吉佩服得五体投地。

“我是不是早说过，他准有鬼精鬼精的妙计？”他兴冲冲地对爱丽丝说，“给她写封信！还能有更妙的？简直是诗，老天爷！”他的脸色随即阴了下来。“可是信里写什么呢？这可是个相当伤脑筋的关节。”

“一点也不伤脑筋。把话摊开来，直截了当。就说你不愿违逆她的心愿，深感抱歉——”

“心愿。”雷吉一面喃喃念叨，一面在结婚许可证的背面埋头疾书。

===Original===

“﻿—But you know that all she wants is your happiness﻿—”

Reggie looked doubtful.

“I’m not sure about that last bit, old thing. You don’t know the mater!”

“Never mind, Reggie,” put in Alice. “Say it, anyhow. Mr. Bevan is perfectly right.”

“Right ho, darling! All right, laddie﻿—‘happiness.’ And then?”

===Chinese===

“——可你也知道，她图的无非是你的幸福——”

雷吉一脸迟疑。

“最后这半句我可不敢保，老宝贝。你是不了解老妈！”

“别管这个，雷吉，”爱丽丝插进来，“照写就是。贝文先生说得千真万确。”

“好嘞，亲爱的！行，老弟——‘幸福’。然后呢？”

===Original===

“Point out in a few well-chosen sentences how charming Mrs. Byng is﻿ ﻿…”

“Mrs. Byng!” Reggie smiled fatuously. “I don’t think I ever heard anything that sounded so indescribably ripping. That part’ll be easy enough. Besides, the mater knows Alice.”

“Lady Caroline has seen me at the castle,” said his bride doubtfully, “but I shouldn’t say she knows me. She has hardly spoken a dozen words to me.”

“There,” said Reggie, earnestly, “you’re in luck, dear heart! The mater’s a great speaker, especially in moments of excitement. I’m not looking forward to the time when she starts on me. Between ourselves, laddie, and meaning no disrespect to the dear soul, when the mater is moved and begins to talk, she uses up most of the language.”

===Chinese===

“再用几句斟酌得宜的话，点明宾太太是一位多么讨人喜欢的太太……”

“宾太太！”雷吉傻乎乎地笑了。“我从来没听过什么字眼，听着有这般说不出的受用。这一段容易得很。再说，老妈也认得爱丽丝。”

“卡罗琳夫人在城堡里见过我，”他的新娘拿不定地说，“可我不好意思说她认得我。她同我说过的话，满打满算不到一打。”

“这不就对了吗，”雷吉诚恳地说，“你运气好着呢，我的心肝儿！老妈是位了不起的演说家，激动的时候尤其了得。她哪天掉转炮口来对我开讲，我可毫不期待。咱们私下说说，老弟——绝没有对那位好人不敬的意思——老妈一动了感情开腔，语言库存的大半就得叫她一个人消耗掉。”

===Original===

“Outspoken, is she?”

“I should hate to meet the person who could out-speak her,” said Reggie.

George sought information on a delicate point.

“And financially? Does she exercise any authority over you in that way?”

“You mean has the mater the first call on the family doubloons?” said Reggie. “Oh, absolutely not! You see, when I call her the mater, it’s using the word in a loose sense, so to speak. She’s my stepmother really. She has her own little collection of pieces of eight, and I have mine. That part’s simple enough.”

===Chinese===

“她很能说，是吧？”

“谁能说得过她，我可不愿意同那人照面。”雷吉说。

乔治就一个微妙的问题探询。

“那么钱财上呢？这一层她也管得着你吗？”

“你是问，家里的达布隆金币（doubloons）是不是老妈优先调用？”雷吉说。“哦，完全没有的事！你要明白，我叫她老妈，可以说只是这个词儿的宽泛用法。她其实是我继母。她自己有一小窖八片币（pieces of eight），我也有我的一份。这一层再简单不过。”

===Original===

“Then the whole thing is simple. I don’t see what you’ve been worrying about.”

“Just what I keep telling him, Mr. Bevan,” said Alice.

“You’re a perfectly free agent. She has no hold on you of any kind.”

Reggie Byng blinked dizzily.

“Why, now you put it like that,” he exclaimed, “I can see that I jolly well am! It’s an amazing thing, you know, habit and all that. I’ve been so accustomed for years to jumping through hoops and shamming dead when the mater lifted a little finger, that it absolutely never occurred to me that I had a soul of my own. I give you my honest word I never saw it till this moment.”

===Chinese===

“那么整桩事情就简单了。我看不出你原先愁的到底是什么。”

“我一直就是这么跟他讲的，贝文先生。”爱丽丝说。

“你如今是个彻底自由自在的人。她拿不住你半点儿，哪一样都拿不住。”

雷吉·宾晕乎乎地眨着眼睛。

“嗐，你这么一摆出来，”他嚷道，“我看我千真万确是自由的！你知道，习惯这东西真是神了。这么多年，老妈小指头一抬，我就钻火圈、装死，惯得我压根儿想都没想过：我自己还有一个灵魂。我拿老实话向你担保，直到这一刻之前，我从来没看见过它。”

===Original===

“And now it’s too late!”

“Eh?”

George indicated Alice with a gesture. The newly-made Mrs. Byng smiled.

“Mr. Bevan means that now you’ve got to jump through hoops and sham dead when I lift a little finger!”

Reggie raised her hand to his lips, and nibbled at it gently.

“Blessums ’ittle finger! It shall lift it and have ’ums Reggie jumping through.﻿ ﻿…” He broke off and tendered George a manly apology. “Sorry, old top! Forgot myself for the moment. Shan’t occur again! Have another chicken or an eclair or some soup or something!”

===Chinese===

“而如今已经太迟了！”

“嗯？”

乔治朝爱丽丝那边扬了扬下巴。新出炉的宾太太嫣然一笑。

“贝文先生是说，从今往后，我小指头一抬，钻火圈、装死的就该轮到你啦！”

雷吉把她的手举到唇边，轻轻地啃了啃。

“乖乖小指头小宝贝！它只管一抬，管保叫雷吉乖乖钻圈圈。……”他忽然刹住，向乔治端端正正地道了个歉。“对不住，老伙计！一时忘形了。下不为例！再来块鸡，或者来只奶油泡芙，或者来点儿汤，随便什么！”

===Original===

Over the cigars Reggie became expansive.

“Now that you’ve lifted the frightful weight of the mater off my mind, dear old lad,” he said, puffing luxuriously, “I find myself surveying the future in a calmer spirit. It seems to me that the best thing to do, as regards the mater and everybody else, is simply to prolong the merry wedding-trip till Time the Great Healer has had a chance to cure the wound. Alice wants to put in a week or so in Paris.﻿ ﻿…”

“Paris!” murmured the bride ecstatically.

“Then I would like to trickle southwards to the Riviera﻿ ﻿…”

“If you mean Monte Carlo, dear,” said his wife with gentle firmness, “no!”

“No, no, not Monte Carlo,” said Reggie hastily, “though it’s a great place. Air﻿—scenery﻿—and whatnot! But Nice and Bordighera and Mentone and other fairly ripe resorts. You’d enjoy them. And after that﻿ ﻿… I had a scheme for buying back my yacht, the jolly old Siren, and cruising about the Mediterranean for a month or so. I sold her to a local sportsman when I was in America a couple of years ago. But I saw in the paper yesterday that the poor old buffer had died suddenly, so I suppose it would be difficult to get hold of her for the time being.” Reggie broke off with a sharp exclamation.

===Chinese===

抽上雪茄之后，雷吉的话匣子敞开了。

“既然你把我心头那座老妈大山给卸了下来，亲爱的小老弟，”他喷着痛快惬意的烟圈说，“我觉得自己能心平气和地瞻望前程了。依我看，说到老妈和旁的什么人，顶好的办法无非是把欢欢喜喜的蜜月旅行拖长些，好让‘时间’这位伟大的疗伤圣手有机会把伤口治愈。爱丽丝想在巴黎盘桓个把星期。……”

“巴黎！”新娘出神地喃喃道。

“然后呢，我想再一路往南涓涓地淌到里维埃拉（the Riviera）去……”

“亲爱的，你若说的是蒙特卡洛（Monte Carlo）——不行！”他太太温柔而坚决地说。

“不不，不去蒙特卡洛，”雷吉连忙说，“虽说那地方确实不赖。空气啦——风景啦——诸如此类！我说的是尼斯（Nice）、博尔迪盖拉（Bordighera）、门通（Mentone），还有旁几个呱呱叫的休假地。你去了准高兴。再往后嘛……我原本打了个算盘，把我那条快活的老游艇‘塞壬号’买回来，到地中海上巡游个把月。两年前我在美洲把她卖给了一位当地的玩主。可昨天我在报上瞧见，那位可怜的老兄突然过世了，所以我想，眼下要把她弄到手只怕不容易。”雷吉说到这里猛地顿住，失声惊呼。

===Original===

“My sainted aunt!”

“What’s the matter?”

Both his companions were looking past him, wide-eyed. George occupied the chair that had its back to the door, and was unable to see what it was that had caused their consternation; but he deduced that someone known to both of them must have entered the restaurant; and his first thought, perhaps naturally, was that it must be Reggie’s “mater.” Reggie dived behind a menu, which he held before him like a shield, and his bride, after one quick look, had turned away so that her face was hidden. George swung around, but the newcomer, whoever he or she was, was now seated and indistinguishable from the rest of the lunchers.

“Who is it?”

===Chinese===

“我的老天姑奶奶！”

“怎么了？”

他那两位同伴都睁圆了眼睛，直勾勾地望着他的身后。乔治坐的那把椅子背对着门，看不出是什么叫他们如此惊慌；不过他推断，准是有他们两人都认识的人走进了这家馆子；他头一个念头——或许也出于自然——便是雷吉的“老妈”来了。雷吉一头扎到一张菜单后面，把菜单举在身前，活像举着一面盾牌；他的新娘飞快瞄了一眼，随即扭过脸去，把脸藏了起来。乔治猛地转过身，可这位新来的人物——不管是谁——已经落了座，同满堂用午餐的客人再分不出彼此。

“是谁呀？”

===Original===

Reggie laid down the menu with the air of one who after a momentary panic rallies.

“Don’t know what I’m making such a fuss about,” he said stoutly. “I keep forgetting that none of these blighters really matter in the scheme of things. I’ve a good mind to go over and pass the time of day.”

“Don’t!” pleaded his wife. “I feel so guilty.”

“Who is it?” asked George again. “Your stepmother?”

“Great Scott, no!” said Reggie. “Nothing so bad as that. It’s old Marshmoreton.”

===Chinese===

雷吉放下菜单，那副神气，像一个慌了一瞬便重整旗鼓的人。

“真不知道我在这儿大惊小怪个什么劲，”他强自镇定地说，“我老记不住：照天地大局而论，这些家伙没一个当真要紧。我倒真想走过去，同那位寒暄两句。”

“别去！”他的太太央求道，“我实在觉得心里有愧。”

“到底是谁呀？”乔治又问，“你的继母？”

“老天爷，不是！”雷吉说，“还没糟到那个地步。是老马什莫顿。”

===Original===

“Lord Marshmoreton!”

“Absolutely! And looking positively festive.”

“I feel so awful, Mr. Bevan,” said Alice. “You know, I left the castle without a word to anyone, and he doesn’t know yet that there won’t be any secretary waiting for him when he gets back.”

Reggie took another look over George’s shoulder and chuckled.

“It’s all right, darling. Don’t worry. We can nip off secretly by the other door. He’s not going to stop us. He’s got a girl with him! The old boy has come to life﻿—absolutely! He’s gassing away sixteen to the dozen to a frightfully pretty girl with gold hair. If you slew the old bean round at an angle of about forty-five, Bevan, old top, you can see her. Take a look. He won’t see you. He’s got his back to us.”

===Chinese===

“马什莫顿勋爵！”

“千真万确！而且看上去简直一派过节的兴头。”

“我心里难受极了，贝文先生，”爱丽丝说，“您瞧，我不辞而别，一个人也没告诉，就这样离开了城堡；他还不知道，等他回去的时候，不会再有秘书等着他了。”

雷吉越过乔治的肩头又望了一眼，吃吃笑了。

“没事儿的，亲爱的。别担心。我们可以从另一扇门悄悄溜掉。他拦不住咱们。他身边带着一位姑娘呢！老家伙活过来了——千真万确！他正跟一位金头发、漂亮得要命的姑娘一口气十六车地神聊。贝文，老伙计，你把老脑袋瓜斜转个四十五度上下，就能瞧见她。瞧瞧去。他看不见你的。他背对着咱们呢。”

===Original===

“Do you call her pretty?” asked Alice disparagingly.

“Now that I take a good look, precious,” replied Reggie with alacrity, “no! Absolutely not! Not my style at all!”

His wife crumbled bread.

“I think she must know you, Reggie dear,” she said softly. “She’s waving to you.”

“She’s waving to *me*,” said George, bringing back the sunshine to Reggie’s life, and causing the latter’s face to lose its hunted look. “I know her very well. Her name’s Dore. Billie Dore.”

===Chinese===

“你管这个叫漂亮？”爱丽丝不以为然地问。

“如今我再仔细一端详，宝贝儿，”雷吉赶忙答道，“不！绝对不漂亮！根本不是我中意的那一路！”

他太太捻着面包屑。

“我看她准认得你，亲爱的雷吉，”她轻声说，“她在朝你招手呢。”

“她招手是朝*我*招。”乔治说，这一下把阳光重新带回了雷吉的生活，叫他脸上那副被追猎的神气一扫而空。“我跟她熟得很。她姓多尔。比莉·多尔。”

===Original===

“Old man,” said Reggie, “be a good fellow and slide over to their table and cover our retreat. I know there’s nothing to be afraid of really, but I simply can’t face the old boy.”

“And break the news to him that I’ve gone, Mr. Bevan,” added Alice.

“Very well, I’ll say goodbye, then.”

“Goodbye, Mr. Bevan, and thank you ever so much.”

Reggie shook George’s hand warmly.

“Goodbye, Bevan old thing, you’re a ripper. I can’t tell you how bucked up I am at the sportsmanlike way you’ve rallied round. I’ll do the same for you one of these days. Just hold the old boy in play for a minute or two while we leg it. And, if he wants us, tell him our address till further notice is Paris. What ho! What ho! What ho! Toodle-oo, laddie, toodle-oo!”

===Chinese===

“老兄，”雷吉说，“劳你大驾，溜达到他们那桌去，替我们打掩护撤退。我知道其实没什么可怕的，可我实在没脸见那老爷子。”

“再劳驾跟他说一声，我已经走了，贝文先生。”爱丽丝补上一句。

“好，那么我就此告辞了。”

“再见，贝文先生，多谢多谢您。”

雷吉热络地握住乔治的手。

“再见，贝文老伙计，你可真是个响当当的好人。你这样够交情地赶来助阵，我提神振气得没法儿同你说。改天我也照样帮你一回。你只消把那老爷子绊住一两分钟，好让我们开溜。还有，他要是问起我们，就告诉他：另行通知以前，我们的地址是巴黎。好哇！好哇！好哇！回见啦，老弟，回见啦！”

===Original===

George threaded his way across the room. Billie Dore welcomed him with a friendly smile. The earl, who had turned to observe his progress, seemed less delighted to see him. His weather-beaten face wore an almost furtive look. He reminded George of a schoolboy who has been caught in some breach of the law.

“Fancy seeing you here, George!” said Billie. “We’re always meeting, aren’t we? How did you come to separate yourself from the pigs and chickens? I thought you were never going to leave them.”

“I had to run up on business,” explained George. “How are you, Lord Marshmoreton?”

The earl nodded briefly.

===Chinese===

乔治七弯八绕地穿过餐厅。比莉·多尔带着友好的笑容迎他。伯爵早已转过身来，一路注视他的行进，见了他却不怎么欢喜。他那张风吹日晒的脸上，带着近乎做贼心虚的神气。他叫乔治想起一个犯了校规、被当场拿住的小学生。

“真想不到在这儿碰见你，乔治！”比莉说，“咱们老碰头，是不是？你怎么舍得撇下那些猪呀鸡呀的？我还当你这辈子都不肯离开它们了。”

“我不得不上城里来办点事。”乔治解释道。“您好吗，马什莫顿勋爵？”

伯爵简短地点了点头。

===Original===

“So you’re on to him, too?” said Billie. “When did you get wise?”

“Lord Marshmoreton was kind enough to call on me the other morning and drop the incognito.”

“Isn’t dadda the foxiest old thing!” said Billie delightedly. “Imagine him standing there that day in the garden, kidding us along like that! I tell you, when they brought me his card last night after the first act and I went down to take a slant at this Lord Marshmoreton and found dadda hanging round the stage door, you could have knocked me over with a whisk-broom.”

“I have not stood at the stage-door for twenty-five years,” said Lord Marshmoreton sadly.

“Now, it’s no use your pulling that Henry W. Methuselah stuff,” said Billie affectionately. “You can’t get away with it. Anyone can see you’re just a kid. Can’t they, George?” She indicated the blushing earl with a wave of the hand. “Isn’t dadda the youngest thing that ever happened?”

===Chinese===

“这么说，你也把他看穿了？”比莉说，“什么时候开的窍？”

“马什莫顿勋爵前两天早上屈驾光临寒舍，摘掉了隐身招牌。”

“我们家老爹是不是天底下顶狡猾的老家伙！”比莉乐不可支地说，“想想看，那天他就站在园子里，那样儿地糊弄咱们！我跟你说，昨天晚上第一幕演完，他们把他的名片递上来，我下楼去瞧瞧这位马什莫顿勋爵到底是何方神圣，一瞧老爹正在后台门口打转——拿把小衣刷都能把我撂个大跟头。”

“我已经有二十五年没在后台门口站过了。”马什莫顿勋爵凄然说。

“得啦，您少来您那套‘亨利·W·玛土撒拉’（Henry W. Methuselah）的老把戏，”比莉亲亲热热地说，“蒙不了人的。谁看得出您不过是个毛头小子。是不是，乔治？”她一挥手，指着那位羞红了脸的伯爵。“老爹是不是开天辟地以来顶年轻的人物？”

===Original===

“Exactly what I told him myself.”

Lord Marshmoreton giggled. There is no other verb that describes the sound that proceeded from him.

“I feel young,” he admitted.

“I wish some of the juveniles in the shows I’ve been in,” said Billie, “were as young as you. It’s getting so nowadays that one’s thankful if a juvenile has teeth.” She glanced across the room. “Your pals are walking out on you, George. The people you were lunching with,” she explained. “They’re leaving.”

“That’s all right. I said goodbye to them.” He looked at Lord Marshmoreton. It seemed a suitable opportunity to break the news. “I was lunching with Mr. and Mrs. Byng,” he said.

===Chinese===

“这话跟我亲口对他说的，一字不差。”

马什莫顿勋爵咯咯笑了。再没有别的动词，能形容从他喉咙里发出的那个声音。

“我觉得自己年轻。”他承认道。

“我演过的那些戏里头，有些个‘小生’（juvenile），”比莉说，“要是有您这么年轻就好了。如今这世道，小生嘴里还剩着满口牙，就得谢天谢地了。”她朝餐厅那头瞥了一眼。“你的伙伴们把你撂下走啦，乔治。跟你一块儿吃饭的那几位，”她解释道，“他们走了。”

“没关系，我已经同他们道过别了。”他望着马什莫顿勋爵。看来得把消息捅出去，这正是机会。“我方才是同宾先生和宾太太一道用的午餐。”他说。

===Original===

Nothing appeared to stir beneath Lord Marshmoreton’s tanned forehead.

“Reggie Byng and his wife, Lord Marshmoreton,” added George.

This time he secured the earl’s interest. Lord Marshmoreton started.

“What!”

“They are just off to Paris,” said George.

“Reggie Byng is not married!”

===Chinese===

马什莫顿勋爵晒得黧黑的额头底下，似乎毫无动静。

“是雷吉·宾和他的太太，马什莫顿勋爵。”乔治补上一句。

这一回总算把伯爵的注意抓着了。马什莫顿勋爵吓了一跳。

“什么！”

“他们这就要动身去巴黎。”乔治说。

“雷吉·宾没有结婚！”

===Original===

“Married this morning. I was best man.”

“Busy little creature!” interjected Billie.

“But﻿—but﻿—!”

“You know his wife,” said George casually. “She was a Miss Faraday. I think she was your secretary.”

It would have been impossible to deny that Lord Marshmoreton showed emotion. His mouth opened, and he clutched the tablecloth. But just what the emotion was George was unable to say till, with a sigh that seemed to come from his innermost being, the other exclaimed “Thank Heaven!”

===Chinese===

“今天早上结的婚。我就是伴郎。”

“真是只忙忙叨叨的小活宝！”比莉插了一句。

“可是——可是——！”

“他的太太您是认得的。”乔治漫不经心地说，“她原来姓法拉第。我想，她正是您的秘书。”

没法不承认，马什莫顿勋爵动了感情。他张大了嘴，一把抓住桌布。可这感情究竟是什么，乔治一时说不准；直到对方长叹一声——那叹息像是打心底最深处发出来的——失声喊道：“谢天谢地！”

===Original===

George was surprised.

“You’re glad?”

“Of course I’m glad!”

“It’s a pity they didn’t know how you were going to feel. It would have saved them a lot of anxiety. I rather gathered they supposed that the shock was apt to darken your whole life.”

“That girl,” said Lord Marshmoreton vehemently, “was driving me crazy. Always bothering me to come and work on that damned family history. Never gave me a moment’s peace﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

乔治大出意外。

“您倒是高兴？”

“我当然高兴！”

“可惜他们没能未卜先知，知道您会作何感想，不然倒省得他们操好些心。我多少听出一个意思来——他们只怕以为，这番打击准会把您的整个后半生都罩黑了。”

“那个姑娘，”马什莫顿勋爵激愤地说，“快把我逼疯了。成天缠着我，催我去编那部该死的《家族史》。一刻也不让我安生……”

===Original===

“I liked her,” said George.

“Nice enough girl,” admitted his lordship grudgingly. “But a damned nuisance about the house; always at me to go on with the family history. As if there weren’t better things to do with one’s time than writing all day about my infernal fools of ancestors!”

“Isn’t dadda fractious today?” said Billie reprovingly, giving the Earl’s hand a pat. “Quit knocking your ancestors! You’re very lucky to have ancestors. I wish I had. The Dore family seems to go back about as far as the presidency of Millard Fillmore, and then it kind of gets discouraged and quite cold. Gee! I’d like to feel that my great-great-great-grandmother had helped Queen Elizabeth with the rent. I’m strong for the fine old stately families of England.”

“Stately old fiddlesticks!” snapped the earl.

“Did you see his eyes flash then, George? That’s what they call aristocratic rage. It’s the fine old spirit of the Marshmoretons boiling over.”

“I noticed it,” said George. “Just like lightning.”

===Chinese===

“我挺喜欢她。”乔治说。

“够好的姑娘。”勋爵大人勉强承认。“可她在家里真是个天大的麻烦；成天催我接着写那部《家族史》。倒好像一个人的一天光阴，除了成天写我那些见鬼的祖宗蠢货，再没有更好的使法似的！”

“老爹今天是不是有点儿闹脾气？”比莉带着几分数落的口气说，在伯爵手背上拍了一拍。“别再数落您的祖宗了！您有祖宗，福气大着呢。我还巴不得有呢。多尔家的家谱往上数，大约能数到米勒德·菲尔莫尔（Millard Fillmore）当总统那阵儿，再往上就有点儿泄了气，慢慢凉透了。天哪！我倒真想能觉得我的曾曾曾祖母当年帮伊丽莎白女王凑过房租。英国这些气派堂皇的古老世家，我举双手拥护。”

“气派个什么劲，老掉牙的胡扯！”伯爵抢白道。

“方才您瞧见他眼睛冒火没有，乔治？这就是人家说的‘贵族之怒’。是马什莫顿家那股古老的烈性，正滚沸外溢呢。”

“我注意到了。”乔治说。“跟闪电一个样。”

===Original===

“It’s no use trying to fool us, dadda,” said Billie. “You know just as well as I do that it makes you feel good to think that, every time you cut yourself with your safety-razor, you bleed blue!”

“A lot of silly nonsense!” grumbled the earl.

“What is?”

“This foolery of titles and aristocracy. Silly fetish-worship! One man’s as good as another.﻿ ﻿…”

“This is the spirit of ’76!” said George approvingly.

“Regular I.W.W. stuff,” agreed Billie. “Shake hands with the President of the Bolsheviki!”

===Chinese===

“想糊弄咱们是没用的，老爹，”比莉说，“您心里跟我一样门儿清：每回叫安全剃刀刮破了口子，一想到自己淌出来的是蓝血，您心里就美滋滋的！”

“一派傻里傻气的胡话！”伯爵咕哝道。

“什么胡话？”

“这班头衔和贵族的把戏。傻里傻气的拜物教！人跟人本是一般好。……”

“这正是 76 年的精神！”乔治嘉许道。

“地地道道的世界产业工人联盟（I.W.W.）的路数。”比莉附和道，“快跟布尔什维克的主席握个手吧！”

===Original===

Lord Marshmoreton ignored the interruption. There was a strange look in his eyes. It was evident to George, watching him with close interest, that here was a revelation of the man’s soul; that thoughts, locked away for years in the other’s bosom, were crying for utterance.

“Damned silly nonsense! When I was a boy, I wanted to be an engine-driver. When I was a young man, I was a Socialist and hadn’t any idea except to work for my living and make a name for myself. I was going to the colonies. Canada. The fruit farm was actually bought. Bought and paid for!” He brooded a moment on that long-lost fruit farm. “My father was a younger son. And then my uncle must go and break his neck hunting, and the baby, poor little chap, got croup or something﻿ ﻿… And there I was, saddled with the title, and all my plans gone up in smoke﻿ ﻿… Silly nonsense! Silly nonsense!”

He bit the end of a cigar. “And you can’t stand up against it,” he went on ruefully. “It saps you. It’s like some damned drug. I fought against it as long as I could, but it was no use. I’m as big a snob as any of them now. I’m afraid to do what I want to do. Always thinking of the family dignity. I haven’t taken a free step for twenty-five years.”

George and Billie exchanged glances. Each had the uncomfortable feeling that they were eavesdropping and hearing things not meant to be heard. George rose.

===Chinese===

马什莫顿勋爵没有理会这场打岔。他眼睛里有一种异样的光。乔治兴味十足地紧看着他，看得明明白白：这袒露出来的是这个人的灵魂；多年锁在他胸中的念头，正在嚷着要一吐为快。

“该死的傻话！我小时候，一心想当个火车司机。年轻时，我是个社会主义者，心里就一个念头：靠干活吃饭，给自己挣出个名堂来。我本要去殖民地。加拿大。果园都当真买下了。买了，钱也付清了！”他对那座久已失落的果园出了一会儿神。“我父亲是次子。偏偏我伯父去打猎，摔断了脖子；还有那个小娃娃，可怜的小家伙，得了哮吼还是什么毛病……于是就这么着，头衔套上了我的脖子，满腹盘算统统化成了青烟……傻话！傻话！”

他咬掉一支雪茄的头儿。“可你拗不过它，”他凄然接着说，“它把你一点一点泡软。就像某种该死的毒品。我跟它对抗，能撑多久撑多久，可没有用。如今我同他们里头随便哪一个一样，是个大大的势利眼。我想做的事，我不敢做。时时刻刻想着家族的体面。二十五年来，我没有迈过一步自由的步子。”

乔治和比莉交换了一个眼色。两个人都有一种不自在的感觉，仿佛在偷听，听见了本不打算叫人听见的话。乔治站起身来。

===Original===

“I must be getting along now,” he said. “I’ve one or two things to do. Glad to have seen you again, Billie. Is the show going all right?”

“Fine. Making money for you right along.”

“Goodbye, Lord Marshmoreton.”

The earl nodded without speaking. It was not often now that he rebelled even in thoughts against the lot which fate had thrust upon him, and never in his life before had he done so in words. He was still in the grip of the strange discontent which had come upon him so abruptly.

There was a silence after George had gone.

===Chinese===

“我这就得走了，”他说，“还有一两件事要办。比莉，很高兴又见到了你。戏上演得还顺当吧？”

“好得很。一直在替您赚钱呢。”

“再见，马什莫顿勋爵。”

伯爵点了点头，没有作声。如今他连在念头里反抗命运强加给他的那份安排，也是难得的了；至于说出口，他一辈子还从来不曾有过。那股来得这样突兀的莫名不快，这会儿还牢牢攥着他。

乔治走后，沉默了一阵。

===Original===

“I’m glad we met George,” said Billie. “He’s a good boy.” She spoke soberly. She was conscious of a curious feeling of affection for the sturdy, weather-tanned little man opposite her. The glimpse she had been given of his inner self had somehow made him come alive for her.

“He wants to marry my daughter,” said Lord Marshmoreton. A few moments before, Billie would undoubtedly have replied to such a statement with some jocular remark expressing disbelief that the earl could have a daughter old enough to be married. But now she felt oddly serious and unlike her usual flippant self.

“Oh?” was all she could find to say.

“She wants to marry him.”

Not for years had Billie Dore felt embarrassed, but she felt so now. She judged herself unworthy to be the recipient of these very private confidences.

===Chinese===

“我很高兴咱们见到了乔治，”比莉说，“他是个好小伙。”她说得一本正经。她对对面这位结实健壮、满脸风霜的小个子男人，心头涌起一种说不出的怜爱。方才那一眼得见的内心深处，不知怎么，竟叫他整个儿在她眼前活了起来。

“他想娶我的女儿。”马什莫顿勋爵说。换在几分钟以前，这样一句话，比莉必定要拿一句打趣的话来顶回去，表示难以相信伯爵会有一个到了出嫁年纪的女儿。可这一刻她觉得自己异样地严肃，全不像平日那个没正形的样子。

“哦？”她找得出来的话只有这么一声。

“她也想嫁他。”

好几年来，比莉·多尔从没觉得过窘，这会儿却窘了。她掂量自己不配承接这样极隐秘的知心话。

===Original===

“Oh?” she said again.

“He’s a good fellow. I like him. I liked him the moment we met. He knew it, too. And I knew he liked me.”

A group of men and girls from a neighbouring table passed on their way to the door. One of the girls nodded to Billie. She returned the nod absently. The party moved on. Billie frowned down at the tablecloth and drew a pattern on it with a fork.

“Why don’t you let George marry your daughter, Lord Marshmoreton?”

The earl drew at his cigar in silence.

===Chinese===

“哦？”她又应了一声。

“他是个好人。我喜欢他。头一回见面我就喜欢他了。他也知道我喜欢他。我也知道他喜欢我。”

邻桌的一群男男女女起身往门口去。其中一个姑娘朝比莉点了点头。她心不在焉地点头回了礼。那一行人走远了。比莉蹙着眉，低头望着桌布，拿叉子在布上划出一道道花样。

“您为什么不索性让乔治娶了您的女儿呢，马什莫顿勋爵？”

伯爵默默抽着雪茄。

===Original===

“I know it’s not my business,” said Billie apologetically, interpreting the silence as a rebuff.

“Because I’m the Earl of Marshmoreton.”

“I see.”

“No you don’t,” snapped the earl. “You think I mean by that that I think your friend isn’t good enough to marry my daughter. You think that I’m an incurable snob. And I’ve no doubt he thinks so, too, though I took the trouble to explain my attitude to him when we last met. You’re wrong. It isn’t that at all. When I say ‘I’m the Earl of Marshmoreton,’ I mean that I’m a poor spineless fool who’s afraid to do the right thing because he daren’t go in the teeth of the family.”

“I don’t understand. What have your family got to do with it?”

“They’d worry the life out of me. I wish you could meet my sister Caroline! That’s what they’ve got to do with it. Girls in my daughter’s unfortunate position have got to marry position or money.”

===Chinese===

“我知道这不该我多嘴。”比莉道歉说，把这份沉默当成了回绝。

“因为我是马什莫顿伯爵。”

“哦，我明白了。”

“不，您不明白。”伯爵没好气地说。“您以为我说这话，是想说您那位朋友配不上我的女儿。您以为我是个无可救药的势利鬼。我毫不怀疑他也是这么想的，虽然上回见面，我费了好一番口舌向他剖白过我的态度。您错了。根本不是那么回事。我说‘我是马什莫顿伯爵’，意思是说，我是个可怜巴巴、没脊梁骨的蠢货，明明知道对的事也不敢去做，就因为他不敢逆着家族行事。”

“我还是不懂。您的家族跟这有什么相干？”

“他们会把我磨得脱层皮。但愿您能见见我妹妹卡罗琳！相干就在这儿。我女儿那样身陷不幸境地的姑娘，只能嫁地位，或者嫁金钱。”

===Original===

“Well, I don’t know about position, but when it comes to money﻿—why, George is the fellow that made the dollar-bill famous. He and Rockefeller have got all there is, except the little bit they have let Andy Carnegie have for carfare.”

“What do you mean? He told me he worked for a living.” Billie was becoming herself again. Embarrassment had fled.

“If you call it work. He’s a composer.”

“I know. Writes tunes and things.”

Billie regarded him compassionately.

===Chinese===

“这个嘛，地位我不懂，可要说钱——嗨，让美钞名满天下的人物就是乔治。天底下的钱，他和洛克菲勒（Rockefeller）全占了，只留下那么一丁点儿让安迪·卡内基（Andy Carnegie）拿去当车马费。”

“这话怎么讲？他跟我说过，他是靠干活吃饭的。”比莉又缓过劲儿来了。窘迫早飞到九霄云外。

“要是那也算干活的话。他是个作曲家。”

“我知道。写写曲子什么的。”

比莉满怀怜惜地望着他。

===Original===

“And I suppose, living out in the woods the way that you do that you haven’t a notion that they pay him for it.”

“Pay him? Yes, but how much? Composers were not rich men in my day.”

“I wish you wouldn’t talk of ‘your day’ as if you telling the boys down at the corner store about the good times they all had before the Flood. You’re one of the Younger Set and don’t let me have to tell you again. Say, listen! You know that show you saw last night. The one where I was supported by a few underlings. Well, George wrote the music for that.”

“I know. He told me so.”

“Well, did he tell you that he draws three percent of the gross receipts? You saw the house we had last night. It was a fair average house. We are playing to over fourteen thousand dollars a week. George’s little bit of that is﻿—I can’t do it in my head, but it’s a round four hundred dollars. That’s eighty pounds of your money. And did he tell you that this same show ran over a year in New York to big business all the time, and that there are three companies on the road now? And did he mention that this is the ninth show he’s done, and that seven of the others were just as big hits as this one? And did he remark in passing that he gets royalties on every copy of his music that’s sold, and that at least ten of his things have sold over half a million? No, he didn’t, because he isn’t the sort of fellow who stands around blowing about his income. But you know it now.”

===Chinese===

“我猜呀，您那样住在树林子深处，多半不知道人家做这个是要付他钱的。”

“付他钱？是啊，可付多少？在我那个年代，作曲家可算不上阔人。”

“求您别再把‘您那个年代’挂在嘴上，倒像您在对街角小店那帮伙计讲大洪水以前大伙儿过的好日子似的。您是年轻一代里的人物，别叫我再说第二遍。哎，您听着！您昨晚看的那出戏，还记得吧。就是我被底下几个小喽啰捧着的那出。跟您说，那出戏的音乐就是乔治写的。”

“我知道。他同我说过。”
