“哦，那他跟您说过没有，总票房收入他要抽百分之三？昨晚我们的上座您亲眼见了，那不过是平平常常的一场。我们眼下每星期做一万四千多美元的生意。乔治分到的那一小份——我心算不来，反正整四百美元。折成你们英国的钱就是八十镑。那他讲没讲过，这出戏在纽约连演了一年多，上座自始至终红火，如今外埠还有三个班子在巡演？他提没提过，这是他写的第九出戏，其余几出里有七出都同这出一样轰动？他顺没顺嘴说过，每卖一份他的曲谱他都有版税可抽，少说有十支曲子各卖过了五十万份？没有，他没说过，因为他不是那种杵在那儿吹嘘自己进项的人。不过这会儿您知道了。”

===Original===

“Why, he’s a rich man!”

“I don’t know what you call rich, but, keeping on the safe side, I should say that George pulls down in a good year, during the season﻿—around five thousand dollars a week.”

Lord Marshmoreton was frankly staggered.

“A thousand pounds a week! I had no idea!”

“I thought you hadn’t. And, while I’m boosting George, let me tell you another thing. He’s one of the whitest men that ever happened. I know him. You can take it from me, if there’s anything rotten in a fellow, the show-business will bring it out, and it hasn’t come out in George yet, so I guess it isn’t there. George is all right!”

===Chinese===

“哎呀，他是个阔人哪！”

“我不知道您管什么样儿叫阔，往稳妥里说，我估摸乔治碰上好年成，当红季里头——一星期能进五千美元上下。”

马什莫顿勋爵这回是结结实实惊呆了。

“一星期一千镑！我做梦也没想到！”

“我就想您没想过。既然正替乔治吹嘘，索性再告诉您一件事。他是开天辟地以来顶清白的那路人。这个人我摸得透。您记着我的话：一个人骨子里但凡有点儿烂的，戏行这一行当早晚给它泡出来；乔治身上至今没泡出来，我估摸压根儿就没有。乔治没得挑！”

===Original===

“He has at least an excellent advocate.”

“Oh, I’m strong for George. I wish there were more like him﻿ ﻿… Well, if you think I’ve butted in on your private affairs sufficiently, I suppose I ought to be moving. We’ve a rehearsal this afternoon.”

“Let it go!” said Lord Marshmoreton boyishly.

“Yes, and how quick do you think they would let me go, if I did? I’m an honest working-girl, and I can’t afford to lose jobs.”

Lord Marshmoreton fiddled with his cigar-butt.

“I could offer you an alternative position, if you cared to accept it.”

===Chinese===

“至少他有一位出色的辩护人。”

“哦，我可是死心塌地拥乔治的。但愿天下多几个他这样的人……好啦，要是您觉得我把您的私事搅和得也够可以了，我想我该挪窝了。我们下午还有排练。”

“别去了嘛！”马什莫顿勋爵孩子气十足地说。

“是啊——那您倒想想，我真这么撂了挑子，人家能多痛快放我走？我是个老老实实做工挣饭的姑娘，丢不起活计。”

马什莫顿勋爵捻弄着雪茄头。

“只要您肯屈就，我倒能给您提供一个别的差事。”

===Original===

Billie looked at him keenly. Other men in similar circumstances had made much the same remark to her. She was conscious of feeling a little disappointed in her new friend.

“Well?” she said dryly. “Shoot.”

“You gathered, no doubt, from Mr. Bevan’s conversation, that my secretary has left me and run away and got married? Would you like to take her place?”

It was not easy to disconcert Billie Dore, but she was taken aback. She had been expecting something different.

“You’re a shriek, dadda!”

===Chinese===

比莉目光锐利地端详他。处在同样情形下的别的男人，也对她说过大同小异的话。她觉得自己对这位新朋友，有几分失望了。

“哦？”她干巴巴地说，“说吧。”

“贝文先生方才那番话，您想必也听出来了：我的秘书丢下我，跑掉结婚了。您愿意顶她的缺吗？”

要叫比莉·多尔慌了神，可不是件容易事，可这一回她着实吃了一惊。她原以为要来的话是另一路。

“您可真逗死人了，老爹！”

===Original===

“I’m perfectly serious.”

“Can you see me at a castle?”

“I can see you perfectly.” Lord Marshmoreton’s rather formal manner left him. “Do please accept, my dear child. I’ve got to finish this damned family history some time or other. The family expect me to. Only yesterday my sister Caroline got me in a corner and bored me for half an hour about it. I simply can’t face the prospect of getting another Alice Faraday from an agency. Charming girl, charming girl, of course, but﻿ ﻿… but﻿ ﻿… well, I’ll be damned if I do it, and that’s the long and short of it!”

Billie bubbled over with laughter.

“Of all the impulsive kids!” she gurgled. “I never met anyone like you, dadda! You don’t even know that I can use a typewriter.”

===Chinese===

“我完全是当真的。”

“您想象得出我在一座城堡里的模样吗？”

“我看得清清楚楚。”马什莫顿勋爵那副拘拘谨谨的架子卸了下来。“千万请您答应吧，我亲爱的孩子。这部该死的《家族史》，我横竖总得把它编完。家里人都指望着我编完。就在昨天，我妹妹卡罗琳还把我逼到墙角，就为这件事足足磨了我半个钟头。一想到要从事务所里再领一位爱丽丝·法拉第来，我就压根儿没法面对。招人喜欢的姑娘，当然招人喜欢，可是……可是……唉，打死我也不干，话说到头就这一句！”

比莉笑得咯咯直冒泡。

“您这说干就干的毛头孩子，真是天下头一号！老爹，我可从来没见过您这样的人！您连我会不会用打字机都不知道呢。”

===Original===

“I do. Mr. Bevan told me you were an excellent stenographer.”

“So George has been boosting me, too, has he?” She mused. “I must say, I’d love to come. That old place got me when I saw it that day.”

“That’s settled, then,” said Lord Marshmoreton masterfully. “Go to the theatre and tell them﻿—tell whatever is usual in these cases. And then go home and pack, and meet me at Waterloo at six o’clock. The train leaves at six-fifteen.”

“Return of the wanderer, accompanied by dizzy blonde! You’ve certainly got it all fixed, haven’t you! Do you think the family will stand for me?”

“Damn the family!” said Lord Marshmoreton, stoutly.

===Chinese===

“我知道。贝文先生告诉我，您的速记工夫好得很。”

“这么说，乔治也替我吹过嘘了，是不是？”她沉吟起来。“说真的，我很乐意去。那天头一回瞧见那座老宅子，我就叫它给迷住了。”

“那就一言为定。”马什莫顿勋爵拿出专断的口吻说。“上剧院去同他们讲一声——照这类事情的通例，该怎么说就怎么说。然后回家收拾行李，六点钟到滑铁卢车站同我碰头。火车六点一刻开。”

“浪子荣归，还带着个晕头晕脑的金发妞儿！您可真是一下子全安排妥帖了，是不是！您看家里那几位容得下我吗？”

“该死的家族！”马什莫顿勋爵硬邦邦地说。

===Original===

“There’s one thing,” said Billie complacently, eyeing her reflection in the mirror of her vanity-case, “I may glitter in the fighting-top, but it is genuine. When I was a kid, I was a regular little towhead.”

“I never supposed for a moment that it was anything but genuine.”

“Then you’ve got a fine, unsuspicious nature, dadda, and I admire you for it.”

“Six o’clock at Waterloo,” said the earl. “I will be waiting for you.”

Billie regarded him with affectionate admiration.

“Boys will be boys,” she said. “All right. I’ll be there.”

===Chinese===

“有一点，”比莉照着随身粉盒的小镜子顾影自赏，得意扬扬地说，“我在‘战斗桅楼’（fighting-top）上固然闪闪发亮，可这亮光是纯金足赤的。我小时候，本就是个地地道道的小黄毛丫头。”

“我可从来没有一秒钟疑心过它不是天生的。”

“那您就是长了一副坦荡不起疑心的好性子，老爹，就冲这个我也佩服您。”

“六点整，滑铁卢车站。”伯爵说，“我等您。”

比莉用又亲又敬的目光望着他。

“男孩子终究是男孩子。”她说，“行。我准到。”

## XXII / XXII

===Original===
“Young blighted Albert,” said Keggs the butler, shifting his weight so that it distributed itself more comfortably over the creaking chair in which he reclined, “let this be a lesson to you, young feller me lad.”
The day was a week after Lord Marshmoreton’s visit to London, the hour six o’clock. The housekeeper’s room, in which the upper servants took their meals, had emptied. Of the gay company which had just finished dinner only Keggs remained, placidly digesting. Albert, whose duty it was to wait on the upper servants, was moving to and fro, morosely collecting the plates and glasses. The boy was in no happy frame of mind. Throughout dinner the conversation at table had dealt almost exclusively with the now celebrated elopement of Reggie Byng and his bride, and few subjects could have made more painful listening to Albert.
“What’s been the result and what I might call the upshot,” said Keggs, continuing his homily, “of all your making yourself so busy and thrusting of yourself forward and meddling in the affairs of your elders and betters? The upshot and issue of it ’as been that you are out five shillings and nothing to show for it. Five shillings what you might have spent on some good book and improved your mind! And goodness knows it wants all the improving it can get, for of all the worthless, idle little messers it’s ever been my misfortune to have dealings with, you are the champion. Be careful of them plates, young man, and don’t breathe so hard. You ’aven’t got hasthma or something, ’ave you?”
“I can’t breathe now!” complained the stricken child.

===Chinese===
“小倒霉蛋阿尔伯特，”管家凯格斯说，一边挪了挪身子，好让全身的分量在身下那把咯吱作响的躺椅上摊布得更舒坦些，“这可要引以为戒，我的小伙子。”
这一天，距马什莫顿勋爵的伦敦之行已有一个星期，钟点是六点。女管家室——上房仆役们用饭的屋子——已经空了。方才欢欢喜喜吃完晚饭的那一伙人，只剩下凯格斯一个，安安然然地消化着。阿尔伯特的差事是伺候上房仆役，这会儿正走来走去，愁眉苦脸地收着盘子和杯子。这孩子心里痛快不了。整顿饭的工夫，桌上谈的几乎全是雷吉·宾同他新娘那桩如今名噪一时的私奔；天底下再挑不出几个题目，比这更叫阿尔伯特听着扎耳朵的了。
“结果是什么，用我的话说，收场又落着了什么，”凯格斯接着他的训话说下去，“你这么上赶着忙活，这么往前硬凑，这么插手你长辈们、上头人们的事务，到头来落着了什么？结果和收场就是：你白折了五先令，什么也没捞着。这五先令，你本可以拿去买本好书，长长学问！再说你的学问，老天知道，多么迫切需要长进——在我打过交道的那些一无所用、游手好闲的小混子里头，你是头一号。盘子小心着点儿，年轻人，喘气别这么粗。你不是得了哮喘（hasthma）什么的吧？”
“我如今都不敢喘气了！”这位受难的孩子叫屈道。

===Original===
“Not like a grampus you can’t, and don’t you forget it.” Keggs wagged his head reprovingly. “Well, so your Reggie Byng’s gone and eloped, has he! That ought to teach you to be more careful another time ’ow you go gambling and plunging into sweepstakes. The idea of a child of your age ’aving the audacity to thrust ’isself forward like that!”
“Don’t call him my Reggie Byng! I didn’t draw ’im!”
“There’s no need to go into all that again, young feller. You accepted ’im freely and without prejudice when the fair exchange was suggested, so for all practical intents and purposes he is your Reggie Byng. I ’ope you’re going to send him a wedding-present.”
“Well, you ain’t any better off than me, with all your ’ighway robbery!”

===Chinese===
“像头喷气的海豚似的，你喘得可欢了，这点你给我记住。”凯格斯把脑袋一摇，训诫道。“好哇，你那位雷吉·宾跟人私奔了，是吧！这一回总该教你学乖：往后再赌钱、往斯威普斯泰克里头砸注子，得多留几个心眼。你这般年纪的毛孩子，竟有这么大的胆子往前硬凑，像话吗！”
“别叫他‘我的雷吉·宾’！我又没抽着他！”
“这些账不必再翻腾了，小伙子。公换的提议一提出来，你是自愿的、毫无成见地收下了他；所以照实际效用说，他就是你那位雷吉·宾了。我盼着你打算给他送件结婚礼物哪。”
“哼，你也没比我强到哪儿去，使的还不是你那套拦路抢劫！”

===Original===
“My what!”
“You ’eard what I said.”
“Well, don’t let me ’ear it again. The idea! If you ’ad any objections to parting with that ticket, you should have stated them clearly at the time. And what do you mean by saying I ain’t any better off than you are?”
“I ’ave my reasons.”

===Chinese===
“我的什么！”
“我说的话你听清了。”
“那就别叫我再听一遍。亏你想得出！你若当真舍不得那张票子，当时就该把话亮明白。你又凭什么说我没比你强到哪儿去？”
“我有我的缘故。”

===Original===
“You think you ’ave, which is a very different thing. I suppose you imagine that you’ve put a stopper on a certain little affair by surreptitiously destroying letters entrusted to you.”
“I never!” exclaimed Albert with a convulsive start that nearly sent eleven plates dashing to destruction.
“ ’Ow many times have I got to tell you to be careful of them plates?” said Keggs sternly. “Who do you think you are﻿—a juggler on the ’Alls, ’urling them about like that? Yes, I know all about that letter. You thought you was very clever, I’ve no doubt. But let me tell you, young blighted Albert, that only the other evening ’er ladyship and Mr. Bevan ’ad a long and extended interview in spite of all your hefforts. I saw through your little game, and I proceeded and went and arranged the meeting.”
In spite of himself Albert was awed. He was oppressed by the sense of struggling with a superior intellect.

===Chinese===
“你是自以为有——这可是两码事。我猜你满心以为，偷偷销毁那些托付给你的信件，就把某一桩小小的勾当给掐断了。”
“我可没有！”阿尔伯特惊得浑身一抽，差点儿叫十一只盘子一齐跌个粉身碎骨。
“得叫我说你多少遍，才肯小心那些盘子？”凯格斯声色俱厉地说。“你当你是什么人——音乐厅里耍杂技的，把盘子这么抛来抛去？不错，那封信我全知道。你只当你自己机灵得很，这自然不消说得。可我告诉你，小倒霉蛋阿尔伯特：就在前几天晚上，小姐大人同贝文先生全不顾你百般使出的努力（hefforts），着实谈了一场又长又透的话。你那点小把戏，我一眼看穿；于是我便着手行动，把那次会面安排下了。”
阿尔伯特不由自主地起了敬畏。他心头沉甸甸地压着一种滋味：自己是在同一位高出一头的智力过招。

===Original===
“Yes, you did!” he managed to say with the proper note of incredulity, but in his heart he was not incredulous. Dimly, Albert had begun to perceive that years must elapse before he could become capable of matching himself in battles of wits with this master-strategist.
“Yes, I certainly did!” said Keggs. “I don’t know what ’appened at the interview﻿—not being present in person. But I’ve no doubt that everything proceeded satisfactorily.”
“And a fat lot of good that’s going to do you, when ’e ain’t allowed to come inside the ’ouse!”
A bland smile irradiated the butler’s moon-like face.

===Chinese===
“你还真安排了？！”他总算挤出这么一句，配上恰到好处的怀疑腔调；心里头却半点也不疑。隐隐约约地，阿尔伯特已经开始明白：还得再熬上好几个年头，他才配得上同这位军师爷在斗智的战场上一较短长。
“不错，我的确安排了！”凯格斯说。“会面上谈了些什么，我不知道——本人没有在场。可我毫不怀疑，一切进行得称心如意。”
“那对你顶个屁用——反正不许他进这个宅子的门！”
一团和气的笑容，照亮了管家那张月亮似的圆脸。

===Original===
“If by ’e you’re alloodin’ to Mr. Bevan, young blighted Albert, let me tell you that it won’t be long before ’e becomes a regular duly invited guest at the castle!”
“A lot of chance!”
“Would you care to ’ave another five shillings even money on it?”
Albert recoiled. He had had enough of speculation where the butler was concerned. Where that schemer was allowed to get within reach of it, hard cash melted away.

===Chinese===
“倘若你暗指（alloodin’）的是贝文先生，小倒霉蛋阿尔伯特，那我告诉你：要不了多久，他就会成为城堡里正正经经、持帖受邀的客人啦！”
“机会可大着呢！”
“再来五先令，一赔一，你敢不敢押？”
阿尔伯特连连后缩。凡同这位管家沾边儿的投机买卖，他已经领教够了。凡是让那位密谋家伸手够得着的地方，现钱都要化成水。

===Original===
“What are you going to do?”
“Never you mind what I’m going to do. I ’ave my methods. All I ’ave to say to you is that tomorrow or the day after Mr. Bevan will be seated in our dining-’all with ’is feet under our table, replying according to his personal taste and preference, when I ask ’im if ’e’ll ’ave ’ock or sherry. Brush all them crumbs carefully off the tablecloth, young blighted Albert﻿—don’t shuffle your feet﻿—breathe softly through your nose﻿—and close the door be’ind you when you’ve finished!”
“Oh, go and eat cake!” said Albert bitterly. But he said it to his immortal soul, not aloud. The lad’s spirit was broken.

===Chinese===
“那你打算怎么办？”
“我打算怎么办，不用你操心。我有我的法子。我只有一句话奉告：明天也好，后天也好，贝文先生就要坐在咱们的大饭厅里，两只脚伸在咱们的桌子底下；等我问他要霍克酒（hock）还是雪利酒的时候，他就好按他个人的口味偏好作答了。桌布上的面包屑，给我仔细刷干净，小倒霉蛋阿尔伯特——别蹭着脚走——拿鼻子轻轻出气——干完了，随手把门带上！”
“哦，去吃你的蛋糕去吧！”阿尔伯特狠狠地说。可这话他只说给自己不朽的灵魂听，没有出声。这孩子的气性，已经给彻底压垮了。

---

===Original===
Keggs, the processes of digestion completed, presented himself before Lord Belpher in the billiard-room. Percy was alone. The house-party, so numerous on the night of the ball and on his birthday, had melted down now to reasonable proportions. The second and third cousins had retired, flushed and gratified, to obscure dens from which they had emerged, and the castle housed only the more prominent members of the family, always harder to dislodge than the small fry. The Bishop still remained, and the Colonel. Besides these, there were perhaps half a dozen more of the closer relations: to Lord Belpher’s way of thinking, half a dozen too many. He was not fond of his family.
“Might I have a word with your lordship?”
“What is it, Keggs?”
Keggs was a self-possessed man, but he found it a little hard to begin. Then he remembered that once in the misty past he had seen Lord Belpher spanked for stealing jam, he himself having acted on that occasion as prosecuting attorney; and the memory nerved him.

===Chinese===
消化的工序进行完毕，凯格斯便来到台球室，谒见贝尔弗勋爵。珀西独自一人。舞会之夜和他生日那天多到不可胜数的宾客，如今已经消融到说得过去的规模。那些隔房的表亲堂亲，红光满面、心满意足地退回了他们当初从中钻出来的那些无名巢穴；城堡里住下的，只有家族里比较显赫的人物——这类人向来比小鱼小虾更难撵走。主教还没有走，上校也没有走。除他们以外，另有六位上下的近亲：照贝尔弗勋爵的想法，是整整多了六位。他不喜欢他的家人。
“可以跟勋爵大人说句话吗？”
“什么事，凯格斯？”
凯格斯是个镇定自若的人，可他觉得起头有点儿难。后来他记起，渺渺茫茫的往昔有一回，他亲眼见过贝尔弗勋爵因为偷果酱挨打屁股，那一回充任检察官的正是他本人；这段回忆给他壮了胆。

===Original===
“I earnestly ’ope that your lordship will not think that I am taking a liberty. I ’ave been in his lordship your father’s service many years now, and the family honour is, if I may be pardoned for saying so, extremely near my ’eart. I ’ave known your lordship since you were a mere boy, and﻿ ﻿…”
Lord Belpher had listened with growing impatience to this preamble. His temper was seldom at its best these days, and the rolling periods annoyed him.
“Yes, yes, of course,” he said. “What is it?”
Keggs was himself now. In his opening remarks he had simply been, as it were, winding up. He was now prepared to begin.

===Chinese===
“在下恳切地盼望，勋爵大人不要以为在下斗胆放肆。在下伺候勋爵大人——也就是您父亲大人——到如今已有多年；家族的名誉，恕在下斗胆一言，实在是贴在心尖儿上。打您还是个小小男孩的时候起，在下就认得勋爵大人了，而且……”
这段开场白，贝尔弗勋爵越听越不耐烦。这些天他的脾气难得有好时候，这种滔滔滚滚的长句子叫他冒火。
“是，是，当然。”他说，“什么事？”
这会儿，凯格斯恢复了本来面目。方才那几句开场白，不过是——可以这么说——在上发条。现在，他才准备真正开始。

===Original===
“Your lordship will recall inquiring of me on the night of the ball as to the *bona fides* of one of the temporary waiters? The one that stated that ’e was the cousin of young bli﻿—of the boy Albert, the page? I have been making inquiries, your lordship, and I regret to say I find that the man was a impostor. He informed me that ’e was Albert’s cousin, but Albert now informs me that ’e ’as no cousin in America. I am extremely sorry this should have occurred, your lordship, and I ’ope you will attribute it to the bustle and haste inseparable from duties as mine on such a occasion.”
“I know the fellow was an impostor. He was probably after the spoons!”
Keggs coughed.
“If I might be allowed to take a further liberty, your lordship, might I suggest that I am aware of the man’s identity and of his motive for visiting the castle.”

===Chinese===
“舞会那天晚上，勋爵大人想必记得曾向在下打听一位临时侍者来历的真假（bona fides）？就是那个自称是小倒——是那位当小堂馆的孩子阿尔伯特的表哥的人？在下作了些查访，勋爵大人，很遗憾地发现，那人是个冒牌货。他向在下声称，他是阿尔伯特的表哥；可阿尔伯特如今向在下声明，他在美国根本没有表哥。出了这档子事，在下万分抱歉，勋爵大人，还望您把这归因于：逢这样的大场面，干在下这份差事的，手忙脚乱在所难免。”
“我知道那家伙是个冒牌货。他八成是惦记那些银汤匙来的！”
凯格斯咳了一声。
“倘蒙勋爵大人容在下再放肆一步，可否容在下进言：那人的身份，连同他光临城堡的动机，在下是知晓的。”

===Original===
He waited a little apprehensively. This was the crucial point in the interview. If Lord Belpher did not now freeze him with a glance and order him from the room, the danger would be past, and he could speak freely. His light blue eyes were expressionless as they met Percy’s, but inwardly he was feeling much the same sensation as he was wont to experience when the family was in town and he had managed to slip off to Kempton Park or some other racecourse and put some of his savings on a horse. As he felt when the racing steeds thundered down the straight, so did he feel now.
Astonishment showed in Lord Belpher’s round face. Just as it was about to be succeeded by indignation, the butler spoke again.
“I am aware, your lordship, that it is not my place to offer suggestions as to the private and intimate affairs of the family I ’ave the honour to serve, but, if your lordship would consent to overlook the liberty, I think I could be of ’elp and assistance in a matter which is causing annoyance and unpleasantness to all.”
He invigorated himself with another dip into the waters of memory. Yes. The young man before him might be Lord Belpher, son of his employer and heir to all these great estates, but once he had seen him spanked.

===Chinese===
他惴惴地等了一等。这是整场谈话的紧要关头。倘若贝尔弗勋爵此刻不拿一眼把他冻僵、喝令他滚出房去，险关就算闯过了，他便能放胆直陈。他那双浅蓝色的眼睛迎着珀西的注视，毫无表情；心里头呢，那股滋味，同他惯常体验的一模一样——全家在伦敦的时节，他设法溜到肯普顿公园（Kempton Park）或别的哪个赛马场，从积蓄里抠出几个钱押在一匹马身上，便是那股滋味。当年赛马隆隆冲下直道时他心里是什么滋味，这会儿就是什么滋味。
贝尔弗勋爵的圆脸上露出了惊愕。那惊愕正要换成恼怒，管家又开了口。
“在下知道，勋爵大人，对在下有幸伺候的这一家的私事内务指手画脚，不是在下的本分；可是倘使勋爵大人肯宽恕这份放肆，在下想，在一件叫人人烦恼扫兴的事情上，在下是能帮上忙、出上力的。”
他又往记忆的水里浸了一浸，给自己鼓了鼓劲。是的。眼前这个青年，或许是贝尔弗勋爵，是他东家的公子、这一片偌大家业的继承人；可他见过他挨打屁股。

===Original===
Perhaps Percy also remembered this. Perhaps he merely felt that Keggs was a faithful old servant and, as such, entitled to thrust himself into the family affairs. Whatever his reasons, he now definitely lowered the barrier.
“Well,” he said, with a glance at the door to make sure that there were no witnesses to an act of which the aristocrat in him disapproved, “go on!”
Keggs breathed freely. The danger-point was past.
“ ’Aving a natural interest, your lordship,” he said, “we of the Servants’ ’All generally manage to become respectfully aware of whatever ’appens to be transpirin’ above stairs. May I say that I became acquainted at an early stage with the trouble which your lordship is unfortunately ’aving with a certain party?”

===Chinese===
也许珀西也记得这一节。也许他只是觉得，凯格斯是个忠心耿耿的老仆人；忠心耿耿的老仆人，自然有资格插手家务。无论出于什么缘故，这会儿他确确实实放下了栅栏。
“说吧，”他说，朝门那边扫了一眼，核实没有见证人目睹这桩他骨子里的贵族做派深以为耻的行径，“接着说！”
凯格斯畅快地吸了口气。险点已经过了。
“出于天生的兴趣，勋爵大人，”他说，“我们仆役大厅里的人，对楼上发生的无论什么事情，一般总能恭而敬之地有所风闻。恕在下进言：勋爵大人不幸同某位人物之间的那桩麻烦，在下在很早的阶段就已有所闻了。”

===Original===
Lord Belpher, although his whole being revolted against what practically amounted to hobnobbing with a butler, perceived that he had committed himself to the discussion. It revolted him to think that these delicate family secrets were the subject of conversation in menial circles, but it was too late to do anything now. And such was the wholeheartedness with which he had declared war upon George Bevan that, at this stage in the proceedings, his chief emotion was a hope that Keggs might have something sensible to suggest.
“I think, begging your lordship’s pardon for making the remark, that you are acting injudicious. I ’ave been in service a great number of years, startin’ as steward’s room boy and rising to my present position, and I may say I ’ave ’ad experience during those years of several cases where the daughter or son of the ’ouse contemplated a misalliance, and all but one of the cases ended disastrously, your lordship, on account of the family trying opposition. It is my experience that opposition in matters of the ’eart is useless, feedin’, as it, so to speak, does the flame. Young people, your lordship, if I may be pardoned for employing the expression in the present case, are naturally romantic and if you keep ’em away from a thing they sit and pity themselves and want it all the more. And in the end you may be sure they get it. There’s no way of stoppin’ them. I was not on sufficiently easy terms with the late Lord Worlingham to give ’im the benefit of my experience on the occasion when the Honourable Aubrey Pershore fell in love with the young person at the Gaiety Theatre. Otherwise I could ’ave told ’im he was not acting judicious. His lordship opposed the match in every way, and the young couple ran off and got married at a registrar’s. It was the same when a young man who was tutor to ’er ladyship’s brother attracted Lady Evelyn Walls, the only daughter of the Earl of Ackleton. In fact, your lordship, the only entanglement of the kind that came to a satisfactory conclusion in the whole of my personal experience was the affair of Lady Catherine Duseby, Lord Bridgefield’s daughter, who injudiciously became infatuated with a roller-skating instructor.”
Lord Belpher had ceased to feel distantly superior to his companion. The butler’s powerful personality hypnotized him. Long ere the harangue was ended, he was as a little child drinking in the utterances of a master. He bent forward eagerly. Keggs had broken off his remarks at the most interesting point.

===Chinese===
贝尔弗勋爵虽然打心底里反感这近乎同管家称兄道弟的行径，却也看得分明：自己已经搭进了这场谈话，脱不了身。一想到这些微妙的家务秘密竟成了奴仆圈子里的谈资，他胃里就翻腾；可事到如今，做什么都晚了。况且他向乔治·贝文宣战本是全心全意；事到这一步，他的头号心思，已经变成了巴望凯格斯真能拿出点儿像样的主意来。
“恕在下斗胆直言，勋爵大人：在下的看法是，您眼下的做法失于欠斟酌。在下当差，年头为数甚多，从管事餐室的小伙计（steward’s room boy）一路升到今日的位置；这些年里，在下见识过好几桩案子，府上的小姐或少爷动了错配姻缘（misalliance）的念头，除了一桩，桩桩以灾难收场，勋爵大人，缘故就在家里人顶着干。凭在下的经验，在感情的事情上顶着干毫无用处，反倒——可以这么说——给火苗添柴。年轻人，勋爵大人，容在下在眼下这桩事上斗胆用一用这个字眼——天性浪漫；您越不叫他们沾一样东西，他们越坐在那里自怜自叹，越想得到它。末了您放心，他们准能得到。拦，是拦不住的。已故的沃灵厄姆勋爵（Lord Worlingham），在下同他交情还不够深，赶不上在尊贵的奥布里·珀肖阁下（the Honourable Aubrey Pershore）爱上盖蒂剧院（the Gaiety Theatre）里一位年轻女子的时候，把在下的这份经验贡献给他；不然，在下本可以告诉他：您这样做失于欠斟酌。那位勋爵处处拦这桩婚事，那一对年轻人就跑了，跑到结婚登记处成了亲。还有一回也是这样：一位给小姐大人的哥哥当家庭教师的青年，招得阿克尔顿伯爵（the Earl of Ackleton）的独生女伊夫琳·沃尔斯女士（Lady Evelyn Walls）动了心。说实在的，勋爵大人，凭在下亲眼所见，这类纠葛里头唯一收场圆满的，是凯瑟琳·迪斯比女士（Lady Catherine Duseby）那桩——布里奇菲尔德勋爵（Lord Bridgefield）的千金一时糊涂，迷恋上了一位轮滑教练（roller-skating instructor）。”
贝尔弗勋爵早已不再觉得自己比对面这位高出一头、隔着一层了。管家那股慑人的气派把他催眠了。这场宏论还没完，他已经像个小孩子，如饥似渴地啜饮着大师的言语。他急切地把身子凑上前去。凯格斯偏偏在最有味的地方把话头掐断了。

===Original===
“What happened?” inquired Percy.
“The young man,” proceeded Keggs, “was a young man of considerable personal attractions, ’aving large brown eyes and a athletic lissome figure, brought about by roller-skating. It was no wonder, in the opinion of the Servants’ ’All, that ’er ladyship should have found ’erself fascinated by him, particularly as I myself ’ad ’eard her observe at a full luncheon-table that roller-skating was in her opinion the only thing except her toy Pomeranian that made life worth living. But when she announced that she had become engaged to this young man, there was the greatest consternation. I was not, of course, privileged to be a participant at the many councils and discussions that ensued and took place, but I was aware that such transpired with great frequency. Eventually ’is lordship took the shrewd step of assuming acquiescence and inviting the young man to visit us in Scotland. And within ten days of his arrival, your lordship, the match was broken off. He went back to ’is roller-skating, and ’er ladyship took up visiting the poor and eventually contracted an altogether suitable alliance by marrying Lord Ronald Spofforth, the second son of his Grace the Duke of Gorbals and Strathbungo.”
“How did it happen?”
“Seein’ the young man in the surroundings of ’er own ’ome, ’er ladyship soon began to see that she had taken too romantic a view of ’im previous, your lordship. ’E was one of the lower middle class, what is sometimes termed the bourjoisy, and ’is ’abits were not the ’abits of the class to which ’er ladyship belonged. ’E ’ad nothing in common with the rest of the ’ouse-party, and was injudicious in ’is choice of forks. The very first night at dinner ’e took a steel knife to the ontray, and I see ’er ladyship look at him very sharp, as much as to say that scales had fallen from ’er eyes. It didn’t take ’er long after that to become convinced that ’er ’eart ’ad led ’er astray.”

===Chinese===
“后来怎么样了？”珀西问道。
“那位青年，”凯格斯接着说，“是个仪表颇为动人的青年：一双棕色的大眼睛，一副轻健利落的身段——轮滑（roller-skating）轮出来的。在仆役大厅看来，小姐大人叫他迷住，丝毫不奇怪；何况在下亲耳听见她在满桌午宴上说过：依她看，这世上除了她那只玩具博美犬（toy Pomeranian），就数轮滑能让人生值得活下去。可是等她宣布同这位青年订了婚，府上大为惊骇。那以后接连举行的一次次密议商谈，在下自然没有资格躬与其役；可这类聚会开得极其频繁，在下是知道的。末了，勋爵大人使出一记妙招：假装默许，邀那位青年到苏格兰我们家里做客。不出他上门十天，勋爵大人，这桩婚事就吹了。他回去接着滑他的轮滑，小姐大人则做起探访贫民的功课，末了缔结了一门再合式不过的姻缘——嫁给了罗纳德·斯波弗思勋爵（Lord Ronald Spofforth），戈尔巴斯与斯特拉斯邦戈公爵大人（his Grace the Duke of Gorbals and Strathbungo）的二公子。”
“这是怎么成功的？”
“在自己家里的环境里天天看这位青年，小姐大人很快就看出：先前她把他看得太浪漫了，勋爵大人。他是下等中产阶级出身，就是人家有时候叫作‘布尔若西’（bourjoisy）的那一路；他的习惯做派，同小姐大人所属的那个阶级全不是一路。他同宾客里旁的人格格不入，用起叉子来又欠斟酌。头一晚正餐，上到主菜（ontray）的时候，他抓了把钢刀；在下亲眼看见小姐大人狠狠剜了他一眼，那意思分明是说：她眼上的鳞片，就此剥落了。打那以后，没用多久，她就信了：是她的心把她领上了歧路。”

===Original===
“Then you think﻿—?”
“It is not for me to presume to offer anything but the most respectful advice, your lordship, but I should most certainly advocate a similar procedure in the present instance.”
Lord Belpher reflected. Recent events had brought home to him the magnitude of the task he had assumed when he had appointed himself the watcher of his sister’s movements. The affair of the curate and the village blacksmith had shaken him both physically and spiritually. His feet were still sore, and his confidence in himself had waned considerably. The thought of having to continue his espionage indefinitely was not a pleasant one. How much simpler and more effective it would be to adopt the suggestion which had been offered to him.
“﻿—I’m not sure you aren’t right, Keggs.”

===Chinese===
“那么您认为——？”
“除了最恭敬的忠告，在下不敢妄言别的，勋爵大人；可眼下这一桩，在下最坚决地主张照方抓药。”
贝尔弗勋爵沉吟起来。近来的种种事情，叫他痛切地掂出了自己揽下的这副担子的分量——自封为妹妹行踪的监视人，是他自己讨来的差事。副牧师和乡村铁匠的那档子事，把他的身体和心灵都震了个透。他的两只脚至今还疼着，自信心也消退了大半。一想到这场侦查要没完没了地继续下去，他心里就不是滋味。采纳人家送上门来的这条计策，该有多么省事，多么有效。
“——我不敢说你不对，凯格斯。”

===Original===
“Thank you, your lordship. I feel convinced of it.”
“I will speak to my father tonight.”
“Very good, your lordship. I am glad to have been of service.”

===Chinese===
“谢谢勋爵大人。在下深信如此。”
“我今晚就去同父亲谈。”
“遵命，勋爵大人。能效点儿劳，在下很高兴。”

---

===Original===
“Young blighted Albert,” said Keggs crisply, shortly after breakfast on the following morning, “you’re to take this note to Mr. Bevan at the cottage down by Platt’s farm, and you’re to deliver it without playing any of your monkey-tricks, and you’re to wait for an answer, and you’re to bring that answer back to me, too, and to Lord Marshmoreton. And I may tell you, to save you the trouble of opening it with steam from the kitchen kettle, that I ’ave already done so. It’s an invitation to dine with us tonight. So now you know. Look slippy!”
Albert capitulated. For the first time in his life he felt humble. He perceived how misguided he had been ever to suppose that he could pit his pygmy wits against this smooth-faced worker of wonders.
“Crikey!” he ejaculated.

===Chinese===
“小倒霉蛋阿尔伯特，”第二天早晨早点刚过，凯格斯干脆利落地说，“你把这张便条送到普拉特家农场隔壁那座小屋，交给贝文先生；送到便罢，不许耍你那套猴把戏；你等着讨回信；回信取回来，先交我，再交马什莫顿勋爵。省得你费事拿厨房的开水壶汽蒸拆信——我可以告诉你，信我已经拆过了。是请他今晚来我们家吃饭的帖子。这下你明白了。麻利点！”
阿尔伯特投降了。平生头一遭，他觉出了自己的卑微。他明白过来：自己居然妄想拿那点侏儒的才智，去同这位面皮光滑的创造奇迹的高手较量，一向是误入了怎样的歧途。
“老天爷！”他迸出这么一句。

===Original===
It was all that he could say.
“And there’s one more thing, young feller me lad,” added Keggs earnestly, “don’t you ever grow up to be such a fat’ead as our friend Percy. Don’t forget I warned you.”

===Chinese===
他所能说出的，尽在于此。
“还有一句话，我的小伙子，”凯格斯恳切地补上，“往后长大了，千万可别长成咱们那位珀西朋友那样的大胖笨脑瓜。别说我没有警告过你。”

## XXIII / XXIII

===Original===
Life is like some crazy machine that is always going either too slow or too fast. From the cradle to the grave we alternate between the Sargasso Sea and the rapids﻿—forever either becalmed or storm-tossed. It seemed to Maud, as she looked across the dinner-table in order to make sure for the twentieth time that it really was George Bevan who sat opposite her, that, after months in which nothing whatever had happened, she was now living through a period when everything was happening at once. Life, from being a broken-down machine, had suddenly begun to race.

To the orderly routine that stretched back to the time when she had been hurried home in disgrace from Wales there had succeeded a mad whirl of events, to which the miracle of tonight had come as a fitting climax. She had not begun to dress for dinner till somewhat late, and had consequently entered the drawing-room just as Keggs was announcing that the meal was ready. She had received her first shock when the lovesick Plummer, emerging from a mixed crowd of relatives and friends, had informed her that he was to take her in. She had not expected Plummer to be there, though he lived in the neighbourhood. Plummer, at their last meeting, had stated his intention of going abroad for a bit to mend his bruised heart: and it was a little disconcerting to a sensitive girl to find her victim popping up again like this. She did not know that, as far as Plummer was concerned, the whole affair was to be considered opened again. To Plummer, analysing the girl’s motives in refusing him, there had come the idea that there was Another, and that this other must be Reggie Byng. From the first he had always looked upon Reggie as his worst rival. And now Reggie had bolted with the Faraday girl, leaving Maud in excellent condition, so it seemed to Plummer, to console herself with a worthier man. Plummer knew all about the Rebound and the part it plays in the affairs of the heart. His own breach-of-promise case two years earlier had been entirely due to the fact that the refusal of the youngest Devenish girl to marry him had caused him to rebound into the dangerous society of the second girl from the O.P. end of the first row in the “Summertime is Kissing-time” number in the Alhambra revue. He had come to the castle tonight gloomy, but not without hope.

Maud’s second shock eclipsed the first entirely. No notification had been given to her either by her father or by Percy of the proposed extension of the hand of hospitality to George, and the sight of him standing there talking to her aunt Caroline made her momentarily dizzy. Life, which for several days had had all the properties now of a dream, now of a nightmare, became more unreal than ever. She could conceive no explanation of George’s presence. He could not be there﻿—that was all there was to it; yet there undoubtedly he was. Her manner, as she accompanied Plummer down the stairs, took on such a dazed sweetness that her escort felt that in coming there that night he had done the wisest act of a lifetime studded but sparsely with wise acts. It seemed to Plummer that this girl had softened towards him. Certainly something had changed her. He could not know that she was merely wondering if she was awake.

===Chinese===
生活就像一台失了常的机器，不是转得太慢，就是转得太快。从摇篮到坟墓，我们始终在马尾藻海（Sargasso Sea）与湍滩之间来回打转——不是风平船滞，便是惊涛骇浪。在莫德看来，前几个月里分明是什么也不曾发生，如今她却活在一个万事齐发的时段里；她隔着餐桌望过去，已经是第二十次核实坐在对面的确实是乔治·贝文。生活这台抛了锚的机器，忽然之间疯跑起来。

自从当初她灰头土脸地被人从威尔士催回家，日子一向过得井井有条；如今接续上来的，是一场疯狂的变故旋风，而今晚的奇迹恰好凑成了一记压轴的高潮。她动手换晚装本来就不算早，因而走进客厅的时候，凯格斯正在唱名开席。头一桩惊吓来自那位害着相思的普卢默：他从一群七拼八凑的亲戚朋友中间挤出来，通知她说，将由他伴她入席。她没有料到普卢默会到场，虽然他就住在左近。两人上回见面的时候，普卢默还声明要出国晃荡些日子，疗一疗那颗受伤的心；一个心思细密的姑娘，猛然发现自己的手下败将又这样冒了出来，多少有点发窘。她无从知道，在普卢默那一头，这桩事情已经算作重新开张。普卢默把姑娘拒绝自己的动机掰开揉碎地分析了一遍，脑子里冒出一个念头：另有一人，而这人必定是雷吉·宾。打从第一天起，他一向把雷吉看作最危险的情敌。如今雷吉带着法拉第家的姑娘私奔了，留下莫德无牵无挂——照普卢默看来——正可以找个更配得上的人来宽慰自己。感情的反弹（the Rebound）在心头大事里扮演什么角色，普卢默一清二楚。他两年前那桩毁弃婚约案，起因恰恰在于：迪夫尼什（Devenish）家的小女儿回绝了他的求婚，害他一头反弹进危险人物堆里——阿罕布拉剧场（the Alhambra）那台轻歌舞剧里《夏天就是接吻的时节》（“Summertime is Kissing-time”）的歌舞节目中，坐在第一排靠 O.P.（对提词侧）那端的二号姑娘。今晚他到城堡来的时候，愁眉苦脸，倒也不是全无指望。

莫德的第二桩惊吓把头一桩比得无影无踪。父亲也罢，珀西也罢，谁也没有对她打个招呼，说城堡打算把好客之手伸向乔治；一眼望见他站在那儿同姑母卡罗琳说话，她登时觉得天旋地转。这几天，生活忽而处处像一场梦，忽而处处像一场噩梦，此刻愈发真假难辨了。乔治怎么会在这里，她无论怎样也想不出个道理。他不可能在这里——事情就是这样；可他偏偏确确实实在这里。她由普卢默陪着下楼的时候，神情恍惚而甜柔，叫她这位护花人暗自觉得：今晚到这儿来，是他一辈子里寥寥可数的聪明举动当中最聪明的一桩。照普卢默看，这姑娘对他软下来了。不消说，是什么事情改变了她。他没法知道，她只是在琢磨自己究竟醒着没有。

===Original===
George, meanwhile, across the table, was also having a little difficulty in adjusting his faculties to the progress of events. He had given up trying to imagine why he had been invited to this dinner, and was now endeavouring to find some theory which would square with the fact of Billie Dore being at the castle. At precisely this hour Billie, by rights, should have been putting the finishing touches on her makeup in a second-floor dressing-room at the Regal. Yet there she sat, very much at her ease in this aristocratic company, so quietly and unobtrusively dressed in some black stuff that at first he had scarcely recognized her. She was talking to the Bishop﻿ ﻿…

The voice of Keggs at his elbow broke in on his reverie.

“Sherry or ’ock, sir?”

===Chinese===
与此同时，桌子那头的乔治，也一样有点儿回不过神来，脑瓜跟不上事态的进展。他已经不再费神去想自己凭什么受邀赴宴，眼下正努力找一套说法，好解释比莉·多尔怎么会在城堡里。这个钟点，按理说，比莉该在雷加尔剧院二楼一间化妆室里给妆容收尾才对。可她就坐在那儿，在这一屋子贵族中间安然自得，一身黑料子衣裳穿得那么素净、毫不招摇，起初他差点儿没认出来。她正在同主教攀谈……

凯格斯的声音在肘边响起，把他的出神打断了。

“雪莉酒还是霍克酒（hock），先生？”

===Original===
George could not have explained why this reminder of the butler’s presence should have made him feel better, but it did. There was something solid and tranquilizing about Keggs. He had noticed it before. For the first time the sensation of having been smitten over the head with some blunt instrument began to abate. It was as if Keggs by the mere intonation of his voice had said, “All this no doubt seems very strange and unusual to you, but feel no alarm! I am here!”

George began to sit up and take notice. A cloud seemed to have cleared from his brain. He found himself looking on his fellow-diners as individuals rather than as a confused mass. The prophet Daniel, after the initial embarrassment of finding himself in the society of the lions had passed away, must have experienced a somewhat similar sensation.

He began to sort these people out and label them. There had been introductions in the drawing-room, but they had left him with a bewildered sense of having heard somebody recite a page from Burke’s peerage. Not since that day in the free library in London, when he had dived into that fascinating volume in order to discover Maud’s identity, had he undergone such a rain of titles. He now took stock, to ascertain how many of these people he could identify.

===Chinese===
乔治说不上来，为什么这句提醒他管家就在跟前的话，竟叫他觉着好受了些；可事实如此。凯格斯身上有种四平八稳、叫人心定的东西。这一点他从前就留意到。后脑勺挨了什么钝器一记闷击的那种感觉，头一回开始消退。仿佛凯格斯单凭说话的调门就道出了这么一句：“这一切在您想必又古怪又反常，可是不必惊慌！有我在这儿！”

乔治开始打起精神，留神四顾。脑子里像有一团云雾散开了。他发现自己看同桌的食客，已经能一位是一位，不再是一团模糊的人堆。先知但以理在狮子坑里熬过了头一阵窘迫之后，想必也体验过大同小异的心境。

他着手把这些人一个个分拣出来，贴上标签。客厅里确实有人替他们引见过，可那些介绍只留给他一种晕头转向的错觉，仿佛听人背诵了一页《伯克贵族名鉴》。自从那天在伦敦的免费图书馆里，他一头扎进那本迷人的书去查莫德的底细以来，头衔的倾盆大雨他还没淋过第二回。这会儿他开始清点，看看这些人里头有几个是他认得准的。

===Original===
The stocktaking was an absolute failure. Of all those present the only individuals he could swear to were his own personal little playmates with whom he had sported in other surroundings. There was Lord Belpher, for instance, eyeing him with a hostility that could hardly be called veiled. There was Lord Marshmoreton at the head of the table, listening glumly to the conversation of a stout woman with a pearl necklace, but who was that woman? Was it Lady Jane Allenby or Lady Edith Wade-Beverly or Lady Patricia Fowles? And who, above all, was the pie-faced fellow with the moustache talking to Maud?

He sought assistance from the girl he had taken in to dinner. She appeared, as far as he could ascertain from a short acquaintance, to be an amiable little thing. She was small and young and fluffy, and he had caught enough of her name at the moment of introduction to gather that she was plain “Miss” Something﻿—a fact which seemed to him to draw them together.

“I wish you would tell me who some of these people are,” he said, as she turned from talking to the man on her other-side. “Who is the man over there?”

===Chinese===
清点的结果是一败涂地。满座宾客里，他敢拍胸脯担保的，只有他自家那几个小玩伴——在别的场合同他嬉闹过的那几位。比方说贝尔弗勋爵，正拿一种说‘掩藏’都算客气的敌意瞪着他。再比方说坐在主位的马什莫顿勋爵，正愁眉苦脸地听一位戴珍珠项链的胖夫人说话——可那位夫人是谁？是简·艾伦比夫人（Lady Jane Allenby）、伊迪丝·韦德-贝弗利夫人（Lady Edith Wade-Beverly），还是帕特里夏·福尔斯夫人（Lady Patricia Fowles）？最要紧的是，跟莫德说话的那个蓄着小胡子的大饼脸家伙，又是谁？

他向伴他入席的姑娘讨救兵。就初识这短短一刻判断，这姑娘像是个和和气气的小东西。她娇小、年轻、蓬松可爱；引见的那一瞬，她的姓名他总算逮住了个尾巴，知道她是位普普通通的某某‘小姐’——这个事实在他看来一下子把两人拉近了。

“劳驾你给我讲讲，这儿这些人都是谁，”趁她同另一侧的先生谈完话转过头来，他开口道，“那边那位先生是谁？”

===Original===
“Which man?”

“The one talking to Lady Maud. The fellow whose face ought to be shuffled and dealt again.”

“That’s my brother.”

That held George during the soup.

===Chinese===
“哪位先生？”

“跟莫德小姐说话的那位。那张脸理应收回去重新洗牌、再发一副的那家伙。”

“那是我哥哥。”

这句话够乔治就着汤消化一阵的了。

===Original===
“I’m sorry about your brother,” he said rallying with the fish.

“That’s very sweet of you.”

“It was the light that deceived me. Now that I look again, I see that his face has great charm.”

The girl giggled. George began to feel better.

===Chinese===
“你哥哥的事，我很难过，”吃到鱼的时候，他缓过劲来说。

“你嘴真甜。”

“刚才是灯光骗了我。如今再细看，我发现他这张脸极有魅力。”

姑娘咯咯笑了。乔治开始觉得好受些了。

===Original===
“Who are some of the others? I didn’t get your name, for instance. They shot it at me so quick that it had whizzed by before I could catch it.”

“My name is Plummer.”

George was electrified. He looked across the table with more vivid interest. The amorous Plummer had been just a Voice to him till now. It was exciting to see him in the flesh.

===Chinese===
“那其余这些人都是谁？就说你吧，我都没听清你的名字。他们把名字朝我甩过来，快得我还没来得及伸手，它已经嗖地飞过去了。”

“我姓普卢默。”

乔治像触了电。他隔着桌子望过去，兴致一下子浓了起来。害相思的普卢默，到此刻为止在他听来不过是一个声音。能见到他本人，可真够带劲。

===Original===
“And who are the rest of them?”

“They are all members of the family. I thought you knew them.”

“I know Lord Marshmoreton. And Lady Maud. And, of course, Lord Belpher.” He caught Percy’s eye as it surveyed him coldly from the other side of the table, and nodded cheerfully. “Great pal of mine, Lord Belpher.”

The fluffy Miss Plummer twisted her pretty face into a grimace of disapproval.

===Chinese===
“那其余这些人呢？”

“都是自家人呀。我还当你认识呢。”

“我认识马什莫顿勋爵。还有莫德小姐。还有，当然啦，贝尔弗勋爵。”珀西的目光正从桌子那头冷冷地扫过来，叫他逮个正着；他乐呵呵地点了点头。“贝尔弗勋爵是我的老铁哥们儿。”

娇小蓬松的普卢默小姐把那张漂亮脸蛋扭成一团不以为然的怪相。

===Original===
“I don’t like Percy.”

“No!”

“I think he’s conceited.”

“Surely not? What could he have to be conceited about?”

“He’s stiff.”

===Chinese===
“我不喜欢珀西。”

“是嘛！”

“我看他自大得很。”

“不会吧？他有什么可得意的呢？”

“他这人死板。”

===Original===
“Yes, of course, that’s how he strikes people at first. The first time I met him, I thought he was an awful stiff. But you should see him in his moments of relaxation. He’s one of those fellows you have to get to know. He grows on you.”

“Yes, but look at that affair with the policeman in London. Everybody in the county is talking about it.”

“Young blood!” sighed George. “Young blood! Of course, Percy is wild.”

“He must have been intoxicated.”

“Oh, undoubtedly,” said George.

===Chinese===
“对，没错，头一回见面，人人得的都是这个印象。我头一次见他，也当他是顶古板的老木头。可你该瞧瞧他放松下来的时候。他是那种你得处熟了才了解的人。越处越有味道。”

“话是这么说，可你瞧瞧他在伦敦跟那个警察闹的事。全郡的人都在讲它。”

“年少气盛啊！”乔治叹道，“年少气盛啊！珀西当然是个野性子。”

“他当时准是喝醉了。”

“哦，那还用说，”乔治说。

===Original===
Miss Plummer glanced across the table.

“Do look at Edwin!”

“Which is Edwin?”

“My brother, I mean. Look at the way he keeps staring at Maud. Edwin’s awfully in love with Maud,” she rattled on with engaging frankness. “At least, he thinks he is. He’s been in love with a different girl every season since I came out. And now that Reggie Byng has gone and married Alice Faraday, he thinks he has a chance. You heard about that, I suppose?”

===Chinese===
普卢默小姐朝桌子对面瞟了一眼。

“快瞧埃德温（Edwin）呀！”

“哪位是埃德温？”

“我是说我哥哥。你瞧他老盯着莫德瞧的那副样子。埃德温爱莫德爱得要命，”她一路爽爽快快、哔哔剥剥地说个不停。“至少，他自己当是这么回事。打从我走进社交界以来，他一个季度换一个姑娘地爱。如今雷吉·宾带着爱丽丝·法拉第一走了之、结了婚，他就觉得自己有戏了。这事儿你总听说过了吧？”

===Original===
“Yes, I did hear something about it.”

“Of course, Edwin’s wasting his time, really. I happen to know”﻿—Miss Plummer sank her voice to a whisper﻿—“I happen to know that Maud’s awfully in love with some man she met in Wales last year, but the family won’t hear of it.”

“Families are like that,” agreed George.

“Nobody knows who he is, but everybody in the county knows all about it. Those things get about, you know. Of course, it’s out of the question. Maud will have to marry somebody awfully rich or with a title. Her family’s one of the oldest in England, you know.”

===Chinese===
“是的，多少听说了一点。”

“说实在的，埃德温这是白费工夫。我碰巧知道”——普卢默小姐把嗓音压成了耳语——“我碰巧知道，莫德爱着去年在威尔士认识的什么男人，爱得不得了，可家里人死也不答应。”

“家家都是这样，”乔治表示同感。

“谁也不知道那男人是谁，可全郡的人把这件事知道得一清二楚。这种事传得快，你是知道的。当然啦，这是根本谈都不必谈的。莫德非得嫁一个顶有钱的、或者有头衔的不可。她家是英格兰最古老的门第之一，你知道吧。”

===Original===
“So I understand.”

“It isn’t as if she were the daughter of Lord Peebles, somebody like that.”

“Why Lord Peebles?”

“Well, what I mean to say is,” said Miss Plummer, with a silvery echo of Reggie Byng, “he made his money in whisky.”

===Chinese===
“我也有所耳闻。”

“她又不是皮布尔斯勋爵（Lord Peebles）那种人家的女儿。”

“怎么扯到皮布尔斯勋爵了？”

“呃，我的意思是说，”普卢默小姐道，银铃似的学了一句雷吉·宾的腔调，“他的钱是靠威士忌挣来的。”

===Original===
“That’s better than spending it that way,” argued George.

Miss Plummer looked puzzled. “I see what you mean,” she said a little vaguely. “Lord Marshmoreton is so different.”

“Haughty nobleman stuff, eh?”

“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“那也比那样把它喝掉强，”乔治辩道。

普卢默小姐一脸茫然。“我明白你的意思了，”她有点含糊地说，“马什莫顿勋爵就大不一样。”

“傲慢贵族那一套，是吧？”

“是。”

===Original===
“So you think this mysterious man in Wales hasn’t a chance?”

“Not unless he and Maud elope like Reggie Byng and Alice. Wasn’t that exciting? Who would ever have suspected Reggie had the dash to do a thing like that? Lord Marshmoreton’s new secretary is very pretty, don’t you think?”

“Which is she?”

“The girl in black with the golden hair.”

===Chinese===
“这么说，你认为威尔士那位神秘男子没有指望？”

“除非他跟莫德也学雷吉·宾和爱丽丝那样私奔，否则没门儿。那事儿多带劲？谁想得到雷吉竟有那股闯劲，干得出这种事？马什莫顿勋爵新来的秘书很漂亮，你不觉得吗？”

“哪一个是她？”

“穿黑衣裳、一头金发的那个姑娘。”

===Original===
“Is she Lord Marshmoreton’s secretary?”

“Yes. She’s an American girl. I think she’s much nicer than Alice Faraday. I was talking to her before dinner. Her name is Dore. Her father was a captain in the American army, who died without leaving her a penny. He was the younger son of a very distinguished family, but his family disowned him because he married against their wishes.”

“Something ought to be done to stop these families,” said George. “They’re always up to something.”

“So Miss Dore had to go out and earn her own living. It must have been awful for her, mustn’t it, having to give up society.”

===Chinese===
“她是马什莫顿勋爵的秘书？”

“是的。她是个美国姑娘。我觉得她比爱丽丝·法拉第可爱多了。饭前我跟她聊过。她姓多尔。她父亲在美国军队里当过上尉，去世的时候没给她留下一文钱。他出身一个极有名望的人家，却因为违背家里之意结了婚，叫家里给除了名。”

“该想个法子治治这些家庭了，”乔治说，“他们净闹出这种事来。”

“于是多尔小姐只好自己出去谋生啦。这对她一定可怕极了，你说是不是，连交际都得舍下。”

===Original===
“Did she give up society?”

“Oh, yes. She used to go everywhere in New York before her father died. I think American girls are wonderful. They have so much enterprise.”

George at the moment was thinking that it was in imagination that they excelled.

“I wish I could go out and earn my living,” said Miss Plummer. “But the family won’t dream of it.”

“The family again!” said George sympathetically. “They’re a perfect curse.”

===Chinese===
“她舍下交际了吗？”

“哦，那可不。她父亲去世以前，纽约的场子她哪儿都去。我觉得美国姑娘了不起。她们那么有闯劲。”

这会儿乔治心想，她们出类拔萃的本事，恐怕全在编故事上头。

“我真想也能出去自食其力，”普卢默小姐说，“可是家里人做梦也不会答应。”

“又是家里！”乔治深表同情地说，“这家人真是灾星。”

===Original===
“I want to go on the stage. Are you fond of the theatre?”

“Fairly.”

“I love it. Have you seen Hubert Broadleigh in *’Twas Once in Spring*?”

“I’m afraid I haven’t.”

“He’s wonderful. Have you seen Cynthia Dane in *A Woman’s No*?”

===Chinese===
“我想上台演戏。你喜欢看戏吗？”

“还喜欢。”

“我可爱了。休伯特·布罗德利（Hubert Broadleigh）演的《从前在春天》（’Twas Once in Spring）你看过没有？”

“恐怕没看过。”

“他演得好极了。辛西娅·戴恩（Cynthia Dane）的《女人说不》（A Woman’s No）看过吗？”

===Original===
“I missed that one too.”

“Perhaps you prefer musical pieces? I saw an awfully good musical comedy before I left town. It’s called *Follow the Girl*. It’s at the Regal Theatre. Have you seen it?”

“I wrote it.”

“You﻿—what!”

“That is to say, I wrote the music.”

===Chinese===
“那一出也错过了。”

“也许你更喜欢音乐剧？我动身出城以前看过一出好得不得了的音乐喜剧，叫《跟着姑娘走》，正在雷加尔剧院上演。你看过没有？”

“那出戏是我写的。”

“你——什么！”

“就是说，曲子是我写的。”

===Original===
“But the music’s lovely,” gasped little Miss Plummer, as if the fact made his claim ridiculous. “I’ve been humming it ever since.”

“I can’t help that. I still stick to it that I wrote it.”

“You aren’t George Bevan!”

“I am!”

“But﻿—” Miss Plummer’s voice almost failed here﻿—“But I’ve been dancing to your music for years! I’ve got about fifty of your records on the Victrola at home.”

===Chinese===
“可曲子那么好听呀，”小小的普卢默小姐倒抽一口气，仿佛这个事实偏偏叫他那句话显得可笑。“我打那以后一直哼到今天。”

“那我也没办法。反正我咬定了：曲子是我写的。”

“你不是乔治·贝文吧！”

“我就是！”

“可是——”普卢默小姐的声音到这里差点儿接不上来——“可是这些年我一直踩着你的曲子跳舞呀！我家里那台维克托拉留声机（Victrola）上，你的唱片差不多有五十张！”

===Original===
George blushed. However successful a man may be he can never get used to Fame at close range.

“Why, that tricky thing﻿—you know, in the second act﻿—is the darlingest thing I ever heard. I’m mad about it.”

“Do you mean the one that goes lumty-lumty-tum, tumty-tumty-tum?”

“No the one that goes ta-rumty-tum-tum, ta-rumty-tum. You know! The one about Granny dancing the shimmy.”

“I’m not responsible for the words, you know,” urged George hastily. “Those are wished on me by the lyrist.”

===Chinese===
乔治脸红了。一个人无论多么成功，也永远没法习惯凑到鼻尖底下的名声。

“哎哟，那支刁钻的小调子——你知道的，第二幕里那支——是我这辈子听过的最心爱的曲子。我迷死它了。”

“你是说那支哼起来伦姆蒂-伦姆蒂-塔姆、塔姆蒂-塔姆蒂-塔姆的？”

“不，是那支塔-伦姆蒂-塔姆-塔姆、塔-伦姆蒂-塔姆的。你知道嘛！就是唱奶奶跳希米舞（shimmy）的那支。”

“词儿可不归我管，你知道，”乔治赶忙声明，“那是写词的硬派给我的。”

===Original===
“I think the words are splendid. Although poor popper thinks its improper, Granny’s always doing it and nobody can stop her! I loved it.” Miss Plummer leaned forward excitedly. She was an impulsive girl. “Lady Caroline.”

Conversation stopped. Lady Caroline turned.

“Yes, Millie?”

“Did you know that Mr. Bevan was *the* Mr. Bevan?”

===Chinese===
“我觉得词儿棒极了。尽管可怜的老爸爸嫌它不成体统，奶奶照样跳她的，谁也拦不住！我爱煞了。”普卢默小姐兴奋得探过身子来。她是个想到就做的姑娘。“卡罗琳夫人。”

谈话声戛然而止。卡罗琳夫人转过脸来。

“什么事，米莉（Millie）？”

“您知道贝文先生就是‘那位’贝文先生吗？”

===Original===
Everybody was listening now. George huddled pinkly in his chair. He had not foreseen this ballyhooing. Shadrach, Meschach and Abednego combined had never felt a tithe of the warmth that consumed him. He was essentially a modest young man.

“*The* Mr. Bevan?” echoed Lady Caroline coldly. It was painful to her to have to recognize George’s existence on the same planet as herself. To admire him, as Miss Plummer apparently expected her to do, was a loathsome task. She cast one glance, fresh from the refrigerator, at the shrinking George, and elevated her aristocratic eyebrows.

Miss Plummer was not damped. She was at the hero-worshipping age, and George shared with the Messrs. Fairbanks, Francis X. Bushman, and one or two tennis champions an imposing pedestal in her Hall of Fame.

“You know! George Bevan, who wrote the music of *Follow the Girl*.”

===Chinese===
这下全桌都竖起了耳朵。乔治红着脸缩在椅子里。这场大张旗鼓他可没料到。沙得拉、米煞、亚伯尼歌（Shadrach, Meschach and Abednego）仨人捆作一堆，也从来没有领教过把他烧灼着的那份热度的十分之一。他骨子里是个腼腆的年轻人。

“‘那位’贝文先生？”卡罗琳夫人冷冷地重复了一遍。叫她不得不承认乔治同她共处一个星球，本就够她痛苦的；还要她像普卢默小姐显然盼着的那样去仰慕他，更是一桩令她作呕的差事。她朝缩成一团的乔治投去一瞥——那眼色才从冰箱里取出来——随后端起了她那贵族式的眉毛。

普卢默小姐毫不气馁。她正处在崇拜英雄的年纪；在她那座名人堂里，乔治同费尔班克斯（Fairbanks）、弗朗西斯·X·布什曼（Francis X. Bushman）几位先生，外加一两位网球冠军，合用着一座巍峨的基座。

“就是他呀！给《跟着姑娘走》谱曲的乔治·贝文！”

===Original===
Lady Caroline showed no signs of thawing. She had not heard of *Follow the Girl*. Her attitude suggested that, while she admitted the possibility of George having disgraced himself in the manner indicated, it was nothing to her.

“And all those other things,” pursued Miss Plummer indefatigably. “You must have heard his music on the Victrola.”

“Why, of course!”

It was not Lady Caroline who spoke, but a man further down the table. He spoke with enthusiasm.

“Of course, by Jove!” he said. “The Schenectady Shimmy, by Jove, and all that! Ripping!”

===Chinese===
卡罗琳夫人毫无解冻的迹象。《跟着姑娘走》她压根儿没有听说过。她的神气分明是说：她姑且承认乔治有可能真照方才那个样子丢人现眼来着，可这跟她毫不相干。

“还有他那些别的曲子呢，”普卢默小姐不屈不挠地追击，“您一定在维克托拉留声机上听过他的音乐。”

“哟，那当然！”

出声的不是卡罗琳夫人，而是桌子下首的一位先生。他说得热情洋溢。

“那当然，老天在上！”他说，“《斯克内克塔迪希米舞》（The Schenectady Shimmy），老天在上，还有那一套！妙透了！”

===Original===
Everybody seemed pleased and interested. Everybody, that is to say, except Lady Caroline and Lord Belpher. Percy was feeling that he had been tricked. He cursed the imbecility of Keggs in suggesting that this man should be invited to dinner. Everything had gone wrong. George was an undoubted success. The majority of the company were solid for him. As far as exposing his unworthiness in the eyes of Maud was concerned, the dinner had been a ghastly failure. Much better to have left him to lurk in his infernal cottage. Lord Belpher drained his glass moodily. He was seriously upset.

But his discomfort at that moment was as nothing to the agony which rent his tortured soul a moment later. Lord Marshmoreton, who had been listening with growing excitement to the chorus of approval, rose from his seat. He cleared his throat. It was plain that Lord Marshmoreton had something on his mind.

“Er.﻿ ﻿…” he said.

===Chinese===
人人显得又高兴又起劲。人人——就是说，除开卡罗琳夫人和贝尔弗勋爵。珀西只觉得自己叫人耍了。他咒骂凯格斯出的那个蠢主意：居然提议请这个人来赴晚宴。事情全砸了锅。乔治大获成功，无可置疑。满座多半死心塌地站在他那一边。就‘在莫德眼前揭他的老底’这一桩而言，这顿晚饭败得干干净净。还不如听任他窝在他那幢见鬼的小屋里好。贝尔弗勋爵闷闷地把杯中酒一饮而尽。他心里着实乱了套。

可他此刻的不自在，比起片刻之后撕扯他受难灵魂的那阵剧痛，又算不得什么了。马什莫顿勋爵听着这喝彩的声浪，兴致越来越高，竟从座位上站了起来。他清了清嗓子。明摆着，勋爵大人有心事要说。

“呃。……”他说。

===Original===
The clatter of conversation ceased once more﻿—stunned, as it always is at dinner parties when one of the gathering is seen to have assumed an upright position. Lord Marshmoreton cleared his throat again. His tanned face had taken on a deeper hue, and there was a look in his eyes which seemed to suggest that he was defying something or somebody. It was the look which Ajax had in his eyes when he defied the lightning, the look which nervous husbands have when they announce their intention of going round the corner to bowl a few games with the boys. One could not say definitely that Lord Marshmoreton looked pop-eyed. On the other hand, one could not assert truthfully that he did not. At any rate, he was manifestly embarrassed. He had made up his mind to a certain course of action on the spur of the moment, taking advantage, as others have done, of the trend of popular enthusiasm: and his state of mind was nervous but resolute, like that of a soldier going over the top. He cleared his throat for the third time, took one swift glance at his sister Caroline, then gazed glassily into the emptiness above her head.

“Take this opportunity,” he said rapidly, clutching at the tablecloth for support, “take this opportunity of announcing the engagement of my daughter Maud to Mr. Bevan. And,” he concluded with a rush, pouring back into his chair, “I should like you all to drink their health!”

There was a silence that hurt. It was broken by two sounds, occurring simultaneously in different parts of the room. One was a gasp from Lady Caroline. The other was a crash of glass.

For the first time in a long unblemished career Keggs the butler had dropped a tray.

===Chinese===
谈话的喧哗又一次停了下来——宴席上眼看有谁直挺挺地站起了身，众人照例要给震住的。马什莫顿勋爵又清了清嗓子。他那张晒黑的脸颜色更深了一层，眼睛里有种神气，仿佛在向什么东西或者什么人挑战。那是埃阿斯（Ajax）迎战雷霆时的眼色，是神经紧张的丈夫宣布打算上街角去同哥儿们滚几局球时的眼色。要说马什莫顿勋爵瞪眼爆睛，谁也不敢一口咬定；可要说他没有瞪眼爆睛，也没法讲得问心无愧。反正，他明明白白是窘住了。他趁着众人情绪高涨的势头，一冲动打定了主意要有所举动——旁人也这样干过；他的心境紧张然而坚决，好比跃出战壕冲锋的士兵。他第三次清了清嗓子，飞快地朝妹妹卡罗琳瞟了一眼，随即两眼发直，望住她头顶上方的那片虚空。

“趁这个机会，”他急急地说，一把抓住桌布稳住身子，“趁这个机会宣布：小女莫德同贝文先生订婚了。还有，”他一口气赶着收了尾，整个人倒回椅子里，“请诸位一同为他们的健康干杯！”

一阵沉默，疼得人心口发紧。打破沉默的是两个声音，在厅堂的两处同时响起。一个是卡罗琳夫人倒抽的气声。另一个是玻璃的碎裂声。

管家凯格斯在一长段清白无瑕的职业生涯里，头一回失手摔了托盘。

## XXIV / XXIV

===Original===

Out on the terrace the night was very still. From a steel-blue sky the stars looked down as calmly as they had looked on the night of the ball, when George had waited by the shrubbery listening to the wailing of the music and thinking long thoughts. From the dark meadows by the brook came the cry of a corncrake, its harsh note softened by distance.

“What shall we do?” said Maud. She was sitting on the stone seat where Reggie Byng had sat and meditated on his love for Alice Faraday and his unfortunate habit of slicing his approach-shots. To George, as he stood beside her, she was a white blur in the darkness. He could not see her face.

“I don’t know!” he said frankly.

Nor did he. Like Lady Caroline and Lord Belpher and Keggs, the butler, he had been completely overwhelmed by Lord Marshmoreton’s dramatic announcement. The situation had come upon him unheralded by any warning, and had found him unequal to it.

===Chinese===

露台外面，夜十分安静。钢青色的天穹上，星星低垂，安然俯视，同舞会那一夜一模一样——那一晚，乔治守在灌木林旁，听着哀哀切切的乐声，思绪悠长。小溪边黑黢黢的草甸上传来长脚秧鸡（corncrake）的啼声，粗粝的音色叫距离磨得柔和了。

“咱们该怎么办呢？”莫德说。她坐的正是那张石凳——雷吉·宾曾在那儿坐下，一边琢磨自己对爱丽丝·法拉第的一片痴情，一边发愁自己近距切球老打右曲的倒霉毛病。乔治站在她身旁，黑暗里她只是一团模模糊糊的白影。他看不清她的脸。

“我不知道！”他直截了当地说。

他确实不知道。同卡罗琳夫人、贝尔弗勋爵以及管家凯格斯一样，马什莫顿勋爵那戏剧性的当众宣布，把他彻底击垮了。这个局面猝然临头，事先没有半点风吹草动，一来就叫他招架不住。

===Original===

A choking sound suddenly proceeded from the whiteness that was Maud. In the stillness it sounded like some loud noise. It jarred on George’s disturbed nerves.

“Please!”

“I c-can’t help it!”

“There’s nothing to cry about, really! If we think long enough, we shall find some way out all right. Please don’t cry.”

“I’m not crying!” The choking sound became an unmistakable ripple of mirth. “It’s so absurd! Poor father getting up like that in front of everyone! Did you see Aunt Caroline’s face?”

===Chinese===

那团名叫莫德的白色影子，忽然发出一声哽咽。四下里太静，这一声听上去竟像是什么巨响。乔治本已纷乱的神经被它搅得生疼。

“求您了！”

“我忍——忍不住嘛！”

“实在没有什么好哭的！只要咱们肯好好想，总能想出一条出路。求您别哭了。”

“我没有哭！”那哽咽的声音分明已化作一串笑的涟漪。“这事太滑稽了！可怜的爸爸，当着满屋子的人霍地站起来！你瞧见卡罗琳姑妈那张脸没有？”

===Original===

“It haunts me still,” said George. “I shall never forget it. Your brother didn’t seem any too pleased, either.”

Maud stopped laughing.

“It’s an awful position,” she said soberly. “The announcement will be in the *Morning Post* the day after tomorrow. And then the letters of congratulation will begin to pour in. And after that the presents. And I simply can’t see how we can convince them all that there has been a mistake.” Another aspect of the matter struck her. “It’s so hard on you, too.”

“Don’t think about me,” urged George. “Heaven knows I’d give the whole world if we could just let the thing go on, but there’s no use discussing impossibilities.” He lowered his voice. “There’s no use, either, in my pretending that I’m not going to have a pretty bad time. But we won’t discuss that. It was my own fault. I came butting in on your life of my own free will, and, whatever happens, it’s been worth it to have known you and tried to be of service to you.”

===Chinese===

“那一张脸到现在还在我眼前晃，”乔治说，“我一辈子也忘不了。你哥哥那副样子，看着也不怎么痛快。”

莫德收了笑。

“这局面糟透了，”她正色说道，“订婚启事后天就要登上《晨邮报》了。接着贺信就要雪片般飞来。再往后就是贺礼。可我实在想不出，咱们怎么才能叫这所有的人相信：这里头出了个大误会。”她忽然又想到事情的一面。“这也太苦了你了。”

“别惦记我，”乔治催促道，“老天爷知道，倘若这事能将错就错地延续下去，叫我把整个世界搭上都愿意；可是办不到的事，讨论它没有用处。”他放低了声音。“硬要装作我往后的日子不会很难熬，那也是自欺欺人。不过这个咱们不谈了。怪我自己，是我自己心甘情愿一头闯进您的生活里来的；不管往后怎样，能认识您、能为您尽过一点力，就已经值了。”

===Original===

“You’re the best friend I’ve ever had.”

“I’m glad you think that.”

“The best and kindest friend any girl ever had. I wish﻿ ﻿…” She broke off. “Oh, well﻿ ﻿…”

There was a silence. In the castle somebody had begun to play the piano. Then a man’s voice began to sing.

===Chinese===

“你是我有生以来最好的朋友。”

“你这么想，我很高兴。”

“哪一个姑娘也从来没有过这么好、这么善心的朋友。我真希望……”她话说了半截。“唉，算了……”

一阵沉默。城堡里有人弹起了钢琴。跟着，一个男人的嗓音唱了起来。

===Original===

“That’s Edwin Plummer,” said Maud. “How badly he sings.”

George laughed. Somehow the intrusion of Plummer had removed the tension. Plummer, whether designedly and as a sombre commentary on the situation or because he was the sort of man who does sing that particular song, was chanting Tosti’s “Goodbye.” He was giving to its never very cheery notes a wailing melancholy all his own. A dog in the stables began to howl in sympathy, and with the sound came a curious soothing of George’s nerves. He might feel brokenhearted later, but for the moment, with this double accompaniment, it was impossible for a man with humour in his soul to dwell on the deeper emotions. Plummer and his canine duettist had brought him to earth. He felt calm and practical.

“We’d better talk the whole thing over quietly,” he said. “There’s certain to be some solution. At the worst you can always go to Lord Marshmoreton and tell him that he spoke without a sufficient grasp of his subject.”

“I could,” said Maud, “but, just at present, I feel as if I’d rather do anything else in the world. You don’t realize what it must have cost father to defy Aunt Caroline openly like that. Ever since I was old enough to notice anything, I’ve seen how she dominated him. It was Aunt Caroline who really caused all this trouble. If it had only been father, I could have coaxed him to let me marry anyone I pleased. I wish, if you possibly can, you would think of some other solution.”

===Chinese===

“那是埃德温·普卢默，”莫德说，“他唱得可真够呛。”

乔治笑了。说来也怪，普卢默这么一搅和，紧张的气氛反倒消散了。普卢默这会儿正一板一眼地唱着托斯蒂（Tosti）的《告别》（Goodbye）——不知他是有意为之，拿这支歌给眼下的局面做一曲阴沉的注脚，还是因为他天生就是会唱这支歌的那路人。那支曲子的调门本来就不怎么欢快，他偏又给它添上了一种独属于他自己的哀哀欲绝。马厩里有条狗起了共鸣，也跟着嗥了起来；随着这声音，乔治的神经竟奇异地舒缓下来。往后他也许会肝肠寸断，可就眼下而言，有这么一双伴奏在，一个骨子里存着幽默的人是断然深沉不下去的。普卢默和他那位犬声二重唱的搭档，把他拽回到了地面上。他只觉得心平气和，头脑讲求实际。

“咱们最好安安静静把整件事商量一遍，”他说，“办法总归是有的。退一万步说，您总还可以去找马什莫顿勋爵，告诉他：他方才那番话，是对手里的题目没有吃透就开了口。”

“我倒是能去，”莫德说，“可眼下，天底下随便是什么都行，我就是不想去。你不知道，爸爸方才那样公开顶撞卡罗琳姑妈，得豁出去多大的勇气。打从我懂事能看出事来，我就一直看在眼里：她把他拿捏得死死的。这场麻烦，真正的祸根是卡罗琳姑妈。倘若光是爸爸一个人，我磨也磨得他点头，随我嫁谁都成。求你想个别样的法子吧，只要你办得到。”

===Original===

“I haven’t had an opportunity of telling you,” said George, “that I called at Belgrave Square, as you asked me to do. I went there directly I had seen Reggie Byng safely married.”

“Did you see him married?”

“I was best man.”

“Dear old Reggie! I hope he will be happy.”

“He will. Don’t worry about that. Well, as I was saying, I called at Belgrave Square, and found the house shut up. I couldn’t get any answer to the bell, though I kept my thumb on it for minutes at a time. I think they must have gone abroad again.”

“No, it wasn’t that. I had a letter from Geoffrey this morning. His uncle died of apoplexy, while they were in Manchester on a business trip.” She paused. “He left Geoffrey all his money,” she went on. “Every penny.”

===Chinese===

“有件事我一直没得机会告诉您，”乔治说，“我照您的嘱咐上贝尔格雷夫广场去过了。亲眼看着雷吉·宾安安稳稳把婚礼办完，我就直接去了。”

“你亲眼看着他成婚了？”

“我是伴郎。”

“亲爱的老雷吉！但愿他会幸福。”

“他会的，这个您放心。嗯，接着方才的话说：我去了贝尔格雷夫广场，发现宅子门窗紧闭。门铃按了又按，我一按就是好几分钟，里面始终没人应。我想，他们准是又出国了。”

“不，不是那么回事。今天早上我收到了杰弗里的信。他叔叔中风去世了，他们当时正在曼彻斯特（Manchester）办事。”她顿了顿。“他把名下的钱全数留给了杰弗里，”她接着说，“一个便士都不剩。”

---

===Original===

The silence seemed to stretch out interminably. The music from the castle had ceased. The quiet of the summer night was unbroken. To George the stillness had a touch of the sinister. It was the ghastly silence of the end of the world. With a shock he realized that even now he had been permitting himself to hope, futile as he recognized the hope to be. Maud had told him she loved another man. That should have been final. And yet somehow his indomitable subconscious self had refused to accept it as final. But this news ended everything. The only obstacle that had held Maud and this man apart was removed. There was nothing to prevent them marrying. George was conscious of a vast depression. The last strand of the rope had parted, and he was drifting alone out into the ocean of desolation.

“Oh!” he said, and was surprised that his voice sounded very much the same as usual. Speech was so difficult that it seemed strange that it should show no signs of effort. “That alters everything, doesn’t it.”

“He said in his letter that he wanted me to meet him in London and﻿—talk things over, I suppose.”

“There’s nothing now to prevent your going. I mean, now that your father has made this announcement, you are free to go where you please.”

===Chinese===

沉默仿佛没有尽头地延伸下去。城堡里的乐声停了。夏夜的宁静完好无损。在乔治听来，这份静谧却透着一丝不祥，那是世界末日一般的死寂。他猛地一惊，这才发觉，直到此刻他还在由着自己心存指望——尽管他明知这指望注定落空。莫德早就告诉过他，她爱着另一个男人。这句话本该一锤定音。可不知怎的，他那股不肯认输的下意识，始终不肯把这当作定局。如今这个消息把一切都了结了。原本隔在莫德和那个男人之间的唯一障碍没有了。再也没有什么拦着他们成婚。乔治只觉得一阵天塌地陷般的消沉。缆绳的最后一股断了，他孤零零地漂进一片荒凉的大洋。

“哦！”他说，自己倒诧异起来：这声音听上去竟与平常没有什么两样。说话这般费力，出口竟不带一丝费劲的痕迹，真是奇怪。“这一来，什么都变了，是不是。”

“他在信里说，要我上伦敦去见他——我想，是要把事情谈一谈吧。”

“如今再没有什么拦着您去了。我是说，令尊既然已经当众宣布，您爱去哪儿，就可以去哪儿了。”

===Original===

“Yes, I suppose I am.”

There was another silence.

“Everything’s so difficult,” said Maud.

“In what way?”

“Oh, I don’t know.”

===Chinese===

“是啊，我想是的。”

又是一阵沉默。

“什么事都这么难，”莫德说。

“难在哪儿？”

“唉，说不上来。”

===Original===

“If you are thinking of me,” said George, “please don’t. I know exactly what you mean. You are hating the thought of hurting my feelings. I wish you would look on me as having no feelings. All I want is to see you happy. As I said just now, it’s enough for me to know that I’ve helped you. Do be reasonable about it. The fact that our engagement has been officially announced makes no difference in our relations to each other. As far as we two are concerned, we are exactly where we were the last time we met. It’s no worse for me now than it was then to know that I’m not the man you love, and that there’s somebody else you loved before you ever knew of my existence. For goodness’ sake, a girl like you must be used to having men tell her that they love her and having to tell them that she can’t love them in return.”

“But you’re so different.”

“Not a bit of it. I’m just one of the crowd.”

“I’ve never known anybody quite like you.”

===Chinese===

“您要是心里在替我着想，”乔治说，“那就千万别。您的意思我再明白不过：您一想到要伤我的心，就难受。求您把我当成一个没有心的人。我求的只有一件事——看见您幸福。方才我说过了，只要知道自己帮上了您的忙，我就心满意足。这件事上，您务必讲点道理。咱们订婚的事既已正式宣布，也改变不了您我之间的相处。单就咱们两个人而论，一切与上一回见面时分毫不差。明知道您爱的不是我，明知道早在您听说世上有我这个人之前，您心里就已经有了别人——如今知道这些，并不比那时候更让我难受。我的老天爷，像您这样的姑娘，听男人们倾诉爱慕、再回绝他们说您无法以爱相答，想必早就习以为常了。”

“可你跟他们不一样啊。”

“一点儿也不。我不过是人堆里的一个。”

“像你这样的人，我还从来没见过。”

===Original===

“Well, you’ve never known anybody quite like Plummer, I should imagine. But the thought of his sufferings didn’t break your heart.”

“I’ve known a million men exactly like Edwin Plummer,” said Maud emphatically. “All the men I ever have known have been like him﻿—quite nice and pleasant and negative. It never seemed to matter refusing them. One knew that they would be just a little bit piqued for a week or two and then wander off and fall in love with somebody else. But you’re different. You﻿ ﻿… matter.”

“That is where we disagree. My argument is that, where your happiness is concerned, I don’t matter.”

Maud rested her chin on her hand, and stared out into the velvet darkness.

===Chinese===

“这个嘛，我猜，像普卢默这样的人，您八成也没见过第二个。可您想到他那份痛苦，心也没有碎啊。”

“跟埃德温·普卢默一模一样的男人，我见过一百万个，”莫德一板一眼地说，“我认识的男人从来都是他那一类——挺好，和气，也无足轻重。回绝他们，似乎从来不必放在心上。都知道他们至多恼上一两个星期，随后就会溜达开去，爱上别的什么人。可你不一样。你……是有分量的。”

“这正是咱们说不到一处的地方。我的道理是：凡关系到您的幸福，我就算不得什么。”

莫德一手托着下巴，凝望着天鹅绒似的浓黑夜色。

===Original===

“You ought to have been my brother instead of Percy,” she said at last. “What chums we should have been! And how simple that would have made everything!”

“The best thing for you to do is to regard me as an honorary brother. That will make everything simple.”

“It’s easy to talk like that﻿ ﻿… No, it isn’t. It’s horribly hard. I know exactly how difficult it is for you to talk as you have been doing﻿—to try to make me feel better by pretending the whole trouble is just a trifle﻿ ﻿… It’s strange﻿ ﻿… We have only met really for a few minutes at a time, and three weeks ago I didn’t know there was such a person as you, but somehow I seem to know everything you’re thinking. I’ve never felt like that before with any man﻿ ﻿… Even Geoffrey﻿ ﻿… He always puzzled me.﻿ ﻿…”

She broke off. The corncrake began to call again out in the distance.

===Chinese===

“你真该做我的哥哥，把珀西换下来才好，”她终于开口说，“那咱们该处得多亲热！什么事情也都会变得多简单！”

“您顶好就把我当作一位名誉哥哥。那样一来，什么都简单了。”

“说得倒轻巧……不，不轻巧。难得很。你方才那样说话有多为难，我心里清清楚楚——硬装出整场麻烦不过是小事一桩的样子，好叫我心里好受些……真奇怪……咱们真正碰面，满打满算一回不过几分钟；三个星期以前，我还不知道世上有你这么个人，可不知怎么，你心里在想什么，我好像全都知道。跟别的男人，我从来没有过这样的感觉……连杰弗里也……他老是叫我捉摸不透。……”

她住了口。远处，长脚秧鸡又叫了起来。

===Original===

“I wish I knew what to do,” she said with a catch in her voice.

“I’ll tell you in two words what to do. The whole thing is absurdly simple. You love this man and he loves you, and all that kept you apart before was the fact that he could not afford to marry you. Now that he is rich, there is no obstacle at all. I simply won’t let you look on me and my feelings as an obstacle. Rule me out altogether. Your father’s mistake has made the situation a little more complicated than it need have been, but that can easily be remedied. Imitate the excellent example of Reggie Byng. He was in a position where it would have been embarrassing to announce what he intended to do, so he very sensibly went quietly off and did it and left everybody to find out after it was done. I’m bound to say I never looked on Reggie as a master mind, but, when it came to find a way out of embarrassing situations, one has to admit he had the right idea. Do what he did!”

===Chinese===

“真希望我知道该怎么办才好，”她说着，喉咙里哽了一下。

“怎么办，我两句话就能告诉您。整件事简单得近乎可笑。您爱这个男人，这个男人也爱您；先前隔开你们俩的，无非是一桩事实——他囊中羞涩，娶不起您。如今他阔了，障碍一点儿也没有了。我绝不容许您把我、把我的感情，当成什么障碍。把我一笔勾销，彻底排除在外。令尊闹的这个误会，让局面比本来要多绕几个弯，可这不难补救。学一学雷吉·宾那个绝妙的榜样吧。他当时的处境，是把打定的事一宣布就会难堪；于是他很识相，悄悄溜出去把事办了，办完之后才让大伙儿自己发现。说句实话，我从来不曾把雷吉看作什么旷世奇才；可说到替难堪的场面找条出路，不能不承认，他这条路子走得对。照他那样办吧！”

---

===Original===

Maud started. She half rose from the stone seat. George could hear the quick intake of her breath.

“You mean﻿—run away?”

“Exactly. Run away!”

===Chinese===

莫德吓了一跳。她从石凳上欠起身来。乔治听得见她急促地吸了一口气。

“您是说——私奔？”

“正是。私奔！”

===Original===

An automobile swung round the corner of the castle from the direction of the garage, and drew up, purring, at the steps. There was a flood of light and the sound of voices, as the great door opened. Maud rose.

“People are leaving,” she said. “I didn’t know it was so late.” She stood irresolutely. “I suppose I ought to go in and say goodbye. But I don’t think I can.”

“Stay where you are. Nobody will see you.”

More automobiles arrived. The quiet of the night was shattered by the noise of their engines. Maud sat down again.

===Chinese===

一辆汽车从车库那头绕过城堡的墙角，呜呜低吼着，停到了台阶前。大门打开，泻出一片灯光，人声嘈杂。莫德站起身。

“客人们要走了，”她说，“没想到已经这么晚。”她站在那儿，拿不定主意。“按说我该进去道声别。可我怕是做不到。”

“就待在原地别动。不会有人瞧见您的。”

又来了几辆汽车。引擎的喧声把夜的宁静砸得粉碎。莫德重新坐下。

===Original===

“I suppose they will think it very odd of me not being there.”

“Never mind what people think. Reggie Byng didn’t.”

Maud’s foot traced circles on the dry turf.

“What a lovely night,” she said. “There’s no dew at all.”

===Chinese===

“我想他们准会觉得我古怪，人不在场。”

“别管人家怎么想。雷吉·宾就没管。”

莫德用脚尖在干干的草皮上画着圈儿。

“多可爱的夜啊，”她说，“一点儿露水都没有。”

===Original===

The automobiles snorted, tooted, backfired, and passed away. Their clamour died in the distance, leaving the night a thing of peace and magic once more. The door of the castle closed with a bang.

“I suppose I ought to be going in now,” said Maud.

“I suppose so. And I ought to be there, too, politely making my farewells. But something seems to tell me that Lady Caroline and your brother will be quite ready to dispense with the formalities. I shall go home.”

They faced each other in the darkness.

===Chinese===

那些汽车喷着气，按着喇叭，放炮似的回着火，末了一辆辆走远了。喧闹在远处渐渐消歇，夜重新成了一个安宁而神奇的世界。城堡的大门砰的一声关上了。

“我想我这会儿该进去了，”莫德说。

“我也这么想。我也该到场，客客气气地向众人辞行。不过心里仿佛有个声音在告诉我：卡罗琳夫人和令兄一定巴不得免了这份客套。我这就回家去。”

黑暗里，两人面对面站着。

===Original===

“Would you really do that?” asked Maud. “Run away, I mean, and get married in London.”

“It’s the only thing to do.”

“But﻿ ﻿… can one get married as quickly as that?”

“At a registrar’s? Nothing simpler. You should have seen Reggie Byng’s wedding. It was over before one realized it had started. A snuffy little man in a black coat with a cold in his head asked a few questions, wrote a few words, and the thing was done.”

“That sounds rather﻿ ﻿… dreadful.”

===Chinese===

“您当真愿意那么办？”莫德问，“我是说，一走了之，上伦敦去成婚。”

“只有这一条路可走。”

“可是……结婚能那么快吗？”

“在登记官那儿？再简单不过了。您真该见识见识雷吉·宾的婚礼。人还没觉出它开始了，它已经完了。一个穿黑外套的干巴小老头，鼻子里还闹着伤风，问了几句话，写了几行字，事情就成了。”

“那听着相当……可怕。”

===Original===

“Reggie didn’t seem to think so.”

“Unromantic, I mean.﻿ ﻿… Prosaic.”

“You would supply the romance.”

“Of course, one ought to be sensible. It is just the same as a regular wedding.”

“In effects, absolutely.”

===Chinese===

“雷吉好像并不觉得。”

“我是说，不够浪漫。……平淡乏味。”

“浪漫由您来补上。”

“当然了，人也该明理务实。那同一场规规矩矩的婚礼并没有两样。”

“论效力，分毫不差。”

===Original===

They moved up the terrace together. On the gravel drive by the steps they paused.

“I’ll do it!” said Maud.

George had to make an effort before he could reply. For all his sane and convincing arguments, he could not check a pang at this definite acceptance of them. He had begun to appreciate now the strain under which he had been speaking.

“You must,” he said. “Well﻿ ﻿… goodbye.”

===Chinese===

两人一道往露台上头走。到台阶旁的碎石车道上，停住了。

“我去办！”莫德说。

乔治鼓了一把劲，才答得上话来。他那番道理讲得再清醒不过、再叫人信服不过，可眼见她当真一口应承，他心口仍旧猛地一抽。直到此刻，他才开始体会到，自己方才是绷紧到什么地步在说话。

“您必须去办，”他说，“那么……再见。”
