===Original===

There was light on the drive. He could see her face. Her eyes were troubled.

“What will you do?” she asked.

“Do?”

“I mean, are you going to stay on in your cottage?”

“No, I hardly think I could do that. I shall go back to London tomorrow, and stay at the Carlton for a few days. Then I shall sail for America. There are a couple of pieces I’ve got to do for the Fall. I ought to be starting on them.”

===Chinese===

车道上有灯光。他看得见她的脸了。她眼神里含着不安。

“那您往后怎么办呢？”她问。

“怎么办？”

“我是说，您还打算在您那幢小屋里住下去吗？”

“不，那样我怕是熬不住的。明天我就回伦敦，在卡尔顿饭店小住几天，随后就搭船回美国。入秋还有两支曲子非写不可，我也早该动笔了。”

===Original===

Maud looked away.

“You’ve got your work,” she said almost inaudibly.

George understood her.

“Yes, I’ve got my work.”

===Chinese===

莫德把目光移开了。

“你还有你的工作，”她说，声音低得几乎听不见。

乔治懂得她。

“是啊，我还有我的工作。”

===Original===

“I’m glad.”

She held out her hand.

“You’ve been very wonderful﻿ ﻿… Right from the beginning﻿ ﻿… You’ve been﻿ ﻿… oh, what’s the use of me saying anything?”

“I’ve had my reward. I’ve known you. We’re friends, aren’t we?”

===Chinese===

“我很高兴。”

她伸出手来。

“你待我一直好极了……打一开头就好……你一直都……唉，我说这些又有什么用呢？”

“我已经得到我的报偿了。我认识了您。咱们是朋友，对不对？”

===Original===

“My best friend.”

“Pals?”

“Pals!”

They shook hands.

===Chinese===

“我最好的朋友。”

“好伙伴？”

“好伙伴！”

他们握了握手。

## XXV / XXV

===Original===

“I was never so upset in my life!” said Lady Caroline.

She had been saying the same thing and many other things for the past five minutes. Until the departure of the last guest she had kept an icy command of herself and shown an unruffled front to the world. She had even contrived to smile. But now, with the final automobile whirring homewards, she had thrown off the mask. The very furniture of Lord Marshmoreton’s study seemed to shrink, seared by the flame of her wrath. As for Lord Marshmoreton himself, he looked quite shrivelled.

It had not been an easy matter to bring her erring brother to bay. The hunt had been in progress full ten minutes before she and Lord Belpher finally cornered the poor wretch. His plea, through the keyhole of the locked door, that he was working on the family history and could not be disturbed, was ignored; and now he was face to face with the avengers.

“I cannot understand it,” continued Lady Caroline. “You know that for months we have all been straining every nerve to break off this horrible entanglement, and, just as we had begun to hope that something might be done, you announce the engagement in the most public manner. I think you must be out of your mind. I can hardly believe even now that this appalling thing has happened. I am hoping that I shall wake up and find it is all a nightmare. How you can have done such a thing, I cannot understand.”

“Quite!” said Lord Belpher.

===Chinese===

“我活了一辈子，从没这么心烦意乱过！”卡罗琳夫人说。

这番话她已经说了足足五分钟，此外还说了许多别的。直到最后一位客人告辞之前，她始终冷冰冰地克制着自己，人前摆出一副波澜不惊的脸色，甚至还硬挤出了笑容。可如今，最后一辆汽车嗡嗡响着驶上归途，她把假面具一把扯了下来。连马什莫顿勋爵书房里的家具，仿佛都被她怒火的烈焰灼得瑟瑟畏缩；至于马什莫顿勋爵本人，看上去简直是蔫缩成一团了。

要把她那位误入歧途的兄弟逼入绝境，可不是件容易事。围猎足足进行了十分钟，她和贝尔弗勋爵才终于把那可怜的倒霉鬼堵进了死角。他隔着上锁房门的钥匙孔申辩，说自己正在撰写《家族史》，不容打扰；这番央告没人理会。如今，他已经和复仇者们面对面了。

“我想不通，”卡罗琳夫人接着说，“你也知道，这几个月来，我们大伙儿都在铆足了劲，要斩断这桩可怕的孽缘；偏偏就在我们刚开始觉得有望办成点什么的时候，你却用最招摇的方式当众宣布了订婚。我看你准是疯了。直到此刻，我几乎还不敢相信这件骇人听闻的事当真发生了。我只盼一觉醒来，发现这一切不过是一场噩梦。你怎么干得出这种事来，我想不通。”

“正是！”贝尔弗勋爵说。

===Original===

If Lady Caroline was upset, there are no words in the language that will adequately describe the emotions of Percy.

From the very start of this lamentable episode in high life, Percy had been in the forefront of the battle. It was Percy who had had his best hat smitten from his head in the full view of all Piccadilly. It was Percy who had suffered arrest and imprisonment in the cause. It was Percy who had been crippled for days owing to his zeal in tracking Maud across country. And now all his sufferings were in vain. He had been betrayed by his own father.

There was, so the historians of the Middle West tell us, a man of Chicago named Young, who once, when his nerves were unstrung, put his mother (unseen) in the chopping-machine, and canned her and labelled her “Tongue.” It is enough to say that the glance of disapproval which Percy cast upon his father at this juncture would have been unduly severe if cast by the Young offspring upon their parent at the moment of confession.

Lord Marshmoreton had rallied from his initial panic. The spirit of revolt began to burn again in his bosom. Once the die is cast for revolution, there can be no looking back. One must defy, not apologize. Perhaps the inherited tendencies of a line of ancestors who, whatever their shortcomings, had at least known how to treat their women folk, came to his aid. Possibly there stood by his side in this crisis ghosts of dead and buried Marshmoretons, whispering spectral encouragement in his ear﻿—the ghosts, let us suppose, of that earl who, in the days of the seventh Henry, had stabbed his wife with a dagger to cure her tendency to lecture him at night; or of that other earl who, at a previous date in the annals of the family, had caused two aunts and a sister to be poisoned apparently from a mere whim. At any rate, Lord Marshmoreton produced from some source sufficient courage to talk back.

===Chinese===

若说卡罗琳夫人算是心烦意乱，那么珀西胸中的百感翻腾，普天下的语言里找不出一个词形容得恰到好处。

这场伤心事打从在上流社会开场起，珀西就一直奋战在最前线。在皮卡迪利满街人的众目睽睽之下，一顶最好的帽子被人一击打飞的，是珀西。为了这项大业横遭逮捕、身陷囹圄的，是珀西。凭着跟踪莫德穿越乡野的一腔热忱，伤筋动骨一连好几天起不了身的，还是珀西。如今，他受的这些苦统统付诸东流。他叫自己的亲生父亲给出卖了。

据美国中西部的历史学家们讲，芝加哥有一个姓扬的男子，有一回神经绷断了，把自己的母亲（趁着没人瞧见）塞进了剁肉机，做成罐头，贴上“牛舌”的标签。这里只需说一句也就尽够了：此刻珀西投向他父亲的那一瞥不以为然的目光，即便由扬家的儿女在他们的老子招供认罪的一刻投出，也要嫌太严厉了。

马什莫顿勋爵已经从起初的惊慌中缓过劲来。反叛的火焰重新在他胸中烧了起来。革命一旦定局，便再无回头路可走。人只能挺身抗命，不能低声赔罪。也许是一脉列祖列宗传下的禀性赶来助阵——这一脉祖先纵有千般短处，至少懂得该怎么对付自家的女眷；也许，危难当头，那些入土多年的马什莫顿家的鬼魂正站在他身旁，往他耳朵里灌幽灵的打气话——我们不妨设想，是那位在亨利七世当朝的岁月里用匕首捅了夫人一刀、治好了她夜里训话毛病的伯爵的鬼魂；又或是那位年代更早的伯爵的鬼魂，在家乘的篇章里，他单凭一时兴起，就作主让两位姑母和一个妹妹叫人毒死了。总而言之，马什莫顿勋爵不知打哪儿鼓起了足够的勇气，顶了回去。

===Original===

“Silly nonsense!” he grunted. “Don’t see what you’re making all this fuss about. Maud loves the fellow. I like the fellow. Perfectly decent fellow. Nothing to make a fuss about. Why shouldn’t I announce the engagement?”

“You must be mad!” cried Lady Caroline. “Your only daughter and a man nobody knows anything about!”

“Quite!” said Percy.

Lord Marshmoreton seized his advantage with the skill of an adroit debater.

“That’s where you’re wrong. I know all about him. He’s a very rich man. You heard the way all those people at dinner behaved when they heard his name. Very celebrated man! Makes thousands of pounds a year. Perfectly suitable match in every way.”

===Chinese===

“瞎胡闹！”他咕哝道，“不明白你们这通大惊小怪图个什么。莫德爱那个小伙子。我也喜欢那个小伙子。规规矩矩的好小伙子。没什么可大惊小怪的。我宣布订婚又有什么使不得？”

“你准是疯了！”卡罗琳夫人大声说，“你的独生女儿，要嫁给一个谁也摸不着底细的男人！”

“正是！”珀西说。

马什莫顿勋爵抓住战机，手法娴熟，俨然一位机敏的辩手。

“这你就说错了。他的底细我一清二楚。他是个非常有钱的人。席面上众人一听见他的名字，那副光景你是亲眼看见的。鼎鼎大名的人物！一年挣好几千镑。这门亲事方方面面都般配。”

===Original===

“It is not a suitable match,” said Lady Caroline vehemently. “I don’t care whether this Mr. Bevan makes thousands of pounds a year or twopence-ha’penny. The match is not suitable. Money is not everything.”

She broke off. A knock had come on the door. The door opened, and Billie Dore came in. A kindhearted girl, she had foreseen that Lord Marshmoreton might be glad of a change of subject at about this time.

“Would you like me to help you tonight?” she asked brightly. “I thought I would ask if there was anything you wanted me to do.”

Lady Caroline snatched hurriedly at her aristocratic calm. She resented the interruption acutely, but her manner, when she spoke, was bland.

“Lord Marshmoreton will not require your help tonight,” she said. “He will not be working.”

===Chinese===

“这门亲事就是不般配，”卡罗琳夫人激愤地说，“我不管这位贝文先生一年挣的是好几千镑还是两便士半。亲事就是不般配。钱不是一切。”

她蓦地收了口。有人敲门。门开了，比莉·多尔走了进来。这姑娘一副热心肠，早已料到这个时辰，马什莫顿勋爵多半巴不得有人来给他换个话头。

“今晚要不要我来帮您干活？”她兴冲冲地问，“我想着还是来问一声，看您有没有什么事要我做的。”

卡罗琳夫人慌忙一把抓回她那副世家贵妇的从容。这场打岔叫她恼火到了极点，可开口时语气却温文和婉。

“马什莫顿勋爵今晚不需要你帮忙，”她说，“他不干活了。”

===Original===

“Good night,” said Billie.

“Good night,” said Lady Caroline.

Percy scowled a valediction.

“Money,” resumed Lady Caroline, “is immaterial. Maud is in no position to be obliged to marry a rich man. What makes the thing impossible is that Mr. Bevan is nobody. He comes from nowhere. He has no social standing whatsoever.”

“Don’t see it,” said Lord Marshmoreton. “The fellow’s a thoroughly decent fellow. That’s all that matters.”

===Chinese===

“晚安。”比莉说。

“晚安。”卡罗琳夫人说。

珀西板起一张阴沉的脸，权作临别的赠言。

“钱嘛，”卡罗琳夫人接着说，“不在话下。莫德还没落到非得嫁个有钱人不可的地步。这件事万万办不得，是因为贝文先生是个无名之辈。他不知打哪儿冒出来的。他毫无社会地位可言。”

“我不明白，”马什莫顿勋爵说，“那小伙子是个地地道道的好小伙子。这才顶要紧。”

===Original===

“How can you be so pigheaded! You are talking like an imbecile. Your secretary, Miss Dore, is a nice girl. But how would you feel if Percy were to come to you and say that he was engaged to be married to her?”

“Exactly!” said Percy. “Quite!”

Lord Marshmoreton rose and moved to the door. He did it with a certain dignity, but there was a strange hunted expression in his eyes.

“That would be impossible,” he said.

“Precisely,” said his sister. “I am glad that you admit it.”

===Chinese===

“你怎么能这样顽固不化！你这番话，蠢得像个傻子。你的秘书多尔小姐是个好姑娘。可要是珀西跑到你跟前，说他同她订了婚，你又作何感想？”

“说得对！”珀西说，“正是！”

马什莫顿勋爵站起身，朝门口挪去。动作倒还端着几分气派，眼睛里却挂着一副古怪的、被追猎的神色。

“那可是断然使不得的。”他说。

“一点不错，”他妹妹说，“你能承认这一点，我很高兴。”

===Original===

Lord Marshmoreton had reached the door, and was standing holding the handle. He seemed to gather strength from its support.

“I’ve been meaning to tell you about that,” he said.

“About what?”

“About Miss Dore. I married her myself last Wednesday,” said Lord Marshmoreton, and disappeared like a diving duck.

===Chinese===

马什莫顿勋爵已经到了门口，握着门把站在那里。他仿佛从这门把的支撑里汲取了力气。

“这事我一直想找机会跟你们说一声，”他说。

“说什么？”

“说多尔小姐呀。上个星期三，我自己把她娶了。”马什莫顿勋爵说完，人已像一头扎进水里的鸭子似的没了踪影。

## XXVI / XXVI

===Original===

At a quarter past four in the afternoon, two days after the memorable dinner-party at which Lord Marshmoreton had behaved with so notable a lack of judgment, Maud sat in Ye Cosy Nooke, waiting for Geoffrey Raymond. He had said in his telegram that he would meet her there at four-thirty: but eagerness had brought Maud to the tryst a quarter of an hour ahead of time: and already the sadness of her surroundings was causing her to regret this impulsiveness. Depression had settled upon her spirit. She was aware of something that resembled foreboding.

Ye Cosy Nooke, as its name will immediately suggest to those who know their London, is a teashop in Bond Street, conducted by distressed gentlewomen. In London, when a gentlewoman becomes distressed﻿—which she seems to do on the slightest provocation﻿—she collects about her two or three other distressed gentlewomen, forming a quorum, and starts a teashop in the West-End, which she calls Ye Oak Leaf, Ye Olde Willow-Pattern, Ye Linden-Tree, or Ye Snug Harbour, according to personal taste. There, dressed in Tyrolese, Japanese, Norwegian, or some other exotic costume, she and her associates administer refreshments of an afternoon with a proud languor calculated to knock the nonsense out of the cheeriest customer. Here you will find none of the coarse bustle and efficiency of the rival establishments of Lyons and Co., nor the glitter and gaiety of Rumpelmayer’s. These places have an atmosphere of their own. They rely for their effect on an insufficiency of light, an almost total lack of ventilation, a property chocolate cake which you are not supposed to cut, and the sad aloofness of their ministering angels. It is to be doubted whether there is anything in the world more damping to the spirit than a London teashop of this kind, unless it be another London teashop of the same kind.

Maud sat and waited. Somewhere out of sight a kettle bubbled in an undertone, like a whispering pessimist. Across the room two distressed gentlewomen in fancy dress leaned against the wall. They, too, were whispering. Their expressions suggested that they looked on life as low and wished they were well out of it, like the body upstairs. One assumed that there was a body upstairs. One cannot help it at these places. One’s first thought on entering is that the lady assistant will approach one and ask in a hushed voice “Tea or chocolate? And would you care to view the remains?”

===Chinese===

下午四点一刻，在那场难忘的晚宴——席间马什莫顿勋爵的判断力失灵得未免太惹眼——过去两天之后，莫德坐在“舒心角落”（Ye Cosy Nooke）里，等杰弗里·雷蒙德。他在电报里说是四点半在那儿碰头；可是一颗心等不及，莫德提前一刻钟就赶到了约会地点；而四下里的凄清已经叫她后悔起这阵冲动来。一种沮丧沉落在她的心头。她觉察到几分近乎凶兆的东西。

“舒心角落”，凡是熟悉伦敦的人，一望其名便立刻心领神会：那是邦德街（Bond Street）上一家由落难闺秀经营的茶室。在伦敦，一位闺秀一旦落了难——似乎稍稍一点由头就足以叫她落难——她便把另外两三位落难闺秀聚拢到身边，凑成一个开张的班底，在西区开一家茶室，取名悉随己意：或是“老橡叶”，或是“古柳纹轩”，或是“菩提树”，或是“安泊港”。在那里，她同她的伙伴们身穿蒂罗尔式、日式、挪威式或别的什么异国服装，整个下午端出茶点来，神情倨傲而恹恹，那份功力足以把最兴高采烈的顾客的兴头敲打干净。这里你见不到对手里昂公司（Lyons and Co.）那种粗俗的忙碌与麻利，也见不到伦佩尔迈尔（Rumpelmayer’s）那种金光闪闪的欢快。这类地方自有一派气象。它们全仗以下诸端收其效果：光线不足，空气几乎全无流通，一块道具巧克力蛋糕——不许你切——还有侍茶天使们那份哀伤而落落寡合的气度。世上恐怕再没有什么比伦敦这一路茶室更叫人扫兴的了，除非是另一家同一路数的伦敦茶室。

莫德坐着等。看不见的什么地方，一把茶壶低声咕嘟着，像个窃窃私语的悲观主义者。屋子那头，两个化了装的落难闺秀靠墙立着。她们也在窃窃私语。那神情分明是说：她们把人生看作一件低劣的玩意儿，巴不得早早抽身，就像楼上那具遗体一样。大家自然认定楼上有一具遗体。到了这种地方，由不得你不这么想。一进门，头一个念头就是那位女侍会款款走近，压着嗓子问上一句：“茶还是巧克力？要不要顺便瞻仰一下遗体？”

===Original===

Maud looked at her watch. It was twenty past four. She could scarcely believe that she had only been there five minutes, but the ticking of the watch assured her that it had not stopped. Her depression deepened. Why had Geoffrey told her to meet him in a cavern of gloom like this instead of at the Savoy? She would have enjoyed the Savoy. But here she seemed to have lost beyond recovery the first gay eagerness with which she had set out to meet the man she loved.

Suddenly she began to feel frightened. Some evil spirit, possibly the kettle, seemed to whisper to her that she had been foolish in coming here, to cast doubts on what she had hitherto regarded as the one rock-solid fact in the world, her love for Geoffrey. Could she have changed since those days in Wales? Life had been so confusing of late. In the vividness of recent happenings those days in Wales seemed a long way off, and she herself different from the girl of a year ago. She found herself thinking about George Bevan.

It was a curious fact that, the moment she began to think of George Bevan, she felt better. It was as if she had lost her way in a wilderness and had met a friend. There was something so capable, so soothing about George. And how well he had behaved at that last interview. George seemed somehow to be part of her life. She could not imagine a life in which he had no share. And he was at this moment, probably, packing to return to America, and she would never see him again. Something stabbed at her heart. It was as if she were realizing now for the first time that he was really going.

She tried to rid herself of the ache at her heart by thinking of Wales. She closed her eyes, and found that that helped her to remember. With her eyes shut, she could bring it all back﻿—that rainy day, the graceful, supple figure that had come to her out of the mist, those walks over the hills﻿ ﻿… If only Geoffrey would come! It was the sight of him that she needed.

===Chinese===

莫德看了看表。四点二十。她简直不能相信自己才来了五分钟，可是腕表滴答的走针向她担保，表并没有停。她的沮丧更深了。杰弗里为什么叫她到这么一个阴森洞窟里同他见面，不去萨沃伊饭店？萨沃伊饭店她本是会喜欢的。可是在这儿，她动身来见心爱的人时那份轻快的急切，似乎已经丢得找不回来了。

忽然，她害怕起来。有个邪祟——多半是那把茶壶——仿佛在附耳低语，说她跑到这儿来是干了蠢事，在她素来视作世上唯一磐石般稳固的那件事实上凿起疑来：她对杰弗里的爱。打从威尔士那些日子以来，她莫非变了？近来的日子实在叫人糊涂。眼前的一桩桩事太鲜明，威尔士的那些日子显得隔了老远，她自己也同一年前那个姑娘不一样了。她发觉自己想起了乔治·贝文。

说来也怪，一想乔治·贝文，她心里就好受了些。仿佛一个人在荒野里迷了路，迎面遇上一位朋友。乔治身上有种东西那么可靠、那么抚慰人心。还有上回见面，他的举止又是多么妥帖。乔治不知怎么竟像成了她生命的一部分。她想不出一个没有他份儿的人生。而此刻，他多半正在打点行装回美国去，她再也见不着他了。有什么东西扎了她心口一针。她仿佛直到此刻才头一回明白过来：他是真的要走了。

她试着去想威尔士，好把心口那阵疼赶走。她闭上眼睛，发觉这样倒帮助记忆。眼睛一闭，一切都能召回来——那个雨天，那个从雾里向她走来的袅娜柔韧的身影，那些翻山越岭的漫步……只要杰弗里来就好了！她需要的就是亲眼见到他。

===Original===

“There you are!”

Maud opened her eyes with a start. The voice had sounded like Geoffrey’s. But it was a stranger who stood by the table. And not a particularly prepossessing stranger. In the dim light of Ye Cosy Nooke, to which her opening eyes had not yet grown accustomed, all she could see of the man was that he was remarkably stout. She stiffened defensively. This was what a girl who sat about in tearooms alone had to expect.

“Hope I’m not late,” said the stranger, sitting down and breathing heavily. “I thought a little exercise would do me good, so I walked.”

Every nerve in Maud’s body seemed to come to life simultaneously. She tingled from head to foot. It was Geoffrey!

He was looking over his shoulder and endeavouring by snapping his fingers to attract the attention of the nearest distressed gentlewoman; and this gave Maud time to recover from the frightful shock she had received. Her dizziness left her; and, leaving, was succeeded by a panic dismay. This couldn’t be Geoffrey! It was outrageous that it should be Geoffrey! And yet it undeniably was Geoffrey. For a year she had prayed that Geoffrey might be given back to her, and the gods had heard her prayer. They had given her back Geoffrey, and with a careless generosity they had given her twice as much of him as she had expected. She had asked for the slim Apollo whom she had loved in Wales, and this colossal changeling had arrived in his stead.

===Chinese===

“你可来啦！”

莫德一惊，睁开了眼睛。那嗓音听着像杰弗里的。可是站在桌边的却是个陌生人。还不是个怎么讨人喜欢的陌生人。“舒心角落”灯光昏暗，她初睁的双眼还没适应过来，只看出这人胖得可观。她浑身一僵，摆出自卫的架势。一个姑娘家独自坐在茶室里，等来的原就该是这种事。

“希望我没迟到，”陌生人说着坐下来，呼哧呼哧直喘气。“我想着稍稍活动活动对我有好处，所以走着来的。”

莫德浑身的神经仿佛同时活了过来。她从头顶到脚尖一阵酥麻。是杰弗里！

他正扭头望着背后，打着一串响指，想招引离得最近的那位落难闺秀的注意；这倒给了莫德工夫，从她受的那记骇人的震骇里缓过神来。眩晕退去了；退去之后，接踵而来的是一片惊慌的颓丧。这不可能是杰弗里！这要是杰弗里，也太岂有此理了！然而这千真万确就是杰弗里。一年来她祷告上苍，求上天把杰弗里还给她，诸神听见了她的祷告。他们把杰弗里还给了她，而且以一种漫不经心的慷慨，给了她双份——比她指望的多出一倍。她求的是她在威尔士爱过的那具瘦削的阿波罗，来的却是这么个庞然巨物的调包货。

===Original===

We all of us have our prejudices. Maud had a prejudice against fat men. It may have been the spectacle of her brother Percy, bulging more and more every year she had known him, that had caused this kink in her character. At any rate, it existed, and she gazed in sickened silence at Geoffrey. He had turned again now, and she was enabled to get a full and complete view of him. He was not merely stout. He was gross. The slim figure which had haunted her for a year had spread into a sea of waistcoat. The keen lines of his face had disappeared altogether. His cheeks were pink jellies.

One of the distressed gentlewomen had approached with a slow disdain, and was standing by the table, brooding on the corpse upstairs. It seemed a shame to bother her.

“Tea or chocolate?” she inquired proudly.

“Tea, please,” said Maud, finding her voice.

“One tea,” sighed the mourner.

===Chinese===

咱们人人都有各自的偏见。莫德的偏见是嫌恶胖子。也许是她哥哥珀西——打她认识他起，他一年鼓过一年——那副景象在她性情里拧出了这个疙瘩。反正这偏见是有的，她默默看着杰弗里，直看得心里发腻。这会儿他又转过身来，她得以把他从头到脚饱览无遗。他岂止是壮实，他是痴肥。那副萦绕她整整一年的瘦削身形，已经漫成了一片马甲的海。脸上一道道利落的线条消失净尽。两腮是两团粉红的肉冻。

一位落难闺秀已经带着一份缓慢的倨傲踱了过来，立在桌旁，心里还惦记着楼上那具遗体。去搅扰她似乎有些造孽。

“茶还是巧克力？”她倨傲地问。

“劳驾，来杯茶，”莫德找回了声音。

“一份茶，”送葬人叹道。

===Original===

“Chocolate for me,” said Geoffrey briskly, with the air of one discoursing on a congenial topic. “I’d like plenty of whipped cream. And please see that it’s hot.”

“One chocolate.”

Geoffrey pondered. This was no light matter that occupied him.

“And bring some fancy cakes﻿—I like the ones with icing on them﻿—and some teacake and buttered toast. Please see there’s plenty of butter on it.”

Maud shivered. This man before her was a man in whose lexicon there should have been no such word as butter, a man who should have called for the police had some enemy endeavoured to thrust butter upon him.

“Well,” said Geoffrey leaning forward, as the haughty ministrant drifted away, “you haven’t changed a bit. To look at, I mean.”

===Chinese===

“我要巧克力，”杰弗里爽利地说，那神情仿佛在谈论一件正中下怀的题目。“鲜奶油要多多的。还有，请务必弄热。”

“一份巧克力。”

杰弗里沉吟起来。占据他心头的可不是件小事。

“再拿来几样花色蛋糕——我喜欢上头有糖霜的那种——再来一块茶点蛋糕、几片黄油吐司。请务必让吐司上多放黄油。”

莫德打了个寒噤。她眼前这个人，他的词典里本不该有黄油这个词；倘若有哪个仇人胆敢把黄油硬塞到他跟前，此人理应立刻喊警察。

“呃，”趁那位倨傲的侍茶人飘然走开，杰弗里探身向前，“你一点儿没变。我是说，瞧模样。”

===Original===

“No?” said Maud.

“You’re just the same. I think I”﻿—he squinted down at his waistcoat﻿—“have put on a little weight. I don’t know if you notice it?”

Maud shivered again. He thought he had put on a little weight, and didn’t know if she had noticed it! She was oppressed by the eternal melancholy miracle of the fat man who does not realize that he has become fat.

“It was living on the yacht that put me a little out of condition,” said Geoffrey. “I was on the yacht nearly all the time since I saw you last. The old boy had a Japanese cook and lived pretty high. It was apoplexy that got him. We had a great time touring about. We were on the Mediterranean all last winter, mostly at Nice.”

“I should like to go to Nice,” said Maud, for something to say. She was feeling that it was not only externally that Geoffrey had changed. Or had he in reality always been like this, commonplace and prosaic, and was it merely in her imagination that he had been wonderful?

“If you ever go,” said Geoffrey, earnestly, “don’t fail to lunch at the Hotel Côte d’Azur. They give you the most amazing selection of *hors d’oeuvres* you ever saw. Crayfish as big as baby lobsters! And there’s a fish﻿—I’ve forgotten it’s name, it’ll come back to me﻿—that’s just like the Florida pompano. Be careful to have it broiled, not fried. Otherwise you lose the flavour. Tell the waiter you must have it broiled, with melted butter and a little parsley and some plain boiled potatoes. It’s really astonishing. It’s best to stick to fish on the Continent. People can say what they like, but I maintain that the French don’t really understand steaks or any sort of red meat. The veal isn’t bad, though I prefer our way of serving it. Of course, what the French are real geniuses at is the omelet. I remember, when we put in at Toulon for coal, I went ashore for a stroll, and had the most delicious omelet with chicken livers beautifully cooked, at quite a small, unpretentious place near the harbour. I shall always remember it.”

===Chinese===

“是吗？”莫德说。

“你还是老样子。我想我”——他斜眼瞟着自己的马甲——“胖了一小点儿。不知道你看出来没有？”

莫德又打了个寒噤。他觉得自己不过胖了一小点儿，还不知道她看出来没有！胖人而不自知其胖——这桩永恒的忧郁奇迹压得她透不过气来。

“在游艇上住着，把我的身子骨搞糟了一点点，”杰弗里说。“自打上回见你，我几乎一直在游艇上。那位老先生雇了个日本厨子，日子过得很阔。他末了是中风去的。我们四下里游览，快活极了。去年一冬都在地中海，多半待在尼斯（Nice）。”

“我倒想去尼斯呢，”莫德说，没话找话。她觉得杰弗里变的不光是外表。又或者他其实一向就是这么个人，平庸又乏味，他的妙不可言只存在于她的想象里？

“你要是去的话，”杰弗里恳切地说，“千万别错过蔚蓝海岸饭店（Hotel Côte d’Azur）的午饭。他们的开胃冷盘花样之多，包你平生未见。小龙虾大得跟龙虾崽儿似的！还有那么一种鱼——名字我忘了，会想起来的——跟佛罗里达鲳鲹（Florida pompano）一模一样。千万记着要烤不要炸，一炸鲜味就没了。只管跟堂倌说，非烤不可，配化开的黄油、一点欧芹、再加几只清水煮土豆。那味道真叫绝。到了欧洲大陆，还是盯着鱼吃最稳妥。人家爱怎么说随他们说去，我可认定，法国人压根儿不懂牛排，什么红肉都不懂。小牛肉倒还不坏，虽然我更中意咱们那种做法。当然啦，法国人真正称得上天才的是摊鸡蛋。我记得有一回我们进土伦（Toulon）港加煤，我上岸遛弯儿，在港口附近一家毫不起眼的小铺子里吃了一份美味无比的摊鸡蛋，鸡肝煎得漂亮极了。我这辈子忘不了它。”

===Original===

The mourner returned, bearing a laden tray, from which she removed the funeral bakemeats and placed them limply on the table. Geoffrey shook his head, annoyed.

“I particularly asked for plenty of butter on my toast!” he said. “I hate buttered toast if there isn’t lots of butter. It isn’t worth eating. Get me a couple of pats, will you, and I’ll spread it myself. Do hurry, please, before the toast gets cold. It’s no good if the toast gets cold. They don’t understand tea as a meal at these places,” he said to Maud, as the mourner withdrew. “You have to go to the country to appreciate the real thing. I remember we lay off Lyme Regis down Devonshire way, for a few days, and I went and had tea at a farmhouse there. It was quite amazing! Thick Devonshire cream and homemade jam and cakes of every kind. This sort of thing here is just a farce. I do wish that woman would make haste with that butter. It’ll be too late in a minute.”

Maud sipped her tea in silence. Her heart was like lead within her. The recurrence of the butter theme as a sort of leitmotif in her companion’s conversation was fraying her nerves till she felt she could endure little more. She cast her mind’s eye back over the horrid months and had a horrid vision of Geoffrey steadily absorbing butter, day after day, week after week﻿—ever becoming more and more of a human keg. She shuddered.

Indignation at the injustice of Fate in causing her to give her heart to a man and then changing him into another and quite different man fought with a cold terror, which grew as she realized more and more clearly the magnitude of the mistake she had made. She felt that she must escape. And yet how could she escape? She had definitely pledged herself to this man. (“Ah!” cried Geoffrey gaily, as the pats of butter arrived. “That’s more like it!” He began to smear the toast. Maud averted her eyes.) She had told him that she loved him, that he was the whole world to her, that there never would be anyone else. He had come to claim her. How could she refuse him just because he was about thirty pounds overweight?

===Chinese===

送葬人回来了，端着一只满满的托盘，把那几样丧仪点心从盘里卸下来，有气无力地摆上桌。杰弗里恼得直摇头。

“我明明特意交代过，吐司上要多放黄油！”他说。“黄油不够多的黄油吐司，我恨死了。不值一吃。给我拿两小块来，成不成？我自己抹。千万请快些，趁吐司还没凉。吐司一凉就完蛋了。这些地方根本不懂下午茶是一顿正经饭，”送葬人退下去时，他对莫德说。“要领教真材实料，你得下乡。我记得我们的船在莱姆里吉斯（Lyme Regis）外头——德文郡那一片——停泊了几天，我上那儿一家农舍去喝茶。了不得！厚厚的德文郡奶油，自家熬的果酱，各式各样的糕点。眼前这路货色纯粹是胡闹。就盼着那女人赶紧把黄油送来。再过一分钟就晚啦。”

莫德默默啜着茶。她的心沉得像铅。黄油这个话题像一支主导动机（leitmotif），在同伴的谈吐里反复奏鸣，磨得她神经发毛，只觉得自己再撑不了多久了。她把心灵的眼睛投回那几个月可怕的时光，看见一幅可怕的景象：杰弗里一天又一天、一星期又一星期，稳定地吸收着黄油，一天比一天更像一只人形的酒桶。她哆嗦了一下。

命运不公，先叫她把一颗心交给了一个男人，随后又把他调换成另一个截然不同的男人——这份愤懑，同一股寒丝丝的恐惧在心里交战；那恐惧随着她越看越清楚自己铸下的错有多大多深，一步步涨了起来。她觉得自己非逃走不可。可是逃得掉吗？她分明已经把自身许给了这个人。（“啊！”两小块黄油端到时，杰弗里欢天喜地地嚷道。“这才像话嘛！”他动手往吐司上抹起来。莫德把眼睛挪开了。）她对他讲过，她爱他，他是她的整个世界，她这辈子再不会有别人。他是来认领她的。她怎么能就因为他超重了三十来磅，就把他回绝了呢？

===Original===

Geoffrey finished his meal. He took out a cigarette. (“No smoking, please!” said the distressed gentlewoman.) He put the cigarette back in its case. There was a new expression in his eyes now, a tender expression. For the first time since they had met Maud seemed to catch a far-off glimpse of the man she had loved in Wales. Butter appeared to have softened Geoffrey.

“So you couldn’t wait!” he said with pathos.

Maud did not understand.

“I waited over a quarter of an hour. It was you who were late.”

“I don’t mean that. I am referring to your engagement. I saw the announcement in the Morning Post. Well, I hope you will let me offer you my best wishes. This Mr. George Bevan, whoever he is, is lucky.”

===Chinese===

杰弗里吃完了。他掏出一支香烟。（“请勿吸烟！”落难闺秀说。）他把香烟放回烟盒。这会儿他眼睛里有了一种新的神情，一种温柔的神情。自打重逢以来，莫德仿佛头一回远远瞥见了她在威尔士爱过的那个人的影子。黄油看来把杰弗里泡软了。

“这么说，你没能等哪！”他说得凄凄惨惨。

莫德没听懂。

“我等了一刻多钟。迟到的可是你。”

“我不是说这个。我说的是你订婚的事。《晨邮报》上的启事我看见了。那么，请容我道一声衷心的祝愿。这位乔治·贝文先生，且不论他是何许人也，运气是好的。”

===Original===

Maud had opened her mouth to explain, to say that it was all a mistake. She closed it again without speaking.

“So you couldn’t wait!” proceeded Geoffrey with gentle regret. “Well, I suppose I ought not to blame you. You are at an age when it is easy to forget. I had no right to hope that you would be proof against a few months’ separation. I expected too much. But it is ironical, isn’t it! There was I, thinking always of those days last summer when we were everything to each other, while you had forgotten me﻿—Forgotten me!” sighed Geoffrey. He picked a fragment of cake absently off the tablecloth and inserted it in his mouth.

The unfairness of the attack stung Maud to speech. She looked back over the months, thought of all she had suffered, and ached with self-pity.

“I hadn’t,” she cried.

“You hadn’t? But you let this other man, this George Bevan, make love to you.”

===Chinese===

莫德张开口要解释，要说明那全是一场误会。她又把嘴闭上了，没有作声。

“这么说，你没能等哪！”杰弗里接着说下去，语调温文，带着惋惜。“唉，想来我不该怪你。你这个年纪，最容易忘事。我没有权利指望你挡得住几个月的别离。我期望得太多了。可这事儿够讽刺的，是不是！我呢，念念不忘去年夏天那些彼此就是对方一切的日子；你呢，已经把我忘了——把我忘了！”杰弗里叹息道。他心不在焉地从桌布上拈起一粒蛋糕屑，送进嘴里。

这番指责的不公，把莫德刺得开了口。她回望那几个月，想起自己受过的种种苦楚，自怜得生疼。

“我没有忘！”她嚷道。

“没有忘？可你让另一个男人，让这个乔治·贝文，对你大献殷勤来着。”

===Original===

“I didn’t! That was all a mistake.”

“A mistake?”

“Yes. It would take too long to explain, but﻿ ﻿…” She stopped. It had come to her suddenly, in a flash of clear vision, that the mistake was one which she had no desire to correct. She felt like one who, lost in a jungle, comes out after long wandering into the open air. For days she had been thinking confusedly, unable to interpret her own emotions: and now everything had abruptly become clarified. It was as if the sight of Geoffrey had been the key to a cipher. She loved George Bevan, the man she had sent out of her life forever. She knew it now, and the shock of realization made her feel faint and helpless. And, mingled with the shock of realization, there came to her the mortification of knowing that her aunt, Lady Caroline, and her brother, Percy, had been right after all. What she had mistaken for the love of a lifetime had been, as they had so often insisted, a mere infatuation, unable to survive the spectacle of a Geoffrey who had been eating too much butter and had put on flesh.

Geoffrey swallowed his piece of cake, and bent forward.

“Aren’t you engaged to this man Bevan?”

===Chinese===

“我没有！那全是一场误会。”

“误会？”

“是的。解释起来话就长了，可是……”她收住了话头。刹那间她心头雪亮：这个误会，她根本不想去纠正。她感觉自己像在丛林里迷了路的人，长久的跋涉之后，一头走出了露天敞亮之地。这些天来她一直昏昏沉沉地想，理不清自己的心绪；这会儿一切陡然澄澈了。仿佛一见杰弗里，就是解开暗码的那把钥匙。她爱乔治·贝文，爱那个被她永远打发出了自己人生的男人。此刻她明白了，明白过来的震波震得她头昏腿软。而同这震波搅在一起的，还有一桩难堪：她的姑母卡罗琳夫人，还有她哥哥珀西，毕竟是没有说错的。她错当成终身之爱的东西，正如他们千遍万遍咬定的那样，只是一阵痴迷，一见杰弗里吃了太多黄油、添了那么多膘的模样，便再也活不下去了。

杰弗里把那口蛋糕咽下去，探身向前。

“你不是已经同这个贝文订了婚？”

===Original===

Maud avoided his eye. She was aware that the crisis had arrived, and that her whole future hung on her next words.

And then Fate came to her rescue. Before she could speak, there was an interruption.

“Pardon me,” said a voice. “One moment!”

So intent had Maud and her companion been on their own affairs that neither of them observed the entrance of a third party. This was a young man with mouse-coloured hair and a freckled, badly-shaven face which seemed undecided whether to be furtive or impudent. He had small eyes, and his costume was a blend of the flashy and the shabby. He wore a bowler hat, tilted a little rakishly to one side, and carried a small bag, which he rested on the table between them.

“Sorry to intrude, miss.” He bowed gallantly to Maud, “but I want to have a few words with Mr. Spenser Gray here.”

===Chinese===

莫德躲开他的目光。她意识到关头已到，她的整个未来就悬在她接下去的那句话上。

就在这时，命运前来搭救她了。她还没来得及开口，岔子来了。

“对不起，”一个声音说。“借个光，稍等片刻！”

莫德和她的同伴一门心思扑在自己的事上，谁也没有留心进来了第三个人。这是个头发鼠灰的年轻人，满脸雀斑，胡子刮得潦草，那张脸拿不定主意是要贼头贼脑还是要嬉皮无赖。眼睛小小的，行头是浮华和寒酸的合金。他头顶一顶圆顶礼帽，歪歪地翘向一边，透着几分浪荡气，手里拎着一只小提包，搁在两人之间的桌上。

“冒昧打扰了，小姐。”他向莫德殷勤地一躬身，“我要同这位斯宾塞·格雷先生讲几句话。”

===Original===

Maud, looking across at Geoffrey, was surprised to see that his florid face had lost much of its colour. His mouth was open, and his eyes had taken a glassy expression.

“I think you have made a mistake,” she said coldly. She disliked the young man at sight. “This is Mr. Raymond.”

Geoffrey found speech.

“Of course I’m Mr. Raymond!” he cried angrily. “What do you mean by coming and annoying us like this?”

The young man was not discomposed. He appeared to be used to being unpopular. He proceeded as though there had been no interruption. He produced a dingy card.

===Chinese===

莫德望向对面的杰弗里，吃惊地发现那张红润的脸失了大半颜色。他嘴张着，眼睛里蒙上一层呆滞的玻璃光。

“我想您认错人了，”她冷冷地说。她一眼就讨厌这个年轻人。“这位是雷蒙德先生。”

杰弗里找回了话。

“我当然是雷蒙德先生！”他怒声喝道。“你这么跑来搅扰我们，是什么意思？”

年轻人毫不动摇。看样子，他惯于不得人心。他照旧往下进行，仿佛那番打岔不曾发生过。他摸出一张脏兮兮的名片。

===Original===

“Glance at that,” he said. “Messrs. Willoughby and Son, Solicitors. I’m son. The guv’nor put this little matter into my hands. I’ve been looking for you for days, Mr. Gray, to hand you this paper.” He opened the bag like a conjurer performing a trick, and brought out a stiff document of legal aspect. “You’re a witness, miss, that I’ve served the papers. You know what this is, of course?” he said to Geoffrey. “Action for breach of promise of marriage. Our client, Miss Yvonne Sinclair, of the Regal Theatre, is suing you for ten thousand pounds. And, if you ask me,” said the young man with genial candour, dropping the professional manner, “I don’t mind telling you, I think it’s a walkover! It’s the best little action for breach we’ve handled for years.” He became professional again. “Your lawyers will no doubt communicate with us in due course. And, if you take my advice,” he concluded, with another of his swift changes of manner, “you’ll get ’em to settle out of court, for, between me and you and the lamppost, you haven’t an earthly!”

Geoffrey had started to his feet. He was puffing with outraged innocence.

“What the devil do you mean by this?” he demanded. “Can’t you see you’ve made a mistake? My name is not Gray. This lady has told you that I am Geoffrey Raymond!”

“Makes it all the worse for you,” said the young man imperturbably, “making advances to our client under an assumed name. We’ve got letters and witnesses and the whole bag of tricks. And how about this photo?” He dived into the bag again. “Do you recognize that, miss?”

===Chinese===

“您瞧一眼，”他说。“威洛比父子律师事务所（Messrs. Willoughby and Son, Solicitors）。我就是那位‘子’。这件事儿，老头子交到了我手里。格雷先生，我找您找了有好几天了，就为把这份文书送到您手上。”他像变戏法的亮家伙什似的打开提包，抽出一份硬挺挺、一副法律面孔的文书。“小姐，我文书送达了，请您做个见证。您当然知道这是什么？”他对杰弗里说。“毁弃婚约之诉。我们的当事人、雷加尔剧院的伊冯娜·辛克莱小姐（Miss Yvonne Sinclair），向您索赔一万英镑。而且，您要问我的话，”年轻人收起公事公办的腔调，带着一团和气的坦率说，“我不妨对您直说，我看这官司是稳赢的！这是我们这些年经手的最漂亮的一桩毁约小案子。”他又公事公办起来。“您的律师想必自会适时同我们接洽。还有，您要听我一句劝，”他收梢时又是一副急转直下的嘴脸，“就让律师庭外和解了吧，因为这话只在咱俩外加灯柱之间说说：您半点胜算也没有！”

杰弗里已经一跃而起。他呼哧呼哧地喘着，满腔是无辜遭人构陷的义愤。

“你这一手究竟是什么意思？”他喝问。“你看不出你认错人了？我不姓格雷。这位女士已经告诉你了，我是杰弗里·雷蒙德！”

“那您罪加一等，”年轻人不为所动，“用假名勾搭我们的当事人。信件、证人，全套家伙什儿，我们一应俱全。那这张照片呢？”他又一头扎进提包。“您认得这个吗，小姐？”

===Original===

Maud looked at the photograph. It was unmistakably Geoffrey. And it had evidently been taken recently, for it showed the later Geoffrey, the man of substance. It was a full-length photograph and across the stout legs was written in a flowing hand the legend, “To Babe from her little Pootles.” Maud gave a shudder and handed it back to the young man, just as Geoffrey, reaching across the table, made a grab for it.

“I recognize it,” she said.

Mr. Willoughby junior packed the photograph away in his bag, and turned to go.

“That’s all for today, then, I think,” he said, affably.

He bowed again in his courtly way, tilted the hat a little more to the left, and, having greeted one of the distressed gentlewomen who loitered limply in his path with a polite “If you please, Mabel!” which drew upon him a freezing stare of which he seemed oblivious, he passed out, leaving behind him strained silence.

===Chinese===

莫德端详那张照片。错不了，正是杰弗里。而且分明是不久前拍的，因为照片上是后来的杰弗里，这位添了分量的人物。一张全身照，粗壮的两腿上横着一行流转的花体字：“赠贝比——她的小普特尔（Pootles）。”莫德打了个寒噤，把照片递还给年轻人，恰在这时，杰弗里隔着桌子探过身来，一把要抓。

“我认得，”她说。

小威洛比先生把照片收进提包，转身要走。

“那么今天，我想就到这儿吧，”他和颜悦色地说。

他又照他那副彬彬有礼的派头鞠了一躬，把帽子又往左歪了歪，迎面一个落难闺秀没精打采地荡在他道上，他和和气气地招呼了一声“借光，玛贝尔（Mabel）！”，换来一道冰封的怒视，而他似乎浑然不觉；随后他便出了门，留下一片绷得紧紧的静默。

===Original===

Maud was the first to break it.

“I think I’ll be going,” she said.

The words seemed to rouse her companion from his stupor.

“Let me explain!”

“There’s nothing to explain.”

“It was just a﻿ ﻿… it was just a passing﻿ ﻿… It was nothing﻿ ﻿… nothing.”

===Chinese===

头一个打破这静默的是莫德。

“我想我该走了，”她说。

这句话仿佛把她那位同伴从木僵里震醒了。

“容我解释！”

“没什么可解释的。”

“那不过是……那不过是一时……那算不了什么……什么也算不上。”

===Original===

“Pootles!” murmured Maud.

Geoffrey followed her as she moved to the door.

“Be reasonable!” pleaded Geoffrey. “Men aren’t saints! It was nothing!﻿ ﻿… Are you going to end﻿ ﻿… everything﻿ ﻿… just because I lost my head?”

Maud looked at him with a smile. She was conscious of an overwhelming relief. The dim interior of Ye Cosy Nooke no longer seemed depressing. She could have kissed this unknown “Babe” whose businesslike action had enabled her to close a regrettable chapter in her life with a clear conscience.

“But you haven’t only lost your head, Geoffrey,” she said. “You’ve lost your figure as well.”

===Chinese===

“普特尔！”莫德喃喃道。

她朝门口走去，杰弗里跟在后面。

“讲点道理吧！”杰弗里央告道。“男人又不是圣徒！那算不了什么！……难道你要为了一时糊涂，就把……一切都……了结吗？”

莫德含笑望着他。她只觉一阵排山倒海的轻松。“舒心角落”昏暗的堂奥不再叫人压抑了。她真想去亲一亲那位素不相识的“贝比”——多亏她办事雷厉风行，莫德才能问心无愧地合上自己人生中这令人遗憾的一章。

“可是杰弗里，你丢掉的不光是头脑，”她说。“你还丢掉了身段。”

===Original===

She went out quickly. With a convulsive bound Geoffrey started to follow her, but was checked before he had gone a yard.

There are formalities to be observed before a patron can leave Ye Cosy Nooke.

“If you please!” said a distressed gentlewomanly voice.

The lady whom Mr. Willoughby had addressed as Mabel﻿—erroneously, for her name was Ernestine﻿—was standing beside him with a slip of paper.

“Six and twopence,” said Ernestine.

===Chinese===

她快步走了出去。杰弗里浑身一抽，纵身要跟上去，却在一码还没走满的时候就被拦住了。

在“舒心角落”，主顾离店之前，是有若干手续要履行的。

“劳驾您！”一个落难闺秀的嗓音说。

方才威洛比先生唤作玛贝尔的那位女士——叫错了，她名叫欧内斯廷（Ernestine）——正拿着一纸单子立在他身旁。

“六先令两便士，”欧内斯廷说。

===Original===

For a moment this appalling statement drew the unhappy man’s mind from the main issue.

“Six and twopence for a cup of chocolate and a few cakes?” he cried, aghast. “It’s robbery!”

“Six and twopence, please!” said the queen of the bandits with undisturbed calm. She had been through this sort of thing before. Ye Cosy Nooke did not get many customers; but it made the most of those it did get.

“Here!” Geoffrey produced a half-sovereign. “I haven’t time to argue!”

The distressed brigand showed no gratification. She had the air of one who is aloof from worldly things. All she wanted was rest and leisure﻿—leisure to meditate upon the body upstairs. All flesh is as grass. We are here today and gone tomorrow. But there, beyond the grave, is peace.

===Chinese===

这骇人的账目一时把不幸的人的心思从正题上拽开了。

“一杯巧克力、几块蛋糕，要六先令两便士？”他叫起来，吓了一跳。“这是明抢！”

“六先令两便士，劳驾！”强盗们的女王处变不惊，安然道。这一路场面她见得多了。“舒心角落”的主顾本就不多；可对上门的每一位，它都物尽其用。

“给！”杰弗里摸出一枚半镑金币。“我没工夫争！”

落难的匪徒并无得色。她一副超然于尘世之外的神情。她要的只是安息和闲暇——用来默想楼上那具遗体的闲暇。凡有血气的尽都如草。今日尚在此世，明日便已消逝。唯有坟墓那一边，才有安宁。

===Original===

“Your change?” she said.

“Damn the change!”

“You are forgetting your hat.”

“Damn my hat!”

===Chinese===

“找头呢？”她说。

“见鬼的找头！”

“您的帽子忘了。”

“见鬼的帽子！”

===Original===

Geoffrey dashed from the room. He heaved his body through the door. He lumbered down the stairs.

Out in Bond Street the traffic moved up and the traffic moved down. Strollers strolled upon the sidewalks.

But Maud had gone.

===Chinese===

杰弗里冲出屋子。他把身躯从门洞里挤搡出去，蹬蹬地笨重奔下楼梯。

外面邦德街上，车流上行，车流下行。人行道上，闲人自顾闲逛。

可是莫德已经走了。

## XXVII / XXVII

===Original===

In his bedroom at the Carlton Hotel George Bevan was packing. That is to say, he had begun packing; but for the last twenty minutes he had been sitting on the side of the bed, staring into a future which became bleaker and bleaker the more he examined it. In the last two days he had been no stranger to these grey moods, and they had become harder and harder to dispel. Now, with the steamer-trunk before him gaping to receive its contents, he gave himself up wholeheartedly to gloom.

Somehow the steamer-trunk, with all that it implied of partings and voyagings, seemed to emphasize the fact that he was going out alone into an empty world. Soon he would be on board the liner, every revolution of whose engines would be taking him farther away from where his heart would always be. There were moments when the torment of this realization became almost physical.

It was incredible that three short weeks ago he had been a happy man. Lonely, perhaps, but only in a vague, impersonal way. Not lonely with this aching loneliness that tortured him now. What was there left for him? As regards any triumphs which the future might bring in connection with his work, he was, as Mac the stage-door keeper had said, “blarzy.” Any success he might have would be but a stale repetition of other successes which he had achieved. He would go on working, of course, but﻿—. The ringing of the telephone bell across the room jerked him back to the present. He got up with a muttered malediction. Someone calling up again from the theatre probably. They had been doing it all the time since he had announced his intention of leaving for America by Saturday’s boat.

===Chinese===

卡尔顿饭店的房间里，乔治·贝文正在收拾行李。也就是说，他已经开了个头；可是最近这二十分钟，他一直坐在床沿上，凝望着自己的前程——越凝望，前程越是惨淡。这两天来，这类灰暗的心绪他已不算陌生，而且一时比一时更难驱散。此刻，面前那口远航衣箱（steamer-trunk）张着大口，专候收纳什物，他便索性一门心思扎进了愁云惨雾里。

不知怎么，这口衣箱连同它所暗示的一切离别与远航，似乎格外衬出一个事实：他就要孤零零地走进一个空荡荡的世界。用不了多久，他就会登上那艘邮轮；轮机每转动一圈，都把他带得离那片他这颗心要求远栖居的地方更远一分。有些时刻，这层领悟带来的煎熬几乎成了皮肉之痛。

短短三个星期之前，他还是个快乐的人——这简直叫人难以相信。也许孤单，但那不过是一种模糊的、无对象的孤单；绝不是眼下这种隐隐作痛、折磨得他坐立不安的孤单。往后的日子，他还剩下什么呢？至于工作上将来可能取得的种种成就——用后台门房麦克的话说——他早已“发痴”了。就算再有成功，也无非是把旧日的成功陈年重演一遍。当然，活儿他还是会接着干的，可是——。房间那头电话铃炸响，把他猛地拽回眼前。他嘟囔着一句咒骂，站起身来。多半又是剧院里打来的。打从他宣布打算乘星期六的班船回美国起，这类电话就没消停过。

===Original===

“Hello?” he said wearily.

“Is that George?” asked a voice. It seemed familiar, but all female voices sound the same over the telephone.

“This is George,” he replied. “Who are you?”

“Don’t you know my voice?”

“I do not.”

“You’ll know it quite well before long. I’m a great talker.”

===Chinese===

“哈罗？”他有气无力地说。

“是乔治吗？”一个声音问。听着耳熟，可电话里的女声，听上去全都一个样。

“我是乔治，”他答道。“你是谁？”

“你听不出我的声音？”

“听不出。”

“要不了多久，你就听得烂熟了。我这个人，顶爱说话。”

===Original===

“Is that Billie?”

“It is not Billie, whoever Billie may be. I am female, George.”

“So is Billie.”

“Well, you had better run through the list of your feminine friends till you reach me.”

===Chinese===

“是比莉吗？”

“不是比莉，管她比莉是哪一位。我是女的，乔治。”

“比莉也是女的。”

“那你最好把女性朋友的名册从头数一遍，数到我为止。”

===Original===

“I haven’t any feminine friends.”

“None?”

“No.”

“That’s odd.”

“Why?”

“You told me in the garden two nights ago that you looked on me as a pal.”

===Chinese===

“我没有什么女性朋友。”

“一个没有？”

“没有。”

“这就怪了。”

“怪在哪儿？”

“两天前的晚上，你在花园里亲口说过，你拿我当哥们儿看待。”

===Original===

George sat down abruptly. He felt boneless.

“Is﻿—is that you?” he stammered. “It can’t be﻿—Maud!”

“How clever of you to guess. George, I want to ask you one or two things. In the first place, are you fond of butter?”

George blinked. This was not a dream. He had just bumped his knee against the corner of the telephone table, and it still hurt most convincingly. He needed the evidence to assure himself that he was awake.

===Chinese===

乔治猛地坐倒下去。他只觉得浑身没了骨头。

“是——是你吗？”他结结巴巴地说。“不会吧——莫德！”

“猜中了，真聪明。乔治，我想请教你一两件事。头一件：你爱吃黄油吗？”

乔治眨了眨眼。这不是做梦。他方才膝盖刚磕上电话桌的桌角，这会儿还疼得实实在在。他正需要这样的证据，好叫自己相信是醒着的。

===Original===

“Butter?” he queried. “What do you mean?”

“Oh, well, if you don’t even know what butter means, I expect it’s all right. What is your weight, George?”

“About a hundred and eighty pounds. But I don’t understand.”

“Wait a minute.” There was a silence at the other end of the wire. “About thirteen stone,” said Maud’s voice. “I’ve been doing it in my head. And what was it this time last year?”

“About the same, I think. I always weigh about the same.”

===Chinese===

“黄油？”他问。“什么意思？”

“哦，好，要是你连黄油都不知道是什么，那我想多半还过得去。你的体重是多少，乔治？”

“一百八十磅上下。可我不明白。”

“等一等。”电线那一头静了片刻。“约合十三英石，”莫德的声音说。“我在心里换算的。去年这个时候呢？”

“我想差不多。我的体重向来变化不大。”

===Original===

“How wonderful! George!”

“Yes?”

“This is very important. Have you ever been in Florida?”

“I was there one winter.”

===Chinese===

“妙极了！乔治！”

“嗯？”

“这可是顶要紧的。你去过佛罗里达没有？”

“有一个冬天在那儿待过。”

===Original===

“Do you know a fish called the pompano?”

“Yes.”

“Tell me about it.”

“How do you mean? It’s just a fish. You eat it.”

“I know. Go into details.”

“There aren’t any details. You just eat it.”

===Chinese===

“你认不认得一种叫鲳鲹（pompano）的鱼？”

“认得。”

“给我讲讲。”

“你什么意思？它就是一种鱼。拿来吃的。”

“我知道。讲细一点。”

“没有什么细节。就是拿来吃。”

===Original===

The voice at the other end of the wire purred with approval. “I never heard anything so splendid. The last man who mentioned pompano to me became absolutely lyrical about sprigs of parsley and melted butter. Well, that’s that. Now, here’s another very important point. How about wallpaper?”

George pressed his unoccupied hand against his forehead. This conversation was unnerving him.

“I didn’t get that,” he said.

“Didn’t get what?”

===Chinese===

电话线那头的声音满意地打起了呼噜。“我从没听过这么叫人满意的回答。上一个同我提起鲳鲹的男人，一开口就欧芹小枝、黄油化开，抒情抒得一塌糊涂。好，这就算问完了。眼下还有一件顶要紧的事。壁纸怎么样？”

乔治把空着的那只手按在额头上。这场谈话正叫他心神涣散。

“我没听清，”他说。

“没听清什么？”

===Original===

“I mean, I didn’t quite catch what you said that time. It sounded to me like ‘What about wallpaper?’ ”

“It was ‘What about wallpaper?’ Why not?”

“But,” said George weakly, “it doesn’t make any sense.”

“Oh, but it does. I mean, what about wallpaper for your den?”

“My den?”

“Your den. You must have a den. Where do you suppose you’re going to work, if you don’t? Now, my idea would be some nice quiet grass-cloth. And, of course, you would have lots of pictures and books. And a photograph of me. I’ll go and be taken specially. Then there would be a piano for you to work on, and two or three really comfortable chairs. And﻿—well, that would be about all, wouldn’t it?”

===Chinese===

“我是说，您方才那一句，我没大听清。听着像是‘壁纸怎么样？’”

“本来就是‘壁纸怎么样？’怎么，有什么不对？”

“可是，”乔治声气虚弱地说，“这话没头没脑呀。”

“哦，有头有脑。我是说，你那间小书房的壁纸，打算怎么办？”

“我的小书房？”

“你的小书房。你非有一间小书房不可。不然你琢磨琢磨，你打算在哪儿干活？要我说，糊一种素净的草编壁纸就好。当然，屋里还得挂好多画、摆好多书。再摆一张我的照片，我特地去拍一张。然后给你备一架钢琴，干活用的；再来两三把真正舒服的椅子。还有——嗯，大致也就齐了，你说是不是？”

===Original===

George pulled himself together.

“Hello!” he said.

“Why do you say ‘Hello’?”

“I forgot I was in London. I should have said ‘Are you there?’ ”

“Yes, I’m here.”

===Chinese===

乔治把心神拢了拢。

“哈罗！”他说。

“你说‘哈罗’做什么？”

“我忘了自己是在伦敦。该说‘喂，在吗’才是。”

“对，我在。”

===Original===

“Well, then, what does it all mean?”

“What does what mean?”

“What you’ve been saying﻿—about butter and pompanos and wallpaper and my den and all that? I don’t understand.”

“How stupid of you! I was asking you what sort of wallpaper you would like in your den after we were married and settled down.”

George dropped the receiver. It clashed against the side of the table. He groped for it blindly.

===Chinese===

“那么，这一套到底是什么意思？”

“什么什么意思？”

“您方才说的那些——什么黄油、鲳鲹、壁纸、我的小书房，那一套？我听不懂。”

“你可真迟钝！我问的是，等我们结了婚、安顿下来，你那间小书房想要什么样的壁纸。”

乔治手里的听筒掉了，哐当一声磕在桌沿上。他伸出手去，一通乱摸。

===Original===

“Hello!” he said.

“Don’t say ‘Hello!’ It sounds so abrupt!”

“What did you say then?”

“I said ‘Don’t say Hello!’ ”

“No, before that! Before that! You said something about getting married.”

===Chinese===

“哈罗！”他说。

“别说‘哈罗’！听着太唐突啦！”

“那您方才说什么？”

“我说的是‘别说哈罗’！”

“不，再往前！再往前！你说到结婚来着。”

===Original===

“Well, aren’t we going to get married? Our engagement is announced in the *Morning Post*.”

“But﻿—But﻿—”

“George!” Maud’s voice shook. “Don’t tell me you are going to jilt me!” she said tragically. “Because, if you are, let me know in time, as I shall want to bring an action for breach of promise. I’ve just met such a capable young man who will look after the whole thing for me. He wears a bowler hat on the side of his head and calls waitresses ‘Mabel.’ Answer ‘yes’ or ‘no.’ Will you marry me?”

===Chinese===

“咦，我们不是要结婚吗？我们的订婚启事，《晨邮报》上都登出来了。”

“可是——可是——”

“乔治！”莫德的声音发颤。“别告诉我你要把我甩了！”她悲悲切切地说。“因为，你要真有这个意思，务必趁早知会我一声，我好提起毁弃婚约的诉讼。我刚结识了一位顶能干、顶能干的年轻先生，整桩事情全包在他身上。他歪戴着圆顶礼帽，管女侍应一概叫‘玛贝尔’。回答‘是’或‘不是’。你肯娶我吗？”

===Original===

“But﻿—But﻿—how about﻿—I mean, what about﻿—I mean how about﻿—?”

“Make up your mind what you do mean.”

“The other fellow!” gasped George.

A musical laugh was wafted to him over the wire.

===Chinese===

“可是——可是——那个——我是说，那个怎么办——我是说怎么办——？”

“先拿定主意，你到底指什么。”

“那另外那位先生呢！”乔治倒抽一口气。

一串音乐般悦耳的笑声顺着电线飘了过来。

===Original===

“What about him?”

“Well, what about him?” said George.

“Isn’t a girl allowed to change her mind?” said Maud.

George yelped excitedly. Maud gave a cry.

“Don’t sing!” she said. “You nearly made me deaf.”

===Chinese===

“那又怎么啦？”

“就是呀，那又怎么啦？”乔治说。

“姑娘家就不许改主意吗？”莫德说。

乔治乐得尖叫了一声。莫德也喊出了声。

“别唱啦！”她说。“你差点儿把我震聋了。”

===Original===

“Have you changed your mind?”

“Certainly I have!”

“And you really think﻿—You really want﻿—I mean, you really want﻿—You really think﻿—”

“Don’t be so incoherent!”

===Chinese===

“您当真改了主意？”

“当然改了！”

“您真的觉得——您真的想——我是说，您真的想——您真的觉得——”

“别这么语无伦次！”

===Original===

“Maud!”

“Well?”

“Will you marry me?”

“Of course I will.”

===Chinese===

“莫德！”

“怎么？”

“您肯嫁给我吗？”

“我当然肯。”

===Original===

“Gosh!”

“What did you say?”

“I said Gosh! And listen to me, when I say Gosh, I mean Gosh! Where are you? I must see you. Where can we meet? I want to see you! For Heaven’s sake, tell me where you are. I want to see you! Where are you? Where are you?”

===Chinese===

“天哪！”

“你说什么？”

“我说的是天哪！你听着，我说‘天哪’，就是实实在在的‘天哪’！您在哪儿？我一定要见您。咱们上哪儿碰头？我要见您！看在老天爷份上，告诉我您在哪儿。我要见您！您在哪儿？您在哪儿？”

===Original===

“I’m downstairs.”

“Where? Here at the Carlton?”

“Here at the Carlton!”

“Alone?”

“Quite alone.”

===Chinese===

“我就在楼下。”

“哪儿？就在卡尔顿饭店？”

“就在卡尔顿饭店！”

“就您一个人？”

“就我一个人。”

===Original===

“You won’t be long!” said George.

He hung up the receiver, and bounded across the room to where his coat hung over the back of a chair. The edge of the steamer-trunk caught his shin.

“Well,” said George to the steamer-trunk, “and what are you butting in for? Who wants you, I should like to know!”

===Chinese===

“那您可单不了几天啦！”乔治说。

他挂上听筒，一个箭步蹿过房间，直奔搭在椅背上的外衣。衣箱的箱棱磕上了他的胫骨。

“我说，”乔治冲那口远航衣箱说，“你横插进来这一杠子，算是怎么回事？我倒想请教，谁稀罕你呀！”
